# AUDIT DIGEST

## AREA library

### lib-stores-task  effort=M conf=high sites=52 hazards={'high': 5, 'medium': 4} topLevelReads=0
files: src/stores/taskStore.ts(270), tests/taskStore.test.ts(367)
cats: test-flush=22, other=15, import-store=2, store-produce=2, store-reconcile=2, createComputed=2, write-in-component-body=2, config=2, store-builtin-collection=1, store-path-setter=1, store-delete=1
risks: behavioral=33, mechanical=14, structural=5
SUMMARY: taskStore.ts needs 8 real edits and the test file needs ~30. Verified against the published rc.6 dev build (node-evaluated dist/dev.js), not memory. Headline findings: (1) removeTask is a SILENT CORRECTNESS BREAK — 1.x `setStore(id, undefined)` DELETES the key (solid-js/store/dist/store.js:123-124), 2.0 assigning undefined KEEPS it, so taskCount()/getAllTasks()/collapseAllTasks() all go wrong unless it becomes `delete draft[id]`. (2) The gantt-6hx object-replacement hack can and SHOULD go away: I measured that draft-mutating `_bar.x` re-runs a memo on `tasks[id]._bar.x` (runs 1→2) and leaves a memo on `tasks[id].name` untouched (runs stays 1), while the current `state[id] = {...task,_bar:{...}}` shape re-runs BOTH — i.e. the current produce body invalidates every leaf of every dragged task each mousemove. Bar.tsx:175-187 already reads leaf paths (`getTask()?._bar?.x`), so leaf mutation is sufficient; a memo returning the `_bar` proxy still does NOT invalidate in 2.0 (barRefRuns stayed 1), so gantt-6hx cause #2 is still real and Bar must keep reading leaves. (3) The `if (!tasks[id]) return` guard at L108 reads COMMITTED state — after a staged updateTask/updateTasks in the same tick it sees undefined and silently drops the position update; the draft DOES see staged state (verified), so the guard must move inside the setter. (4) Signal writes inside a createRoot body THROW REACTIVE_WRITE_IN_OWNED_SCOPE in rc.6 — store writes are explicitly exempt (`devGuardStoreSetterWrite` checks `context._root`), `setSignal` has NO such exemption. Verified by running it. That kills 3 tests. (5) vitest.config.ts `environment: 'node'` makes @solidjs/vite-plugin adopt the SERVER posture; solid-js/dist/server.js emits "[SERVER_WRITE] ... this write landed as inert data (nothing re-renders)" — the whole reactivity suite would silently pass-or-noop. (6) The current gantt-6hx guard fails without flush(): I ran the exact test shape and got x()=0/runs=1 where it asserts 999/2. All 4 sma-report sites are covered; I found 24 more.
  STRUCT: The hot path gets FASTER, not slower, if the produce rewrite is done right — this is the main design decision in the batch. Today updateBarPosition/batchMovePositions replace the task object specifically to defeat a 1.x memo identity check (commit 6e5538c, gantt-6hx cause #2). That replacement invalidates every leaf of every moved task on every mousemove, including Bar.tsx:135's t() task-fields memo and the aria-label/tooltip derivations hanging off it. I measured on rc.6 that draft-mutating _bar.x alone invalidates the _bar.x subscriber and nothing else. Combined with microtask batching (a whole mousemove's worth of writes commits once), the 10K-task drag path should do strictly less work in 2.0 than in 1.9 — provided nobody 'ports' the replacement shape verbatim into the draft callback, which would compile, pass the existing tests, and keep the cost. That is why the gantt-6hx guard must grow the negative assertion (sibling memo run count stays 1); without it there is no mechanism preventing regression.
  STRUCT: gantt-6hx cause #2 is NOT fixed by the 2.0 store — I verified a memo returning store.tasks[id]._bar still does not re-run when _bar.x is draft-mutated, because the child proxy identity is stable. So the Bar.tsx:175-187 discipline (each accessor reads its own leaf: getTask()?._bar?.x, ?._bar?.width, …) remains load-bearing after migration, and the comments at Bar.tsx:112/133/174 explaining it should be kept rather than cleaned up as 'legacy 1.x workaround'. Any future refactor that reintroduces a position memo returning the _bar object will silently break drag again.
  STRUCT: The TaskStore public interface (exported from src/index.ts:11) becomes a deferred-commit API without any signature change, which is the least visible part of this migration. Six of its methods are now 'write, visible next microtask' and four are 'read, one commit behind': getTask, getBarPosition, getAllTasks, taskCount, isTaskCollapsed. Every consumer that pairs them in one turn is a latent bug, and the compiler cannot help. Consider making the timing explicit in the type — e.g. renaming the imperative readers or adding a doc block per method — before external consumers depend on 1.x timing. The constraint/cascade layer (taskLayerConstraints.ts, constraintEngine.ts, ShowcaseDemo.tsx:876-930) is the concentration of this risk and should be audited as a unit rather than site-by-site: it is a read-compute-write-read loop over exactly the state this store defers.
  STRUCT: The collapse API on this store (collapsedTasks / toggleTaskCollapse / isTaskCollapsed / expandTask / collapseTask / expandAllTasks / collapseAllTasks — L36-42, L175-244, ~70 lines) has no production caller: the library's collapse behaviour lives in ganttConfigStore.ts (expandAllTasks/collapseAllTasks at :205/:209). Its only consumers are tests L269-329. That means roughly a third of the migration work in the test file, and the whole createRoot signal-write problem, is spent on dead code. Deleting it before migrating would be cheaper than porting it, and would remove the only reason to consider { ownedWrite: true } on these signals. Worth confirming with the owner before doing the work.
  STRUCT: vitest.config.ts is the single highest-leverage change in this batch and it is not in the file list. With test.environment 'node', @solidjs/vite-plugin 3.0.0-next.35 adopts the server posture and solid-js resolves to dist/server.js, whose setters are inert ('[SERVER_WRITE] ... landed as inert data (nothing re-renders)'). Under that config the migrated tests would report a mix of passes (initial-value assertions) and failures (change assertions) that look exactly like reactivity bugs in taskStore.ts, sending whoever runs them hunting in the wrong file. Fix the config first, then migrate the tests, then evaluate results.
  STRUCT: tsconfig.json jsxImportSource is still 'solid-js' and must become '@solidjs/web'. Neither file in this batch contains JSX so it does not gate this work, but it gates the component batches and the same tsconfig covers tests/**/*.ts.
  STRUCT: Dependency floor for this batch: package.json pins solid-js ^1.9.12, vite-plugin-solid ^2.11.12, and four @solid-primitives packages (memo, raf, resize-observer, scroll) plus scheduled, none of which resolve against solid-js 2.x. taskStore.ts itself imports no @solid-primitives, but the test run cannot start until the workspace's peer graph is moved to the 'next' majors (raf 4.0.0-next.2, utils 7.0.0-next.4) — so this batch is not independently testable and should be sequenced after the tooling batch.
  STRUCT: src/utils/profiler.ts (followed one level from taskStore.ts:3 — prof.start at L78) is a pure no-op stub with zero reactive surface: no signals, no stores, no lifecycle. Nothing to migrate. Note that prof.start/endProf bracket getBarPosition's body including its early return at L82-83, so if the real profiler is ever swapped in, it must not create primitives (it would be called from tracking scopes).
  HAZ[high] src/stores/taskStore.ts:108 — updateBarPosition's existence guard `if (!tasks[id]) return;` reads the COMMITTED store, but every caller path can have a staged write to that same id earlier in the same synchronous turn. Concretely: ShowcaseDemo.tsx:657 calls updateTasks(tasks) and then updateBarPosition('task-a', ...) at :807; Gantt.tsx:395 calls updateBarPosition(taskId, {y}) inside a layout pass that may follow a task write; useBarDrag.ts:164 calls updateBarPosition after handlers.onConstrainPosition (taskLayerConstraints.ts:66-74) has already staged cascade writes. Under deferred writes tasks[id] is still undefined/old at the guard, so the setter is never invoked and the position update is silently dropped — no error, no warning, the bar simply does not move. I verified the shape: set(d=>{d.a=task}) followed by an outer read of s.a returns undefined until flush. || FIX: Move the guard inside the draft callback, where staged state IS visible (verified on rc.6): setTasks((draft) => { const task = draft[id]; if (!task?._bar) return; for (const k in position) { const v = position[k]; if (v !== undefined) task._bar[k] = v; } }); No flush() is needed anywhere and the 'no-op for missing task' contract (tests L170-176) is preserved — a bare early return from a draft fn does not shallow-replace the store.
  HAZ[high] src/stores/taskStore.ts:134 — removeTask writes with the 1.x path-setter delete idiom `setTasks(id, undefined)`. In solid-js 1.9.12 (store/dist/store.js:123-124) `value === undefined` triggers `delete state[property]`; in rc.6 the equivalent draft assignment `draft[id] = undefined` KEEPS the key (verified: Object.keys still returned ['a']). Any reader in the same turn or later that counts keys then reads a stale/wrong shape: taskCount() (L150) over-counts forever, getAllTasks() (L144) still walks the dead slot (saved only by its undefined filter), and collapseAllTasks() (L234) iterates a phantom entry whose `task &&` check saves it by luck. The break is silent: types are unchanged, the port compiles, and tests L253-257 still pass because getTask returns undefined either way. || FIX: setTasks((draft) => { delete draft[id]; }); — verified this is the only form that removes the key in rc.6. Keep tests L72-81 (taskCount across add/remove/clear) as the guard for this, since it is the only assertion in the suite that distinguishes delete from undefined-assignment.
  HAZ[high] src/stores/taskStore.ts:80 — getBarPosition returns geometry read from the COMMITTED store, and the constraint/cascade layer both writes and reads bar geometry inside one turn. taskLayerConstraints.ts:86-104 (resolveResizeConstraints) reads getBarPosition(taskId) to obtain x/width and feeds them to resolveConstraints — but it runs after useBarDrag.ts:196/208 has already staged the resize via updateBarPosition, so it computes the successor cascade from LAST frame's geometry. Same shape at ShowcaseDemo.tsx:880 and :910 (getBarPosition(taskId) then updateBarPosition(succId, ...) in a loop) and constraintEngine.ts:632-641/739, all of which receive getBarPosition bound to this store. Under 1.x these read the just-written value; under rc.6 they read the previous commit, so constraints resolve one frame behind and can oscillate or under-clamp during a fast drag. || FIX: Two options, in order of preference: (a) pass the proposed geometry explicitly — resolveResizeConstraints already knows the x/width it just wrote, so take them as parameters instead of re-reading the store (this also removes a proxy read from the 60fps path); (b) if the read must stay, hoist a single flush() to the gesture boundary (mouseup / end-of-move handler) before invoking the cascade, never inside the per-move loop. Do NOT flush per updateBarPosition call — that defeats the microtask batching that now makes batchMovePositions cheap.
  HAZ[medium] src/stores/taskStore.ts:72 — getTask returns the committed task, and two callers do a read-modify-write that straddles a turn boundary: useBarDrag.ts:252 (updateTask(t.id, {...})) after having staged bar writes earlier in the same gesture handler, and BarDemo.tsx:206 (`taskStore.updateTask(taskId, { ...task, progress: newProgress })` where `task` came from getTask). ShowcaseDemo.tsx:666-690 and :829-870 do the same shape (getTask, spread, updateTask). Because the spread source is the pre-write snapshot, any field staged by an earlier write in the same turn is overwritten back to its old value when the replacement commits — a lost update, silent. || FIX: Convert these read-modify-writes into field mutations inside a single draft: taskStore should grow an updateTaskFields(id, patch) that does setTasks(draft => { const t = draft[id]; if (t) Object.assign(t, patch); }). That is correct under staging (the draft sees prior staged writes — verified), avoids the whole-object replacement that invalidates every leaf of the task, and removes the need for callers to hold a snapshot at all.
  HAZ[medium] src/stores/taskStore.ts:234 — collapseAllTasks READS the committed task map (Object.values(tasks)) and then WRITES the collapsedTasks signal. Called in the same turn as updateTasks()/updateTask() — the natural 'load tasks, then collapse all summaries' sequence — it iterates the previous task set and collapses the wrong ids (or none, on first load). tests L306-315 reproduces this exactly: three updateTask calls followed immediately by collapseAllTasks, which under rc.6 sees an empty map and leaves all three assertions false. || FIX: Either require callers to flush() after loading tasks before calling collapseAllTasks (document it on the TaskStore interface), or restructure so the summary-id scan happens against data the caller already holds: collapseAllTasks(tasks?: ProcessedTask[]) taking the array it was just handed, falling back to the store scan only when omitted. The latter is preferable — it makes the function independent of commit timing.
  HAZ[medium] src/stores/taskStore.ts:150 — taskCount() and getAllTasks() (L144) read the committed key set, so any caller that writes tasks and then asks 'how many are there now' in the same turn gets the pre-write answer. Verified: Object.keys(store).length returned 1 immediately after staging a second insert, 2 only after flush(). || FIX: Prefer deriving: consumers that need a live count should read it inside a createMemo (memos see the post-flush value and re-run on the key set changing) rather than calling taskCount() imperatively after a write. Where an imperative call is unavoidable, flush() first. tests L72-81 and L259-266 need the flush.
  HAZ[medium] src/stores/taskStore.ts:196 — isTaskCollapsed() reads the committed collapsedTasks Set immediately after toggleTaskCollapse/collapseTask/expandTask stage a new Set. The mutators themselves compose correctly across a turn (rc.6 setSignal applies the updater against the STAGED value — I verified add-then-delete in one turn yields size 0), so the only broken pattern is toggle-then-query. tests L270-277, L279-287 and L306-315 all use it. || FIX: flush() between the write and the read in tests. In application code, read isTaskCollapsed inside a memo/JSX rather than immediately after a toggle; if an imperative post-toggle branch is genuinely needed, compute the next state locally (`const willCollapse = !store.isTaskCollapsed(id)`) before writing rather than reading back after.
  HAZ[high] tests/taskStore.test.ts:98 — The gantt-6hx regression guard itself is broken by deferred writes: `store.updateBarPosition('t1', { x: 999 }); expect(x()).toBe(999); expect(runs).toBe(2);`. ### lib-stores-config-date  effort=L conf=high sites=42 hazards={'high': 6, 'medium': 3} topLevelReads=0
files: src/stores/ganttConfigStore.ts(340), src/stores/ganttDateStore.ts(378)
cats: write-in-compute=9, other=6, store-path-setter=6, custom-setter-shim=5, store-builtin-collection=5, store-produce=4, import-store=2, createMemo-args=2, store-delete=1, store-unwrap=1, write-in-component-body=1
risks: behavioral=17, mechanical=15, structural=10
SUMMARY: 42 sites across two store factories. ganttConfigStore.ts: one import must move and shed `produce` (line 2); 6 path-argument setters (163, 173, 187, 197, 206, 210) and 4 produce wrappers (175, 189, 199, 216) are removed APIs; the makeSetter shim (151-166) cannot satisfy `Setter<T>` under deferred writes and needs its prev-read moved into the draft plus a void return, which also forces retyping 20 public interface fields (81-100). The file's worst problem is not on SMA's list: expandedTasks is a `Set` inside createStore (146), and rc.6 does not proxy built-in collections, so the three in-place mutation paths notify nothing and expand/collapse becomes a visual no-op for Gantt.tsx:371 and useTaskVirtualization.ts:161. ganttDateStore.ts needs no import changes and SMA reported nothing for it, yet it carries the batch's four high-severity sync hazards: setupDates (152-154, 193-195), extendTimeline (231-237) and changeViewMode (247-248, 251-252) each stage a signal write and then call generateDates(), which reads those signals — and in changeViewMode's case reads step()/unit() MEMOS derived from the staged viewMode, so the timeline silently regenerates at the old granularity. The staleness also leaks across the module boundary into utils/ganttSetup.ts:69-82. Fix by threading computed values into generateDates as optional parameters and having setupDates return its window rather than communicating through signals. Independently, every mutating entry point in both files is currently invoked from a createEffect compute (Gantt.tsx:290/309/321, untrack does not exempt it), so all of it throws REACTIVE_WRITE_IN_OWNED_SCOPE until those effects are split — a hard prerequisite for this batch. No JSX, no lifecycle, no control flow, no top-level reactive reads in either file.
  STRUCT: expandedTasks as a Set inside createStore is the single design-level breakage in this batch. rc.6 does not proxy built-in collections (verified in dist/dev.js isWrappable, line 7341), so all three in-place mutation paths (lines 176-180, 190, 200) become silent no-ops while the accessor at line 304 keeps returning a Set that never changes identity. The fix is a representation change, not a local rewrite: prefer `expandedTasks: Record<string, true>` in GanttConfigState (line 54) — proxied, deep-tracked, per-task path granularity, and `delete draft[k]` for removal — with `Set<string>` reconstructed only at the public boundary (accessor line 304, getConfig line 280, and the `Set<string> | null | undefined` parameter of utils/rowLayoutCalculator.ts:73). The alternative (keep the Set, copy-and-reassign on every write) preserves the API but loses all granularity: every expand/collapse invalidates every reader of the property.
  STRUCT: The makeSetter shim (lines 151-166) exists to project 20 store paths behind solid-js's `Setter<T>` type, and that contract is no longer implementable. `Setter<T>` in rc.6 promises the caller the NEW value and promises an updater a fresh `prev`; a store path under deferred writes can honour neither from outside the draft. The whole 20-field setter surface (interface lines 81-100, construction lines 307-326) should be retyped to a void-returning updater function and reimplemented as a one-line draft write. This is a public API change for consumers of `GanttConfigStore` — but grep shows no in-repo caller uses a setter return value, and only ganttSetup.ts:69-73 and demo/BarDemo.tsx:195 call them at all, so the blast radius is small.
  STRUCT: Every mutating entry point in both files is currently reached from inside a createEffect COMPUTE callback, which throws REACTIVE_WRITE_IN_OWNED_SCOPE in rc.6 dev. The chain is components/Gantt.tsx:290-304 → untrack(() => runSetup(tasks)) → utils/ganttSetup.ts:58/66 → dateStore.setupDates → setGanttStart/setGanttEnd/setDates, plus Gantt.tsx:309 → ganttConfig.updateOptions and Gantt.tsx:321 → dateStore.changeViewMode. The untrack() wrappers do NOT help — the rc.6 guard (dist/dev.js:4916-4934) tests the OWNER, and untrack only suppresses tracking. These three effects must be converted to the split createEffect(compute, apply) form with all writes moved into apply, which runs from the scheduler with no owner. That is a Gantt.tsx/ganttSetup.ts change, but it is a hard prerequisite for either store in this batch functioning at all, so it cannot be sequenced after them.
  STRUCT: generateDates() (ganttDateStore.ts:201-216) encodes a read-your-own-write contract in its signature: it takes no arguments and recovers its four inputs from signals its own callers just wrote. It is simultaneously a public API method (interface line 49, export line 371) where reading the signals is the correct behaviour, and the internal tail of setupDates/extendTimeline/changeViewMode where reading the signals is now wrong. Give it optional (start, end, step, unit) parameters defaulting to the signal reads so both roles stay correct, rather than duplicating the generation loop.
  STRUCT: setupDates (lines 140-196) communicates its results purely through signal side effects, and utils/ganttSetup.ts:69-82 reads five of those signals back immediately to build the config for processTasks. Under deferred writes that handoff is silently one generation stale, and no amount of local rewriting inside setupDates fixes the caller. The durable fix is to make setupDates RETURN its computed window and have ganttSetup consume the return value — an API change that also removes the ganttConfig/dateStore duplication of ganttStart/ganttEnd/unit/step/columnWidth entirely.
  STRUCT: All 7 memos in ganttDateStore (104, 107, 113, 117, 126, 134, 303) now compute eagerly at construction because 2.0 memos are non-lazy by default, and createGanttDateStore runs in a component body (Gantt.tsx:174, GanttStores.tsx:42). Six are trivial; getAllDateInfos (line 303) formats a string pair per timeline column and now recomputes on every dates()/viewMode()/language()/columnWidth() change whether or not the header is mounted. Pass { lazy: true } there to restore 1.x behaviour.
  STRUCT: Coverage note on the advisory SMA report: it lists exactly 5 sites for this batch (ganttConfigStore.ts 2:38, 175:13, 189:13, 199:13, 216:13) and ZERO for ganttDateStore.ts. All 5 are reproduced above. Its produce guidance ('pass the callback directly to the setter') is correct only for site 216; for 175/189/199 it would produce code that compiles and silently never notifies, because the produce target is a raw, unproxied Set. It found no path setters (lines 163, 173, 187, 197, 206, 210), no custom-setter shim, no built-in-collection problem, and none of the 9 sync hazards.
  HAZ[high] src/stores/ganttDateStore.ts:247 — changeViewMode stages setViewModeSignal(found) on line 247 and immediately calls generateDates() on line 248. generateDates reads step() (line 204) and unit() (line 205) — memos derived from viewMode(). Under deferred writes the viewMode signal is only STAGED, so parsedStep/unit/step have not recomputed and still return the PREVIOUS view mode's duration and scale. The entire dates array is therefore regenerated at the old granularity (e.g. switching Day→Month still produces daily columns), and columnWidth() — also derived from viewMode() — is stale too, so gridWidth is wrong. The same bug repeats verbatim in the object branch at lines 251-252. || FIX: Parse the incoming mode locally and pass the derived values in: `const p = dateUtils.parse_duration(found.step); setViewModeSignal(found); generateDates(ganttStart(), ganttEnd(), p?.duration ?? 1, p?.scale ?? 'day');` with generateDates taking optional (start, end, step, unit) params. Do NOT reach for flush() here — changeViewMode is called from an effect at Gantt.tsx:321 and a synchronous flush inside that chain would re-enter the scheduler.
  HAZ[high] src/stores/ganttDateStore.ts:193 — setupDates writes setGanttStart(start) / setGanttEnd(end) on lines 193-194, then calls generateDates() on line 195 which reads ganttStart() and ganttEnd() back (lines 202-203). Both reads return the previously committed boundaries, so the first setupDates call generates dates from the two `new Date()` defaults seeded at lines 70/73 — i.e. an empty or single-element dates array — and every later call generates the PREVIOUS task set's window. The empty-tasks branch has the identical shape at lines 152-154. || FIX: Thread the values explicitly: `setGanttStart(start); setGanttEnd(end); generateDates(start, end);` (and `generateDates(alignedStart, end)` in the empty branch at 152-154), with generateDates accepting optional start/end parameters that default to the signal reads for standalone public use.
  HAZ[high] src/stores/ganttDateStore.ts:195 — Second-order hazard across the module boundary: utils/ganttSetup.ts:66 calls dateStore.setupDates(rawTasks) and then on lines 69-73 immediately reads dateStore.ganttStart(), ganttEnd(), unit(), step(), columnWidth() to push into ganttConfig, and again on lines 74-82 to build the `config` object handed to processTasks(). Every one of those five reads returns the pre-setupDates committed value, so the config store and ALL computed bar geometry are built from the previous timeline window. This is invisible at the ganttSetup call site — the staleness originates from the write on line 193 of this file. || FIX: Have setupDates RETURN the computed window (`{ start, end, unit, step, columnWidth }`) and have ganttSetup consume the returned object rather than re-reading the accessors. That removes the cross-module read-your-own-write entirely without any flush().
  HAZ[high] src/stores/ganttDateStore.ts:237 — extendTimeline stages setGanttStart(newStart) (line 231) or setGanttEnd(newEnd) (line 234) and then calls generateDates() (line 237), which reads both boundaries back. The extended boundary is not yet committed, so the regenerated dates array does not include the newly extended range — the infinite-padding scroll would append nothing and the user would hit a hard wall at the old edge. || FIX: `generateDates(direction === 'left' ? newStart : ganttStart(), direction === 'right' ? newEnd : ganttEnd(), stepVal, unitVal)`. stepVal/unitVal are already captured on lines 222-223 before any write, so they are safe to reuse.
  HAZ[high] src/stores/ganttConfigStore.ts:161 — makeSetter computes the updater's `prev` argument by reading the committed store proxy `state[key]` (line 161) and then stages the write on line 163. Two functional updates to the same key in one synchronous tick both observe the same committed prev, so the first update is discarded — `setStep(n => n + 1); setStep(n => n + 1)` yields +1, not +2. ### lib-stores-resource-selection  effort=M conf=high sites=53 hazards={'high': 3, 'medium': 4, 'low': 1} topLevelReads=0
files: src/stores/resourceStore.ts(154), src/stores/selectionStore.ts(81), tests/selectionStore.test.ts(104), tests/boxSelect.test.ts(148), tests/ganttSetup.test.ts(108), src/utils/ganttSetup.ts(133), src/hooks/useBoxSelect.ts(195)
cats: test-flush=19, other=17, write-in-component-body=8, import-web=2, store-builtin-collection=2, createMemo-args=2, config=2, primitives-in-callback=1
risks: mechanical=28, behavioral=14, structural=11
SUMMARY: Signal-only stores: resourceStore.ts and selectionStore.ts need NO API-level rewrites (no removed imports, no createStore/produce/unwrap, no onMount/batch/on/createComputed, no JSX). The entire migration cost is timing and ownership. Three findings dominate. (1) ganttSetup.ts, exercised by ganttSetup.test.ts, has two high-severity read-after-write pairs: line 101->102 writes resources then reads the resourceIndexMap memo back (empty on first run, so processTasks assigns no resource row and every bar lands on row 0), and line 66 -> lines 69-80 calls dateStore.setupDates then reads ganttStart/ganttEnd/unit/step/columnWidth back into the config store and the processTasks config. Both need restructuring to return plain values, not flush() - initializeTasks also runs from an effect where flush() is a no-op. (2) A rc.6 fact that contradicts the briefed runtime notes and that I confirmed by executing the published dev build: plain SIGNAL setters throw REACTIVE_WRITE_IN_OWNED_SCOPE inside a createRoot body (dev.js:4916 lacks the `!context._root` exemption that the store-setter guard at dev.js:4892 has). That breaks both reactivity suites in selectionStore.test.ts at lines 74/77/80/91/99 and means the usual 'wrap tests in createRoot' recipe is wrong for these stores; ganttSetup.test.ts's unowned makeStores() must stay unowned. (3) 15 test assertions need flush(): all seven resource assertions in ganttSetup.test.ts hard-fail, and six spots in selectionStore.test.ts either fail (lines 16, 31, 47) or pass vacuously against a stale empty Set (lines 30, 38, 41, 56, 59). Verified safe and not to be touched: Sets held in signals (both stores) and functional-updater composition - rc.6 feeds updaters the staged value, so add/remove/toggle/toggleGroup and useBoxSelect's per-hit add loop all still compose within a tick. Remaining behavioral risk sits in the committed-read helpers isSelected/selectionCount/isGroupCollapsed and in collapseAll's read-then-write. boxSelect.test.ts is purely functional and needs no reactive change, but all three test files depend on replacing vite-plugin-solid and dropping test.environment 'node', which would otherwise resolve the server build with no subscription graph. The official migration assistant reports zero sites for these five files.
  STRUCT: VERIFIED DEVIATION FROM THE BRIEFED RUNTIME FACTS: fact 2 lists 'createRoot bodies' as an allowed write scope, but in @solidjs/signals 2.0.0-rc.6 that exemption exists only for STORE setters. dist/dev.js:4892 devGuardStoreSetterWrite explicitly checks `!context._root` with the comment 'Roots are not owned computation scopes - setters inside createRoot bodies are legal'; dist/dev.js:4916 setSignal has NO such check, so any plain signal setter called directly in a createRoot body throws REACTIVE_WRITE_IN_OWNED_SCOPE. Confirmed by executing the rc.6 dev build. This invalidates the standard 'wrap the test in createRoot' migration recipe for every signal-only store in this repo, and it is why both reactivity suites in tests/selectionStore.test.ts must be restructured rather than merely flushed. Escape hatches if a rewrite is undesirable: createSignal(v, { ownedWrite: true }) (verified to work inside a root) or keeping the writes outside the root body.
  STRUCT: The resourceStore/selectionStore modules themselves need essentially NO code change - no removed imports, no createStore, no produce/unwrap, no Index/Show/context, no onMount, no createEffect, no batch, no createComputed. The entire cost of this batch is in (a) their consumers' owner context and (b) read-after-write timing. Any migration plan that treats these two files as 'done' after an import sweep will ship a silently broken chart.
  STRUCT: WRITE-GUARD OWNERSHIP CHAIN: resourceStore.updateResources is reached only via ganttSetup.initializeTasks, which Gantt.tsx invokes from onMount (line 294) and from three createEffect compute bodies (lines 297-308, 320-330 region), all wrapped in untrack(). untrack disables tracking but does NOT clear the owner, so every one of those calls throws in 2.0 dev. The fix is not local to resourceStore: runSetup must move into the APPLY half of a split createEffect (compute reads effectiveTasks()/collapsedGroups()/collapsedTasks() and returns them as plain values; apply calls runSetup with those values) and the onMount initial run folds into that same effect's first run. Note the apply callback runs untracked from the scheduler, so it must NOT read store proxies (STRICT_READ_UNTRACKED) - all reads belong in compute.
  STRUCT: initializeTasks is currently a 'write stores, then read them back' pipeline (3 distinct write->read pairs in 66 lines). Under deferred writes it needs to become a 'compute plain values, use them, then commit once' pipeline: setupDates returns bounds, resource extraction builds its own index map, and the store writes happen at the end purely to publish to reactive consumers. This is a redesign of the function's shape, not a set of flush() insertions - and flush() is unavailable inside it anyway once it runs from an effect apply phase.
  STRUCT: tests/ganttSetup.test.ts is an integration test over four stores created unowned at test scope. That posture is correct and must be preserved for 2.0 (unowned createSignal/createMemo/createStore are legal - verified; none of the four store factories creates a createEffect, so there is no NO_OWNER_EFFECT warning). The only cost is that the memos are never disposed. If leak-freedom is wanted, createRoot may own the store CREATION only, with dispose captured and every initializeTasks call kept outside the root body.
  STRUCT: tests/boxSelect.test.ts needs no reactive changes at all, but it transitively imports src/hooks/useBoxSelect.ts and therefore depends entirely on the vitest resolution posture. vitest.config.ts currently pins test.environment: 'node' plus a hand-written resolve.conditions: ['development','browser']; with @solidjs/vite-plugin@3.0.0-next.35 the 'node' environment deliberately selects the SERVER build (no subscription graph). Moving to environment 'jsdom' (the plugin's own test-mode default) and deleting the manual conditions override is a prerequisite for selectionStore.test.ts's reactivity suites to observe anything, flush() or not.
  STRUCT: Verified-safe pattern worth protecting during the migration: rc.6 setSignal (dist/dev.js:4944) feeds the functional updater the STAGED value when one exists, so N functional updates in one tick compose. That is what keeps selectionStore.add/remove/toggle and resourceStore.toggleGroup correct, and what keeps useBoxSelect.ts:156's `for (const id of hits) sel.add(id)` loop accumulating all hits. A migration that 'simplifies' any of these to a read-then-value-write (setSelectedIds(new Set([...selectedIds(), id]))) would break under deferred writes.
  STRUCT: The official read-only migration assistant (sma-report.txt, 401 lines) reports ZERO sites for all five files in this batch - it flags only solid-js/store subpath imports and produce wrappers in the sibling ganttConfigStore/taskStore. It is structurally blind to this batch's actual failure modes (deferred read-after-write, the createRoot write guard, and owner-context violations reached through a call chain), so its silence here must not be read as 'these files are clean'.
  HAZ[high] src/utils/ganttSetup.ts:101 — WRITE resourceStore.updateResources(extracted) -> setResources(...) on line 101, then READ resourceStore.resourceIndexMap() on line 102, a memo derived from resources(). Under deferred writes the memo still holds the value derived from the PREVIOUSLY COMMITTED resources array. On the first run resources() is [] so resourceIndexMap() is an empty Map, and processTasks(rawTasks, config, emptyMap) on line 109 assigns no resource row index to any task - every bar collapses onto row 0. On later runs it is off by one generation (the previous filter's resource set). || FIX: Build the map from the local `extracted` array instead of reading it back: `const extractedMap = new Map(extracted.map((r, i) => [r.id, i]));` (identical to the loop in resourceStore.ts:62-72, which cannot skip collapsed groups on a fresh extraction anyway), pass that to processTasks, and keep updateResources purely for the reactive consumers. Do not insert flush() here - initializeTasks is also called from Gantt.tsx's effect chain where flush() inside an effect callback is a no-op (FLUSH_IN_EFFECT_CALLBACK).
  HAZ[high] src/utils/ganttSetup.ts:66 — WRITE dateStore.setupDates(rawTasks) on line 66 (which calls setGanttStart/setGanttEnd and generateDates internally, ganttDateStore.ts:140-156), then READ dateStore.ganttStart()/ganttEnd()/unit()/step()/columnWidth() on lines 69-73 to push into ganttConfig, and again on lines 76-80 to build the `config` object handed to processTasks. All five reads return pre-setupDates values, so the config store and every bar x/width computed by processTasks are one generation stale - on the first run they are the store's construction defaults (new Date()), producing bars positioned against today's date instead of the task range. || FIX: Change setupDates to RETURN the bounds it computed ({ ganttStart, ganttEnd, unit, step, columnWidth }) and use the returned object for lines 69-73 and 76-80, writing to the signals purely for downstream reactive consumers. This also removes the redundant double read.
  HAZ[medium] src/utils/ganttSetup.ts:95 — READ resourceStore.resources().length > 0 used as a fallback 'were resources explicitly provided' probe. Any updateResources call earlier in the same tick (including line 101 of a previous initializeTasks call in the same handler) is invisible, so the branch on line 97/99 can pick the wrong arm. || FIX: Make hasExplicitResources a required parameter and delete the `??` fallback; the comment on lines 88-90 already documents that the probe is unreliable. Gantt.tsx:262 already computes the flag correctly.
  HAZ[medium] src/stores/resourceStore.ts:130 — collapseAll() READS the getGroups() memo (derived from resources()) on line 130 and then WRITES setCollapsedGroups on line 131. If the caller ran updateResources() earlier in the same tick, getGroups() returns the previous resource list and collapseAll collapses the stale group ids - new groups stay expanded and removed groups stay in the collapsed set forever. || FIX: Accept the group list explicitly - collapseAll(groups: Resource[] = getGroups()) - so setup code that just wrote resources can pass the array it already has; or have callers flush() before collapseAll (only legal from unowned code such as event handlers).
  HAZ[medium] src/stores/resourceStore.ts:110 — isGroupCollapsed(groupId) reads collapsedGroups() and therefore returns COMMITTED state. toggleGroup(id) followed by isGroupCollapsed(id) in the same handler (the natural 'toggle then branch on the new state' shape) returns the pre-toggle answer. Verified against dist/dev.js:4667 - an unowned read returns _value, never _pendingValue. || FIX: Keep isGroupCollapsed as the reactive predicate for JSX, and have imperative callers that need the post-write answer compute it locally (they already know which way they toggled) or call flush() first. Add a doc comment stating it reads committed state.
  HAZ[medium] src/stores/selectionStore.ts:34 — isSelected(id) reads selectedIds() and returns COMMITTED state; same for selectionCount() on line 73. TaskLayer.tsx:136-140 writes the selection (add/toggle/replace) and TaskLayer.tsx:110 branches on sel.isSelected(taskId) && sel.selectionCount() > 1 to decide whether a drag is a multi-select drag. Today these sit on different DOM events (click vs mousedown) so a microtask flush intervenes, but any refactor that pairs them in one handler - or any consumer callback invoked from handleTaskClickWithSelection - silently sees the pre-write selection. || FIX: Document the committed-read semantics on both methods, and where a same-tick answer is needed pass the intended ids explicitly rather than re-reading the store (e.g. have handleTaskClickWithSelection compute and return the resulting id set). Do not paper over it with flush() inside library code that may run under an effect.
  HAZ[high] tests/ganttSetup.test.ts:30 — Every assertion in the 'resource extraction signal' describe block (lines 30, 45, 52, 66, 76, 78, 85) reads stores.resourceStore.resources() in the same synchronous tick as the initializeTasks call that wrote it. All of these read the committed value and fail (or, at lines 41/56-style spots, pass vacuously against the stale empty Set). || FIX: Insert flush() (imported from 'solid-js') after every initializeTasks/updateResources call, before the matching expect. Do not wrap the test bodies in createRoot - that would make the writes throw.

Gantt.tsx:294 onMount(() => runSetup(...)) and Gantt.tsx:297/303/324 createEffect(... untrack(() => runSetup(...))) reach this line from owned scopes and will throw RE

### lib-contexts-index  effort=S conf=high sites=33 hazards={'high': 2, 'medium': 1, 'low': 1} topLevelReads=3
files: src/contexts/GanttEvents.tsx(69), src/contexts/GanttStores.tsx(62), src/index.ts(65), src/types.ts(418)
cats: other=8, import-jsx-type=6, context-default=4, context-provider=4, top-level-read=3, store-builtin-collection=2, config=2, custom-setter-shim=1, store-produce=1, store-path-setter=1, docs=1
risks: mechanical=17, behavioral=10, structural=6
SUMMARY: Read all four files in full plus the four store factories they delegate to. 31 sites. The four listed files contain no signal/store writes, no effects, no lifecycle, no control flow and no DOM/JSX attribute usage — the entire batch reduces to three themes. (1) Two default-less contexts (GanttEvents.tsx:23, GanttStores.tsx:30) whose hooks treat 'no provider' as a supported state; in rc.6 useContext throws ContextNotFoundError, and GanttStores' fallback is the library's default path (Gantt.tsx:170 `useGanttStores() ?? {…}` for bare `<Gantt tasks={...} />`), so every un-wrapped chart would crash. Fix is `createContext<T | null>(null)` plus `?? undefined` at GanttStores.tsx:59 to keep the documented public signature. (2) Mechanical import/JSX work: both files import JSX as a value from solid-js (must become `import type { JSX } from '@solidjs/web'`, with tsconfig jsxImportSource flipped to '@solidjs/web'), and both `<Ctx.Provider value=…>` elements become `<Ctx value=…>` (GanttEvents.tsx:44/46, GanttStores.tsx:47/49 — the only two sites sma-report.txt found for this batch; both confirmed). (3) Public API surface: index.ts leaks ~40 `Accessor<T>` and ~23 `Setter<T>` from solid-js through TaskStore/GanttConfigStore/GanttDateStore/ResourceStore and transitively through `type GanttStores`; the types still compile in rc.6 but every exported setter now stages its write, so consumers' read-after-write needs flush(). Following one level into createGanttConfigStore surfaced the batch's only real sync hazards: makeSetter (ganttConfigStore.ts:151-166) is a hand-rolled Setter shim that reads committed state for `prev` (line 161) and returns `state[key]` after the write (line 164) — stale and lossy under deferred writes — and expandedTasks is a Set mutated in place via produce inside a store (lines 175-201), which notifies nothing in 2.0. types.ts needs zero mechanical edits (no Solid imports) but owns two invariants: the `expandedTasks?: Set<string>` option shape (line 352) and the unproxied store-held Dates on ProcessedTask (line 198), plus ConstraintContext's committed-read contract (line 268). Verified the four factories perform no writes at construction, so GanttProvider's body does not trip the write guard; only memo eagerness changes. topLevelReadCount 3, all in GanttProvider (props.options twice, props.resources once) — warning-only, reactivity unchanged.
  STRUCT: Optional-provider pattern is load-bearing and default-less contexts now throw. Both contexts are created with createContext<T>() and both hooks treat a missing provider as a normal state (GanttEvents.tsx:55-65 returns no-ops; GanttStores.tsx:59 returns undefined). In rc.6 useContext throws ContextNotFoundError for a default-less context, and Gantt.tsx:170 depends on the undefined path for the bare `<Gantt tasks={...} />` form — the library's most common usage. Both must take a real `null` default. Decide deliberately whether useGanttStores keeps returning `undefined` (via `?? undefined`, preserving README.md:106 and Gantt.tsx:170 untouched) or switches its public signature to `GanttStores | null`.
  STRUCT: Solid types are pervasive in the published API. index.ts re-exports four store interfaces carrying roughly 40 `Accessor<T>` and 23 `Setter<T>` aliases from solid-js (GanttConfigStore 20+20, GanttDateStore 12+3, TaskStore 1+1, ResourceStore 6+0), plus `type GanttStores` which pulls in all of them transitively. Accessor/Setter still exist in rc.6, so nothing fails to compile — but their runtime contract changes for every downstream consumer: a setter stages, the paired accessor keeps returning the committed value until the microtask flush, and read-after-write now requires importing flush() from solid-js. This needs an explicit decision: document the deferred semantics in the README/JSDoc, or stop exporting raw Solid types and wrap the mutators in async/void-returning methods.
  STRUCT: GanttConfigStore's Setter facade cannot survive as-is. makeSetter (ganttConfigStore.ts:151-166) emulates createSignal's Setter over a store path by reading committed state for `prev` and for the return value. Deferred writes break both halves, and 2.0 removes the path-setter form it is built on. The fix is a redesign of the shim (draft-based, returning the locally computed value), and it changes the observable contract of 20 exported setters — so it is an API decision, not a local rewrite.
  STRUCT: expandedTasks is a Set living inside a createStore. ganttConfigStore.ts:146 puts a Set<string> in the store and lines 175-201 mutate it in place through produce. 2.0 neither proxies built-in collections nor ships produce, so this state silently stops notifying. Choose one of two shapes: move it to a signal holding an immutable Set (taskStore.collapsedTasks already does exactly this and is 2.0-clean), or keep it in the store and always assign a fresh Set to the draft property. The public option type types.ts:352 encodes the choice.
  STRUCT: GanttProvider constructs all four stores eagerly in the component body (GanttStores.tsx:39-44). That remains legal in 2.0 — verified that no factory writes during construction, so the REACTIVE_WRITE_IN_OWNED_SCOPE guard is not tripped — but rc.6 memos are non-lazy, so ~9 memos across ganttDateStore and resourceStore now evaluate during provider setup rather than at first read. Anything that assumed lazy first-evaluation ordering (e.g. dates() being empty until setupDates ran) should be re-checked at the provider level.
  STRUCT: types.ts requires no mechanical migration at all (zero Solid imports), but it is where two 2.0 invariants must be pinned as documented contract: store-held Date fields on ProcessedTask (_start/_end/_baselineStart/_baselineEnd) are unproxied and must be replaced rather than mutated, and ConstraintContext's pull-style getBarPosition/getTask readers must be fed a snapshot or a draft when the caller has already written in the same tick.
  HAZ[high] src/stores/ganttConfigStore.ts:164 — makeSetter writes via `setState(key, next)` on line 163 and then, in the same synchronous statement sequence, reads `return state[key]` to hand the caller the 'new' value. Under rc.6 deferred writes the store read still returns the previously committed value, so every one of the 20 exported GanttConfigStore setters (setColumnWidth, setBarHeight, setRenderMode, …) returns the OLD value while claiming the Setter<T> contract. This is public API via index.ts:12. || FIX: Do not re-read the store. Capture the computed value and return it: `let next; setState(draft => { next = …; draft[key] = next; }); return next;`
  HAZ[high] src/stores/ganttConfigStore.ts:161 — The updater branch of makeSetter computes `prev` by reading committed `state[key]` before the write. Two updater-form calls in the same tick (e.g. setColumnWidth(w => w+10) twice, or an updater called from a drag handler that already staged a write) both observe the same committed prev, so the first update is lost. || FIX: Move the updater invocation inside the store draft callback, where the draft reflects staged state: `setState(draft => { draft[key] = typeof value === 'function' ? value(draft[key]) : value; });`
  HAZ[medium] src/stores/ganttConfigStore.ts:186 — expandTask early-returns on a committed read (`if (state.expandedTasks.has(taskId)) return;`) and only then writes; collapseTask does the mirror check at line 196. If a toggle/expand/collapse pair runs in one tick (keyboard repeat, batched UI action, a handler that expands then collapses), the second call still sees the pre-flush membership and either skips a needed write or performs a redundant one. || FIX: Drop the pre-read guard and decide inside the draft: `setState(draft => { if (draft.expandedTasks.has(taskId)) return; const next = new Set(draft.expandedTasks); next.add(taskId); draft.expandedTasks = next; });`
  SITE[structural/context-default] src/contexts/GanttEvents.tsx:23 — const GanttEventsContext = createContext<GanttEventHandlers>(); => const GanttEventsContext = createContext<GanttEventHandlers | null>(null);
  SITE[structural/context-default] src/contexts/GanttStores.tsx:30 — const GanttStoresContext = createContext<GanttStores>(); => const GanttStoresContext = createContext<GanttStores | null>(null);
  SITE[structural/store-builtin-collection] src/types.ts:352 —     expandedTasks?: Set<string>; => Either change the public option to `string[]` (normalised to a Set only in signal-held state), or keep Set<string> but never mutate it in place inside the store — always assign a fresh Set to the draft property.
  SITE[structural/other] src/types.ts:268 —     getBarPosition: (id: string) => BarPosition | undefined; => Document/redefine ConstraintContext as reading a SNAPSHOT: build it from `snapshot(store)` or from the setStore draft, not from live committed store reads, whenever the caller has already written positions in the same ti
  SITE[structural/custom-setter-shim] src/stores/ganttConfigStore.ts:154 — return ((value: unknown) => { const next = typeof value === 'function' ? value(state[key]) : value;  => return ((value: unknown) => { let next: any; setStore(draft => { next = typeof value === 'function' ? ### lib-scroll-container  effort=L conf=high sites=36 hazards={'high': 2, 'medium': 4} topLevelReads=0
files: src/hooks/useGanttScroll.ts(107), src/components/GanttContainer.tsx(363)
cats: other=13, createEffect-split=6, write-in-compute=6, onCleanup-in-lifecycle=3, batch=2, primitives-in-callback=2, import-jsx-type=1, onMount=1, ref-owned=1, jsx-attr=1
risks: mechanical=18, behavioral=10, structural=8
SUMMARY: 36 sites across 2 files. One structural break dominates: GanttContainer.tsx:187 calls props.onContainerReady from onMount, and useGanttScroll.handleContainerReady (useGanttScroll.ts:50-98) creates six createEffect nodes at that moment (58, 68, 71, 78, 93, 96). Under 2.0, onMount -> onSettled makes that scope children-forbidden, so all six throw PRIMITIVE_IN_FORBIDDEN_SCOPE on first mount — verified against @solidjs/signals 2.0.0-rc.6 dev.js:7217-7220 (onSettled = createTrackedEffect wrapping an untracked callback) and dev.js:4444-4455 (setupComputedNode throws under CONFIG_CHILDREN_FORBIDDEN). Not fixable by splitting the effects; the creation site itself is illegal. Recommended fix uses plumbing Gantt.tsx already has: change the hook to useGanttScroll(containerApi: Accessor<ContainerAPILike | null>) (Gantt.tsx:244-245 already holds that signal) and replace all four mirror signals with top-level memos reading containerApi()?.<x>Signal?.() ?? 0. That single move deletes every write (52, 53, 62, 63, 68, 71, 84, 93, 96), every effect, both write-in-compute throw classes, and the extra flush hop that would otherwise make virtualization trail scrolling by a microtask. 6 sync hazards, 2 high: (1) GanttContainer.tsx:172 stages setContainerWidth and useGanttScroll.ts:52 reads it back via api.getContainerWidth() later in the SAME synchronous onSettled run, so viewportWidth seeds to 0 instead of the measured clientWidth and the first virtualization pass sees a zero-width viewport; (2) useGanttScroll.ts:81-89 runs a compute that mutates closure state, writes a signal and drives timers. Also found beyond the SMA report: the entire isScrolling block (45-48, 75-90) is dead — no reader in src/ or tests/, so the file's hardest migration should be a deletion; viewportHeight is never seeded at mount (only the ResizeObserver at 178 writes it); scrollTimeout and pendingUpdate leak past unmount; and getSvgElement/scrollTimingSignal/resetWorstTiming/__ganttScrollDebug are entirely unconsumed. Agreed with all 8 SMA findings for these files (useGanttScroll 58, 61, 68, 71, 78, 93, 96; GanttContainer 170, 183) and dispute the framing of two: 58 and 78 are not mechanical splits, and 183's onCleanup fix requires hoisting the ResizeObserver binding out of the `if (scrollAreaRef)` guard because onSettled accepts only one returned cleanup. JSX tree and all four refs audited clean — no classList, use:, on:, attr:, @once, Index, Show/Match callbacks, Provider or Dynamic; refs are plain variable bindings, still supported. 0 top-level reactive reads: neither file reads reactive state at component/hook body top level, and reads inside onSettled are untracked because onSettled wraps its callback in untrack.
  STRUCT: THE onContainerReady HANDSHAKE IS THE BATCH'S ONE STRUCTURAL BREAK. GanttContainer.tsx:187 invokes props.onContainerReady from inside onMount; migrating that to onSettled puts the callee in a children-forbidden scope, and useGanttScroll.handleContainerReady creates SIX createEffect nodes there (lines 58, 68, 71, 78, 93, 96). Verified against @solidjs/signals 2.0.0-rc.6 dist/dev.js: onSettled builds a createTrackedEffect (dev.js:7217-7220) and setupComputedNode throws PRIMITIVE_IN_FORBIDDEN_SCOPE whenever the active context has CONFIG_CHILDREN_FORBIDDEN (dev.js:4444-4455). This throws on first mount of every Gantt; it is not a warning and not fixable by splitting the effects.
  STRUCT: RECOMMENDED REDESIGN (containerApi accessor, not function-form signals). Gantt.tsx ALREADY has the needed plumbing: `const [containerApi, setContainerApi] = createSignal<ContainerAPILike | null>(null)` at Gantt.tsx:244-245, written from handleContainerReady at Gantt.tsx:490. Change useGanttScroll's signature to `useGanttScroll(containerApi: Accessor<ContainerAPILike | null>)`, delete handleContainerReady (useGanttScroll.ts:50-98) and its interface member (35-36), and declare four memos at hook top level: scrollLeft/scrollTop/viewportWidth/viewportHeight = createMemo(() => containerApi()?.<x>Signal?.() ?? 0). Gantt.tsx:248 becomes useGanttScroll(containerApi) and Gantt.tsx:491 drops the delegation. setContainerApi(api) inside onSettled remains legal (onSettled bodies are exempt from the write guard, dev.js:4895-4900). This removes every write, every effect, and every read-after-write in useGanttScroll.ts in one move — the file collapses from 107 lines to roughly 40.
  STRUCT: THE MEMO REDESIGN IS STRICTLY BETTER THAN A MECHANICAL EFFECT SPLIT, not merely legal. The current mirror layer means the container's scroll signal must land, flush, then the mirror write must land and flush again before Gantt.tsx:454-457 -> createVirtualViewport sees it — two flushes per throttled scroll tick under 2.0's deferred writes, where 1.9's batch() collapsed it to one. Deriving reads the container's signals directly and settles virtualization in the same flush.
  STRUCT: THE isScrolling MACHINERY IS DEAD AND SHOULD BE DELETED, NOT MIGRATED. useGanttScroll.ts:47 discards the accessor and grep across src/ and tests/ finds no reader — only the comments at lines 10 and 75 mention it. It is simultaneously the hardest site to migrate (write in compute at 84, closure mutation in compute at 82-83, timer side effects in compute at 85-86, no disposal for scrollTimeout declared at 48). Deleting lines 45-48 and 75-90 removes two of the six syncHazards outright. If a future consumer needs it, reintroduce it with createSignal(false, { unobserved }) and the prev-argument effect form.
  STRUCT: DEAD ContainerAPI SURFACE. getSvgElement (GanttContainer.tsx:52, 193), scrollTimingSignal (57, 198), resetWorstTiming (58, 199-201), the ScrollTiming interface (39-44), the never-written scrollTiming signal (93-98), svgRef (83, 326), window.__ganttScrollDebug (22-37) and ScrollDebugInfo (13-19) have zero consumers anywhere in src/ or tests/. Pruning them before migrating shrinks the object handed out of the forbidden scope at line 187 and removes one more primitive (line 93) from the component.
  STRUCT: TIMER DISPOSAL GAPS SURFACE NOW. Neither scrollTimeout (useGanttScroll.ts:48) nor pendingUpdate (GanttContainer.tsx:113) is ever cleared on unmount, so both can fire setters after disposal. 2.0's returned-cleanup model is the natural place to fix this: a cleanup returned from the split effect's apply for scrollTimeout, and an onCleanup at GanttContainer's component-body top level (still legal there) for pendingUpdate.
  STRUCT: TOOLING PRECONDITION FOR THIS BATCH: tsconfig.json:8 currently sets "jsxImportSource": "solid-js" and must become "@solidjs/web", otherwise GanttContainer.tsx's JSX.Element annotations (lines 68-73, 81) and the intrinsic-element typing of its 135-line JSX tree will not resolve after the import move on line 1. Flagged here rather than as a site because tsconfig is outside this batch's file list.
  STRUCT: CALLBACK-CONTRACT DOCUMENTATION IS NOW LOad-BEARING. After migration, onContainerReady (GanttContainer.tsx:75, 187) executes inside a children-forbidden scope forever. The prop's type gives callers no hint of that. The other in-tree consumer, src/demo/GanttMinimalTest.tsx:639, happens to be safe (`scrollRef = api`), but the constraint should be stated in a doc comment on the prop so future callers do not reintroduce the same class of crash.
  HAZ[high] src/components/GanttContainer.tsx:172 — WRITE: GanttContainer.tsx:172 `setContainerWidth(scrollAreaRef.clientWidth)`. READ: useGanttScroll.ts:52 `setViewportWidth(api.getContainerWidth())`, where getContainerWidth is the closure defined at GanttContainer.tsx:191 as `() => containerWidth()`. Both run in the SAME synchronous onMount/onSettled call stack: line 172 writes, line 187 synchronously invokes props.onContainerReady, which reaches Gantt.tsx:491 -> useGanttScroll.ts:52 and calls the getter. In 1.9 the setter committed immediately so the getter returned the measured clientWidth. In 2.0 the write is staged until the microtask flush, so the getter returns the previously committed 0 and viewportWidth is seeded to 0. Downstream: Gantt.tsx:456 feeds scroll.viewportWidth into createVirtualViewport, so the first column-virtualization pass computes an empty/zero-width viewport until the first ResizeObserver callback happens to fire. || FIX: Preferred: delete the mirror. Make useGanttScroll derive `viewportWidth = createMemo(() => containerApi()?.containerWidthSignal?.() ?? 0)` so the measured width arrives on the normal flush and no read-after-write exists. If the handshake must stay imperative, pass the measured numbers explicitly instead of re-reading — capture `const w = scrollAreaRef.clientWidth` at line 172 and include `initialWidth: w, initialHeight: h` in the ContainerAPI object at 187 — or, as a last resort, call `flush()` between the write at 172 and the callback at 187.
  HAZ[medium] src/components/GanttContainer.tsx:178 — WRITE: GanttContainer.tsx:178 `setViewportHeight(entry.contentRect.height)` is the ONLY writer of viewportHeight; the mount path never seeds it. READ: useGanttScroll.ts:53 `api.getContainerHeight()` -> GanttContainer.tsx:192 `() => viewportHeight()`, called synchronously during the mount handshake before any ResizeObserver callback has run. This returns 0 in 1.9 as well, but 2.0's deferred writes remove the last chance for a same-tick correction and make the zero durable until the observer's first async tick. || FIX: Add `setViewportHeight(scrollAreaRef.clientHeight)` next to line 172, and derive viewportHeight in useGanttScroll from `containerHeightSignal` rather than seeding it imperatively.
  HAZ[medium] src/hooks/useGanttScroll.ts:76 — WRITE: useGanttScroll.ts:52-53 stage setViewportWidth/setViewportHeight, and the effects registered at 58/68/71 will stage setScrollLeft/setScrollTop on their first run. READ: lines 76-77 `let lastScrollTop = scrollTop(); let lastScrollLeft = scrollLeft();` capture a change-detection BASELINE from committed state at handshake time. Under deferred writes the baseline is one flush older than the first value the effect at 78 observes, so the first scroll delta after mount is classified as a change even when it is not (or the reverse if the container had already scrolled). The effect at 78 then flips isScrolling spuriously and starts the 150ms quiet timer. || FIX: Delete the manual baseline vars and use rc.6's apply-callback `prev` argument: `createEffect(() => [scrollLeft(), scrollTop()] as const, ([sl, st], prev) => { if (prev && sl === prev[0] && st === prev[1]) return; ... })`. The framework's prev is always the value from the previous settled run, which is the correct baseline by construction.
  HAZ[medium] src/hooks/useGanttScroll.ts:58 — CHAINED WRITE->READ ACROSS EFFECTS. The effect at 58-65 reads the container's scrollLeftSignal/scrollTopSignal and WRITES the hook-local scrollLeft/scrollTop; the effect at 78-90 READS those same hook-local signals. In 1.9 (with batch) the write committed within the same flush and the second effect ran in the same synchronous cascade. In 2.0 the mirror write is staged, so the second effect cannot see it until the next flush — isScrolling turns on one microtask after the container's scroll signal changes, and the 150ms quiet window starts correspondingly later. The same one-flush lag applies to every consumer of scroll.scrollLeft/scrollTop, i.e. Gantt.tsx:454-455 -> createVirtualViewport, so virtualization trails the container's own signal by an extra flush on every throttled scroll tick. || FIX: Remove the mirror layer: `scrollLeft = createMemo(() => containerApi()?.scrollLeftSignal?.() ?? 0)` reads the container's signal directly, so consumers and the isScrolling effect settle in the SAME flush as the container update rather than one flush behind. This is the main behavioral reason to prefer the memo redesign over a mechanical effect split.
  HAZ[medium] src/components/GanttContainer.tsx:138 — WRITE-THEN-CALLBACK IN AN EVENT HANDLER. handleScroll writes setScrollLeft(sl)/setScrollTop(st) at 138-139 (or 145-146 on the trailing edge) and then calls `props.onScroll?.(sl, st)` at line 152. The current code is SAFE because it passes the raw sl/st values, but any consumer that responds to onScroll by calling `api.getScrollLeft()` / `api.getScrollTop()` (GanttContainer.tsx:189-190) will read the pre-write committed value, since the handler has not yielded to the microtask flush yet. src/demo/GanttMinimalTest.tsx:604+ stores the api from onContainerReady into `scrollRef` and uses it from handlers, so the shape is one edit away from existing in-tree. || FIX: Keep passing scroll positions as explicit arguments through onScroll and never re-read them from the api getters inside the same handler. Document on ContainerAPI (GanttContainer.tsx:46-59) that getScrollLeft/getScrollTop/getContainerWidth/getContainerHeight return last-committed values and are not valid immediately after a write; direct consumers to the *Signal members or to a flush() when a synchronous read-back is genuinely required.
  HAZ[high] src/hooks/useGanttScroll.ts:81 — SIDE-EFFECTFUL COMPUTE. Lines 81-89 read scrollLeft()/scrollTop() in a tracked compute, compare against mutable closure vars, then MUTATE those vars (82-83), write a signal (84), and start/cancel a timer (85-86) — all inside the compute. In 2.0 the compute is required to be pure and write-free: line 84 throws REACTIVE_WRITE_IN_OWNED_SCOPE outright, and even without the throw, a compute that rc.6 may re-evaluate more than once per settle would desync lastScrollLeft/lastScrollTop and leak orphaned timeouts. || FIX: Split so compute is `() => [scrollLeft(), scrollTop()] as const` and everything else lives in apply, which runs unowned and may write and return a cleanup. Better still, delete lines 45-48 and 75-90 entirely — grep shows no reader of isScrolling anywhere in src/ or tests/, so this whole hazard can be removed rather than migrated.

### lib-gantt  effort=XL conf=high sites=50 hazards={'high': 4, 'medium': 4, 'low': 1} topLevelReads=7
files: src/components/Gantt.tsx(678), src/utils/ganttSetup.ts(133), src/hooks/useGanttScroll.ts(107), src/hooks/useGanttModals.ts(99)
cats: write-in-compute=16, createEffect-split=15, primitives-in-callback=4, onMount=3, top-level-read=3, store-unwrap=2, batch=2, import-jsx-type=1, context-default=1, store-builtin-collection=1, write-in-component-body=1, custom-setter-shim=1
risks: structural=23, mechanical=14, behavioral=13
SUMMARY: Gantt.tsx is the orchestrator and the hardest single file in the library to migrate. 46 sites across 4 files (Gantt.tsx + the three helpers it delegates to: ganttSetup.ts, useGanttScroll.ts, ganttConfigStore.ts's setter shim), 9 sync hazards, 7 structural concerns.

The SMA report lists 8 sites for this file (L184, L234, L288, L290, L307, L316, L382, L502). All 8 appear here and I agree with each as a site — but its framing is incomplete in two ways. First, it treats L288 as 'migrate this onMount' when the correct migration is deletion (it duplicates the L290 effect and, under 2.0's single flush window, the duplicate now reads the original's uncommitted writes). Second, it flags none of the writes: six store/signal writes inside effect computes (L298, L302, L309, L320, L321, L324, L395) that THROW REACTIVE_WRITE_IN_OWNED_SCOPE in 2.0 dev — three of them wrapped in untrack(), which does not help because untrack leaves the owner active. It also misses the two showstoppers below.

Showstopper 1 (structural, not flagged by SMA): Gantt.tsx:491 forwards onContainerReady into useGanttScroll.handleContainerReady, which creates six createEffects. That callback fires from GanttContainer's onMount -> onSettled, a children-forbidden scope where creating any primitive throws. useGanttScroll must invert its API to take an Accessor<ContainerAPILike|null> and declare its effects at construction.

Showstopper 2 (correctness, not flagged by SMA): initializeTasks round-trips derived config through signals twice in one synchronous function — setupDates() then five dateStore reads that feed processTasks' bar geometry (ganttSetup.ts:66 -> 69-84), and updateResources() then the resourceIndexMap memo that assigns row indices (ganttSetup.ts:101 -> 102). Both return pre-write values under deferred writes, so on first run every bar is positioned against `new Date()` instead of the task bounds and every task gets no row index. This needs setupDates/updateResources to return their computed values, not a flush() patch.

Also unflagged: useGanttStores' optional-provider pattern crashes outside a provider (createContext with no default now throws), the ganttConfigStore.makeSetter shim computes `prev` and its return value from committed state, and the view-mode effect's prevViewMode guard plus dateStore.changeViewMode's internal generateDates() both read state they just staged.

Mechanical work is small: 2 import lines, 7 top-level props reads (STRICT_READ warnings only, all one-shot reads that want untrack), one batch() removal. Everything else is compute/apply splits (14) or redesign.
  STRUCT: onContainerReady creates reactive primitives from inside a children-forbidden scope. GanttContainer.tsx:186 invokes props.onContainerReady from inside its onMount (-> onSettled in 2.0). Gantt.tsx:491 forwards that to useGanttScroll.handleContainerReady, which creates up to six createEffects (useGanttScroll.ts L58/68/71/78/93/96). Creating ANY primitive inside onSettled — directly or through a helper it calls — throws in 2.0. This is not a local rewrite: useGanttScroll's API contract ('call me imperatively and I'll wire up reactivity') must invert to 'take an Accessor<ContainerAPILike|null> and declare effects at construction'. Signature becomes useGanttScroll(containerApi) called at Gantt.tsx:248, each effect opening with `const api = containerApi(); if (!api) return;`. The onContainerReady callback then shrinks to just setContainerApi(api) (a legal write in onSettled). Note the same pattern likely repeats at every other onContainerReady/onReady consumer in the repo.
  STRUCT: runSetup / initializeTasks is a write-into-owned-scope pipeline invoked from four places, three of which are effect computes. Gantt.tsx L298, L302 and L324 all call it under untrack() — the classic 1.x escape hatch that 2.0 explicitly closes, since untrack disables tracking but leaves the owner active. Every setter it reaches (taskStore.clear/updateTasks, dateStore.setupDates, ganttConfig's five setters, resourceStore.updateResources, plus setRelationships/setLegacyResources) throws REACTIVE_WRITE_IN_OWNED_SCOPE. The fix is systematic: all three effects split into compute (dependency reads only, returning plain values) + apply (all runSetup calls). The three untrack wrappers then delete themselves.
  STRUCT: initializeTasks round-trips derived configuration through signals inside one synchronous function. It writes the date store (L66), then reads five date-store accessors back (L69-73, L75-84) to build the config that positions every bar; and it writes the resource store (L101) then reads the derived resourceIndexMap memo back (L102) to assign row indices. Under 1.x's synchronous writes this was merely indirect; under 2.0 deferred writes it is simply wrong on every call. This wants a data-flow redesign — setupDates and updateResources should RETURN the values initializeTasks needs — not a flush() sprinkled between the lines. flush() would work but couples task reinitialization to a forced global flush and hides the coupling.
  STRUCT: The optional-provider pattern (useGanttStores returning undefined outside <GanttProvider>) is a load-bearing API of this library and stops working. createContext<GanttStores>() with no default makes useContext THROW ContextNotFoundError in 2.0, so the documented bare `<Gantt tasks={...} />` form crashes on the first line of the component. Fix in src/contexts/GanttStores.tsx: createContext<GanttStores | null>(null) and widen the return type. Same applies to GanttEventsContext (contexts/GanttEvents.tsx L23), whose useGanttEvents already branches on a falsy context to return no-op handlers — that branch becomes unreachable-by-throw.
  STRUCT: Duplicate initialization (onMount L288 + createEffect L290) is a 1.x ordering artifact that becomes actively harmful. In 2.0 both run inside the same flush window and the second pass reads the first pass's uncommitted stores, then overwrites the first pass's output. The onMount should be deleted rather than translated to onSettled — createEffect's apply already runs on first settle.
  STRUCT: The four stores this component composes (taskStore, ganttConfigStore, resourceStore, selectionStore) each carry their own 2.0 breaks that determine the shape of these effects' apply bodies, so they must be migrated together with this file, not after it: taskStore.ts imports createStore/reconcile/produce from 'solid-js/store' (package moved to 'solid-js'; produce removed) and uses path setters at L100 (setTasks(id, taskData)), L134 (setTasks(id, undefined) — which in 2.0 keeps the key; needs `delete draft[id]`) and produce at L111/L161; ganttConfigStore.ts uses createStore + produce + the makeSetter path-setter shim and holds a Set (expandedTasks) inside a store, which 2.0 does not proxy.
  STRUCT: Test suites touching this component will need flush() after every write before asserting. Any test that calls runSetup/initializeTasks (or renders <Gantt> and immediately asserts on taskStore.tasks, relationships(), rowLayouts() or bar geometry) reads committed state and will see the pre-write value. Also relevant to tooling: with @solidjs/vite-plugin 3.0.0-next.35, test.environment must stay jsdom (its default in test mode) — setting 'node' resolves the SERVER build, which has no subscription graph, so none of these effects run at all.
