### lib-hooks  effort=M conf=high sites=45 hazards={'high': 2, 'medium': 3, 'low': 3} topLevelReads=0
files: src/hooks/useDrag.ts(292), src/hooks/useBoxSelect.ts(195), src/hooks/useGanttModals.ts(99), src/hooks/useBarConfig.ts(77)
cats: other=29, createMemo-args=12, onCleanup-in-lifecycle=2, store-unwrap=2
risks: behavioral=29, mechanical=12, structural=4
SUMMARY: Four files, 663 lines, all read in full; imports traced one level into stores/selectionStore.ts, stores/taskStore.ts, stores/ganttConfigStore.ts and consumers components/SummaryBar.tsx, components/TaskLayer.tsx, components/Gantt.tsx, components/Bar.tsx.

MECHANICAL SURFACE IS NEARLY ZERO. All four files import only from 'solid-js' and only symbols that still exist in 2.0 (createSignal, createMemo, onCleanup, Accessor — verified in solid-js-2.0.0-rc.6/types/index.d.ts line 1-2). Grepping the batch for batch/on(/createComputed/produce/unwrap/mergeProps/splitProps/onMount/createEffect/createResource/classList/Index/Provider/createContext/untrack/createSelector returns nothing. No JSX in any of the four (.ts only), so no control-flow, context, ref or DOM-namespace sites. topLevelReadCount is 0 — useBarConfig reads props exclusively inside memo bodies, so no STRICT_READ warnings.

SMA REPORT — both of its entries for this batch are DISPUTED. sma-report.txt:315 (useBoxSelect.ts:189) and :319 (useDrag.ts:250) demand migrating onCleanup to a returned cleanup function. Neither needs to change: onCleanup is still exported from solid-js 2.0, both calls sit at hook top level under a component owner (not inside onSettled/createTrackedEffect, so CLEANUP_IN_FORBIDDEN_SCOPE does not apply), and the onCleanup docstring (signals.d.ts:1-41) names library/custom-primitive internals as its remaining home. Converting to onSettled would be worse — neither hook has settle-phase setup, since both attach their document listeners lazily from a mousedown.

THE REAL WORK IS DEFERRED WRITES. Both gesture hooks use one signal for two jobs — reactive render source and synchronous imperative gate. useDrag reads dragState() at seven internal sites expecting a value it just staged (L79, L84, L130, L155, L161, L177, L196); useBoxSelect does the same once at L135. Verified in the rc.6 runtime that an unowned reader (every DOM event handler and RAF callback) takes the `!c` branch of read() and gets el._value, the committed value. Fix is a plain mirror variable next to the existing `let rafId / pendingMove / dragData`, not a redesign.

TWO HIGH-SEVERITY HAZARDS. (1) useDrag.ts:177 — the consumer's onDragMove writes the task store at L161 and the consumer's onDragEnd re-reads it at L177 in the same stack (SummaryBar.tsx:148 -> TaskLayer.tsx:98 handleResizeEnd -> buildCtx -> resolveResizeConstraints), so constraint resolution runs on pre-drag geometry; needs flush() or an API change that passes final geometry explicitly. (2) useDrag.ts:224 — document listeners are registered in the same synchronous run as the staged setDragState, so any same-task synthetic mousemove reads 'idle' at L130 and the drag is a silent no-op; this makes every synthetic-event test of the drag path fail.

ONE VERIFIED NON-BUG worth recording so it is not "fixed": useBoxSelect.ts:156's `for (const id of hits) sel.add(id)` looks like the classic double-functional-update-in-one-tick failure but is safe — dev.js:4944 computes the updater argument as `_pendingValue === NOT_PENDING ? _value : _pendingValue`, so functional setters chain off staged state. Only non-functional writes and accessor reads see committed state.

BEYOND THE SMA'S SCOPE: 12 createMemo calls (7 in useBarConfig, 5 in useGanttModals) are all legal 1-arg form but change behavior because 2.0 memos are eager and non-lazy by default. useBarConfig runs per Bar, so a chart with hundreds of bars now eagerly computes 7 memos and opens 7 store subscriptions per bar at mount — a mount-time regression on a file whose docstring says it exists for performance. Additionally useGanttModals' two barPosition memos wrap taskStore.getBarPosition, which allocates a fresh object per call, so with default reference equals they notify every frame during a drag; and its getTask memos leak live store proxies into TaskPopup/TaskModal, risking STRICT_READ_UNTRACKED in those components' effect apply phases.

47 sites, 8 sync hazards, 7 structural concerns. Effort M: mostly small local edits, but useDrag's mirror-variable refactor and the L161/L177 flush must be done together and verified against a real drag.
  STRUCT: useDrag's core design — one createSignal serving both as the render source and as a synchronous imperative gate — is the single structural item in this batch. Seven internal call sites (L79, L84, L130, L155, L161, L177, L196) read `dragState()` expecting the value just written. The fix is not a rewrite of the state machine but a discipline change the file already applies to its other state: keep `dragState`/`isDragging` as signals for output only, and add `let dragStateNow: DragState = 'idle'` next to the existing `let rafId`, `let pendingMove`, `let dragData` (L73-75) as the authoritative synchronous truth. Every guard reads the mirror; every setter writes both. isDragging should simultaneously collapse into `createMemo(() => dragState() !== 'idle')`, removing a second independently-staged node. Do this before touching anything else in the file — it resolves five of the nine sync hazards at once.
  STRUCT: useDrag's public API contract assumes synchronous propagation across the hook boundary. `onDragMove` is where consumers write the task store, `onDragEnd` fires in the same synchronous stack at L177, and the actual consumer chain (SummaryBar.tsx:148 -> TaskLayer.tsx:98 handleResizeEnd -> buildCtx -> resolveResizeConstraints) re-reads the store to resolve constraints. No amount of local rewriting inside useDrag fixes a consumer that reads back what a sibling callback wrote. Two viable designs: (1) a flush() between the final onDragMove and onDragEnd — one line, preserves the current contract, costs one full graph flush per gesture end; or (2) widen DragMove/DragData so onDragEnd receives the resolved final geometry as data and consumers never re-read the store. (2) is the better end state; (1) is the correct first migration step.
  STRUCT: useBoxSelect has the same dual-role signal problem as useDrag but in miniature and with a much smaller blast radius: only `overlay()` at L135 is read imperatively. Worth calling out that L135-L137 is otherwise the model 2.0 pattern — capture into a local BEFORE writing, then use the local — and the rest of the drag code in this repo should be brought to that shape.
  STRUCT: useBarConfig is a per-instance memo factory (7 memos x every Bar) and 2.0's switch to eager, non-lazy memos silently converts it from a lazy-evaluation optimization into a mount-time cost. Its own header comment says it exists to avoid repeated work. Blanket-adding `{ lazy: true }` to all seven restores 1.x semantics and adds autodisposal, but note the tradeoff the option carries (signals.d.ts MemoOptions.lazy): a lazy memo that loses all subscribers is torn down and recomputed from scratch on the next read, so for Bars that are virtualized in and out of view this can trade mount cost for churn. Measure before committing to lazy on the always-read ones (readonly, columnWidth); apply it unconditionally to the rarely-read ones (showExpectedProgress, ignoredPositions, readonlyProgress).
  STRUCT: useBarConfig depends on ganttConfigStore.makeSetter (ganttConfigStore.ts:150-166), a Setter<T> shim over a store path that both reads `state[key]` to compute `prev` for updater-form calls AND returns `state[key]` after `setState(key, next)`. Under deferred writes the trailing `return state[key]` returns the PRE-write value, so the shim's Setter contract ('returns the new value') is broken, and consecutive updater-form calls in one tick chain off committed state rather than staged state — unlike a real signal setter, which chains correctly. This is out of this batch's file list but it is the data source behind all seven useBarConfig memos, so useBarConfig's correctness depends on that shim being fixed. It also uses the removed store path-setter form and `produce` (ganttConfigStore.ts:2, 175, 187, 197) which must migrate to `setState(draft => {...})`.
  STRUCT: useGanttModals memos return live store proxies (taskStore.getTask returns `tasks[id]` directly, taskStore.ts:71-73) into TaskPopup/TaskModal. In 2.0 reading proxy fields inside a createEffect apply callback emits STRICT_READ_UNTRACKED. Either snapshot() at the memo boundary in this hook, or audit the two modal components for apply-phase proxy reads. Snapshotting here is the cheaper fix and is confined to four lines (L47, L52, L57, L62).
  STRUCT: Test posture for this batch: the two gesture hooks are only reachable through synthetic DOM events, and neither has a unit test today (tests/boxSelect.test.ts covers only the pure findBoxHits helper and needs no changes). Any new test for useDrag/useBoxSelect must yield between dispatches — `await Promise.resolve()` or an explicit `flush()` after each dispatchEvent — because mousedown/mousemove/mouseup dispatched back-to-back in one task never reach a microtask checkpoint, and the guards at useDrag.ts:130 and useBoxSelect.ts:135 will read pre-gesture state. Also ensure @solidjs/vite-plugin's vitest posture keeps the 'browser' condition (test.environment must not be 'node'), or the server build resolves and there is no subscription graph at all.
  HAZ[high] src/hooks/useDrag.ts:177 — WRITTEN: the task store, by the consumer's onDragMove invoked at L161 (SummaryBar.tsx:145 -> taskStore.batchMovePositions, which does setTasks(...)). READ: the same task store, by the consumer's onDragEnd invoked at L177 in the same synchronous stack — TaskLayer.tsx:98-102 handleResizeEnd calls buildCtx(taskId) and resolveResizeConstraints(taskId, ctx), both of which re-read task geometry out of the store. In 1.x the L161 write committed synchronously so L177 saw the final dragged position. In 2.0 the write is staged in _pendingValue and, because a DOM event handler runs with no owner (dev.js read(): the `!c` branch returns el._value), the L177 read path gets the PRE-drag geometry. Constraint resolution and any date persistence therefore run against the position the bar had before the last frame — the drag visually lands correctly but the committed dates are off by the final delta, and dependent-task constraint pushes are computed from stale coordinates. || FIX: Import flush from 'solid-js' and call it between the final onDragMove and onDragEnd: `if (pendingMove) { onDragMove?.(pendingMove, dragData, dragStateNow); pendingMove = null; flush(); }`. Better long-term: change the UseDragOptions contract so onDragEnd receives the resolved final geometry as part of DragMove/DragData instead of re-reading the store, which removes the ordering dependency entirely.
  HAZ[high] src/hooks/useDrag.ts:224 — WRITTEN: dragState (L218) and isDragging (L219). READ: dragState at L130 inside handleMouseMove and L155 inside handleMouseUp — handlers that are registered at L224-225 in this very same synchronous run. In a real browser the mousedown handler returns, the JS stack empties, the microtask checkpoint runs the flush, and the next mousemove arrives as a fresh task, so the guards see the committed new value. But any code that dispatches mousedown and then mousemove/mouseup without yielding — every synthetic-event unit test (`el.dispatchEvent(new MouseEvent('mousedown')); document.dispatchEvent(new MouseEvent('mousemove'))`), and any programmatic drag driver — stays inside one task, so no microtask checkpoint occurs between the statements. handleMouseMove reads the committed 'idle', hits `dragState() === 'idle'` at L130, and returns immediately. The drag is a complete no-op with no error: pendingMove is never set, the RAF loop never starts, and onDragMove/onDragEnd never fire. || FIX: Introduce the non-reactive mirror `let dragStateNow: DragState = 'idle';` written next to each setDragState, and switch every internal guard (L79, L84, L130, L155, L161, L177, L196) to read it. The signal then exists purely for rendering. This is zero-cost and matches how the file already treats dragData/rafId/pendingMove. A flush() after L219 also works but pays a full graph flush on every mousedown.
  HAZ[medium] src/hooks/useBoxSelect.ts:135 — WRITTEN: overlay, at L126 from handleMouseMove (visible: true once the 5px threshold is passed). READ: overlay at L135 in handleMouseUp, whose result drives `wasDragged` at L136 and is passed as the hit-test box to findBoxHits at L151. When mousemove and mouseup land in separate tasks (normal browser use) the flush has run and this is correct. When they are dispatched in one task — synthetic-event tests, or any driver that replays a gesture synchronously — overlay() returns the committed HIDDEN, `wasDragged` is false, and control takes the click-not-drag branch at L145: instead of selecting the boxed tasks the hook CLEARS the selection. A silent, exactly-inverted outcome. || FIX: Mirror the rect in a plain variable: `let overlayNow: BoxOverlay = HIDDEN;` assigned alongside setOverlay at L126, L137 and L179, and read `overlayNow` at L135 instead of `overlay()`. The signal stays for TaskLayer.tsx:293-301's JSX. Note the surrounding code is already correct in shape — L135 captures into `current` before the L137 write and everything downstream uses `current` — so this is a one-line read swap, not a restructure.
  HAZ[medium] src/hooks/useDrag.ts:196 — WRITTEN: dragState('idle') at L183 in handleMouseUp, while `dragData = null` at L185 takes effect immediately. READ: dragState at L196 in startDrag, which guards against starting a second drag. The two truths diverge for the rest of the task. Direction (a): mouseup then a programmatic/synthetic mousedown in the same task — L196 reads the stale 'dragging_bar' and refuses the new drag. Direction (b): two mousedowns in the same task (double-dispatch, or a synthetic click that fires mousedown twice) — L196 reads the stale 'idle' both times, startDrag runs twice, and document mousemove/mouseup listeners are registered twice while L180-181 only ever removes one pair, leaking a listener that keeps firing after the gesture ends. Every other guard in the file ANDs against `!dragData` and so survives; L196 is the only one that does not. || FIX: Read the `dragStateNow` mirror at L196. As a cheap standalone patch that fixes direction (a) only, add the dragData check the other guards have: `if (dragStateNow !== 'idle' || dragData) return;`.
  HAZ[medium] src/hooks/useDrag.ts:221 — WRITTEN: dragState (L218) and isDragging (L219). READ: whatever the consumer's onDragStart callback reads, invoked at L221 in the same synchronous run. The hook is partly protected because it threads `state` through as an explicit argument, and the current consumer (SummaryBar.tsx:105-124) only reads the x()/y() memos and taskStore.getBarPosition(), none of which depend on dragState. But the hook's public contract advertises `dragState`/`isDragging` accessors alongside a callback that fires before they are readable, so any consumer that writes `if (isDragging())` inside onDragStart silently gets false. The same trap applies to the demo consumers (GanttPerfIsolate.tsx:1180, index-test.tsx:1429/1528) which destructure dragState. || FIX: Keep threading `state` explicitly (already done) and add a doc comment on UseDragOptions.onDragStart stating that dragState()/isDragging() still report the previous value inside the callback. If a consumer genuinely needs the reactive value, call flush() between L219 and L221.

### lib-virtualization  effort=M conf=high sites=29 hazards={'high': 4, 'medium': 3, 'low': 2} topLevelReads=2

SUMMARY: 4 files, 870 lines, 28 sites. Mechanical core is small and matches the advisory report: 3 <Index> -> <For keyed={false}> (TaskLayer.tsx:217,249; TaskLayerMinimal.tsx:130, callback shape identical so bodies are untouched), 2 import lines dropping the removed `Index` and moving `JSX` to @solidjs/web (TaskLayer.tsx:1, TaskLayerMinimal.tsx:1), and 4 createMemo(fn, initialValue, {equals}) -> createMemo(fn, {equals}) in createVirtualViewport.ts (50,69,107,124) which are provably behavior-preserving because no compute reads prev and rangeEquals is undefined-tolerant. useTaskVirtualization.ts needs no import changes at all. Beyond the report: 2 component-body STRICT_READ reads (TaskLayer.tsx:197-198, props resolved eagerly into useBoxSelect), a disputed advisory finding (useBoxSelect.ts:189 onCleanup is at hook top level under the component owner — no change needed), and three confirmations recorded to prevent over-migration (line 235's default <For>, the variable ref at 204, and user-level untrack over store proxies which does NOT emit STRICT_READ_UNTRACKED — that diagnostic requires an internal strictReadLabel). The real risk is not syntactic: 9 sync hazards, 3 high. (1) Both grouping caches invalidate on task-key COUNT (useTaskVirtualization.ts:78, TaskLayerMinimal.tsx:53) — 1.x flushed between unbatched writes so the memo saw intermediate counts; rc.6 coalesces a tick into one flush, so a same-tick add+remove leaves the cache permanently stale. (2) Bar.tsx writes updateBarPosition then calls onResizeEnd in the same handler, and TaskLayer.tsx:98 -> taskLayerConstraints.ts:87 re-reads getBarPosition for that bar, cascading successors from pre-write geometry. (3) ganttConfigStore mutates an `expandedTasks` Set in place inside a store; rc.6 does not proxy Sets, so expansion changes stop notifying splitTaskIds entirely. Plus the pool high-water counters (lines 206/227) are unrollbackable side effects in memo computes that should fold through `prev`. Sequence: stores batch first, then constraints API, then this batch.
  STRUCT: Pooling contract: both <Index> sites feed <For keyed={false}> SPARSE arrays (`new Array(poolSize)` at useTaskVirtualization.ts:208/229, and Array.prototype.map preserving holes at line 218). Before shipping, prove rc.6's non-keyed For (a) reads holes as undefined rather than skipping/truncating rows, and (b) reuses the row at index i in place — Index-equivalent pooling is load-bearing here: a remount tears down Bar's document-level mousemove/mouseup listeners mid-drag, which is exactly what the ID-based design at useTaskVirtualization.ts:99-100 exists to prevent.
  STRUCT: The two closure caches (useTaskVirtualization.ts:70-96 and the copy in TaskLayerMinimal.tsx:45-73) and the two pool counters (lines 201-232) are hidden mutable state inside memo computes. rc.6 treats computes as pure and re-runnable, so this needs a redesign — derive from `prev` (pool counters) and from tracked Object.keys or a store revision signal (grouping cache) — not a local patch. This is the largest non-mechanical item in the batch.
  STRUCT: Cross-batch dependency: ganttConfigStore's `expandedTasks` Set inside a createStore (mutated in place via produce) is unproxied in rc.6, and taskStore's produce/path setters (taskStore.ts:100 setTasks(id, obj), :110 produce, :129/:139 reconcile, :134 setTasks(id, undefined) as delete) must all be rewritten to the single draft form. useTaskVirtualization/TaskLayer are pure consumers of those, so their correctness cannot be validated until the store batch lands — sequence stores first, then this batch.
  STRUCT: TaskLayer's constraint pipeline (handleConstrainPosition / handleResizeEnd / handleClampBatchDelta -> taskLayerConstraints.ts -> constraintEngine) is built on 1.x's synchronous read-your-own-write. Getting it right under deferred writes is an API change, not a rewrite of individual lines: geometry must flow forward as explicit arguments, and the read+cascade-write pair belongs inside one store draft. Decide this shape once, in the constraints batch, and have TaskLayer adopt it.
  STRUCT: useBoxSelect (called from TaskLayer.tsx:196) is rc.6-clean as written, but its deps are captured as plain values at TaskLayer.tsx:197-198. If the deps interface is changed to accessors to fix the STRICT_READ warnings, useBoxSelect.ts:140/152 must be updated in the same change.
  STRUCT: No <Index>-adjacent Solid API in this batch touches removed lifecycle/store surface: zero onMount, zero createEffect, zero batch/on/createComputed/createResource/produce/unwrap/mergeProps/splitProps, zero classList/use:/on:/attr:, zero Context.Provider, zero solid-js/web or solid-js/store imports, zero @solid-primitives usage, zero tests. The remaining tooling work (tsconfig jsxImportSource '@solidjs/web', vite-plugin-solid -> @solidjs/vite-plugin) belongs to the config batch but gates typechecking of the two JSX.Element annotations in this batch (TaskLayer.tsx:65, TaskLayerMinimal.tsx:31).
  HAZ[high] src/hooks/useTaskVirtualization.ts:78 — WRITE: any taskStore mutation that changes the key SET without changing the key COUNT in one tick — e.g. removeTask('a') (setTasks('a', undefined)) followed by updateTask('b', t) , or updateTasks(arr) replacing N tasks with a different N-length set. READ: `taskKeys.length === cachedTaskCount` at line 78 returns the stale `cachedGrouping`. Under 1.x each unbatched setter flushed render effects synchronously, so the visibleTaskIds memo re-ran between the two writes and observed the intermediate count. rc.6 stages every write in a tick and flushes once, so both writes collapse into ONE memo recompute with an unchanged count — the grouping (and therefore every rendered bar) silently keeps deleted tasks and drops added ones until an unrelated count change occurs. || FIX: Delete the count heuristic. Track the key set for real: `const tasksByResource = createMemo(() => { const t = props.taskStore?.tasks; if (!t) return new Map(); const keys = Object.keys(t); return untrack(() => buildGroups(t, keys)); });` — Object.keys on the proxy inside the tracked compute subscribes to ownKeys, so add/remove/replace all invalidate. If the O(keys) scan is too costly, have taskStore expose a monotonic `revision` signal bumped by every mutating method and cache on that.
  HAZ[high] src/components/TaskLayerMinimal.tsx:53 — Identical defect to useTaskVirtualization.ts:78, duplicated: `taskKeys.length === cachedTaskCount` (line 53) plus `cachedTaskCount = taskKeys.length` (line 57), consumed by the visibleTasks memo at line 80. Same-tick add+remove now coalesces into one flush and the cache never invalidates, so TaskLayerMinimal (the perf-demo path, Gantt.tsx:597) renders a stale task list. || FIX: Apply the same rewrite as useTaskVirtualization.ts:78, or better, factor the grouping into a single shared helper so the two copies cannot drift again.
  HAZ[high] src/utils/taskLayerConstraints.ts:87 — WRITE-THEN-READ across a call boundary. Bar.tsx:300-302 does `props.taskStore?.updateBarPosition(taskId, { width: newWidth }); onDateChange?.(...); onResizeEnd?.(taskId);` inside one keydown handler. onResizeEnd is TaskLayer.tsx:98 -> resolveResizeConstraints, which at line 87 READS `ctx.taskStore.getBarPosition(taskId)` and feeds taskBar.x/taskBar.width into resolveConstraints, then WRITES cascade updates at line 99. In rc.6 the updateBarPosition write is still staged, so getBarPosition returns the PRE-resize width: successors are cascaded to positions computed from the old geometry, and because the cascade write itself lands in the same flush, the wrong positions are committed rather than corrected. Same shape on the drag path (useBarDrag.ts:271-278) whenever mouseup lands in the same tick as the final mousemove. || FIX: Stop re-reading state the caller just wrote. Widen the callback to carry geometry — `onResizeEnd(taskId, { x, width })` from Bar.tsx and useBarDrag.ts, threaded through TaskLayer.tsx:98 into resolveResizeConstraints(taskId, ctx, geom) — and use `geom` in place of the getBarPosition call at line 87. If the signature must stay, wrap the resize block in `flush(() => { ... })` at the Bar call site so the write commits before the read; that restores 1.x ordering at the cost of a synchronous cascade.
  HAZ[medium] src/utils/taskLayerConstraints.ts:60 — READ-then-WRITE over the same rows. constrainTaskPosition reads the dragged bar (line 60) and — inside resolveConstraints via the bound getBarPosition/getTask from buildContext (lines 40-41) — reads every successor's committed position, then writes those successors with updateBarPosition (lines 68-73). Invoked from TaskLayer.tsx:88 on every drag tick (useBarDrag.ts:155-164 and :183-196). Correct only while exactly one invocation happens per commit. rc.6 coalesces all writes in a tick, so two constrain calls in the same tick (coalesced pointer events, a keyboard repeat burst, or a batch-drag path that also calls handleClampBatchDelta at TaskLayer.tsx:146 while batchMovePositions from the previous tick is still staged) both compute cascades against the same stale base; the second overwrite wins and the intermediate delta is lost. || FIX: Do the read and the cascade write in a single store draft so reads see staged state: `setTasks(draft => { for (const succ of successors) { const bar = draft[succ]?._bar; ...compute...; if (bar) Object.assign(bar, update); } })`, returning constrainedX as the function result. Alternatively keep the current structure but make useBarDrag rAF-coalesce so at most one constrain call runs per flush.
  HAZ[high] src/stores/ganttConfigStore.ts:171 — `expandedTasks: Set<string>` lives inside a createStore and is mutated IN PLACE via `setState('expandedTasks', produce(set => set.add/delete(taskId)))` (toggleTaskExpansion line 171, expandTask line 188, collapseTask line 198). rc.6 does not proxy built-in collections, so an in-place Set mutation notifies nothing at all. Consumer: useTaskVirtualization.ts:66/161 isTaskExpanded -> splitTaskIds's regular/summary/expanded bucketing, which drives TaskLayer.tsx:235 (<For expandedIds>) and both pooled lists. Result after migration: expanding or collapsing a task never re-renders the bars. Aggravating factor already present in 1.x — the call at line 161 is inside the untrack() opened at line 152, so splitTaskIds has no subscription to expansion either way and currently only refreshes incidentally. || FIX: (1) Reassign instead of mutate: `setState(s => { const next = new Set(s.expandedTasks); next.has(id) ? next.delete(id) : next.add(id); s.expandedTasks = next; })` — replacing the property is what notifies for a non-proxied collection. (2) In useTaskVirtualization, hoist a real dependency out of the untrack: read `props.ganttConfig?.expandedTasks?.()` (or an expansion version accessor) next to `isSimpleMode()` at line 149 so splitTaskIds recomputes on expansion change; keep the per-id `checkExpanded` memoized map inside the untrack for cost.
  HAZ[medium] src/hooks/useTaskVirtualization.ts:206 — `maxRegularCount` (line 206) and `maxSummaryCount` (line 227) are monotonic closure counters written from inside memo computes. rc.6 computes may run and have their result discarded (transition/optimistic re-runs, loading-boundary retries) — the discarded run still permanently raises the high-water mark, so the DOM pool grows on speculative work that was never committed. Also, since the counters live outside the reactive graph, any recompute ordering change between splitTaskIds and the pooled memos leaves pool size and content out of step for one flush (a stale pool slot renders a bar for a task that is no longer visible, since display:none is driven by the accessor value, not the pool length). || FIX: Fold the high-water mark through the memo's own `prev` argument (rc.6 ComputeFunction receives prev): return `{ max, pool }` from a single createMemo and expose `() => memo().pool`. State then rolls back with the computation and the pool size is always derived from the same run that produced its contents.
  HAZ[medium] src/hooks/useTaskVirtualization.ts:218 — pooledRegularTasks snapshots store proxies untracked (`untrack(() => ids.map(id => tasksObj[id]))`). taskStore.updateBarPosition replaces `state[id]` with a brand-new object, so the captured proxy can point at a detached raw object; this memo only re-runs when pooledRegularIds changes (i.e. on scroll/visibility), never on a position write. Under rc.6 the staleness window widens because writes land at flush rather than synchronously. Currently masked by Bar.tsx:113-117, which deliberately re-looks-up `props.taskStore.tasks[v.id]` instead of trusting the passed accessor. || FIX: Keep Bar's re-lookup (add a comment tying it to this memo so it is not 'simplified' during migration), or stop passing objects entirely and pass ids like pooledSummaryIds does, letting Bar own the single tracked store read.

### lib-bars  effort=L conf=high sites=43 hazards={'high': 4, 'medium': 1, 'low': 3} topLevelReads=8
files: src/components/Bar.tsx(659), src/components/BarMinimal.tsx(121), src/components/SummaryBar.tsx(285), src/components/SubtaskBar.tsx(116), src/components/ExpandedTaskContainer.tsx(181), src/hooks/useBarDrag.ts(297), src/hooks/useBarConfig.ts(77), src/hooks/useDrag.ts(292), src/contexts/GanttEvents.tsx(69), src/stores/taskStore.ts(270)
cats: other=12, import-jsx-type=6, store-path-setter=4, store-produce=3, context-default=2, classList=2, jsx-attr=2, createMemo-args=2, top-level-read=1, show-match-callback=1, index-for=1, store-builtin-collection=1, custom-setter-shim=1, onCleanup-in-lifecycle=1, context-provider=1, store-reconcile=1, store-delete=1, import-store=1
risks: mechanical=19, behavioral=16, structural=8
SUMMARY: Read all 5 batch files in full plus 5 followed helpers (useBarDrag.ts, useBarConfig.ts, useDrag.ts, contexts/GanttEvents.tsx, stores/taskStore.ts) and the consuming constraint utils. 40 sites, 8 sync hazards.\n\nThe purely mechanical surface is small: all five files need the same JSX-type import split (JSX is no longer exported from solid-js — confirmed against rc.6 types/index.d.ts; Show and For still are), and two tabIndex -> tabindex renames (rc.6 jsx.d.ts declares only lowercase tabindex, with no string index signature, so this is a build error). BarMinimal.tsx, SubtaskBar.tsx and ExpandedTaskContainer.tsx need NOTHING ELSE — their <Show> takes element children and their <For> is already the default keyed form whose callback shape is unchanged.\n\nTwo findings dominate. (1) A hard blocker the SMA missed entirely: Bar.tsx:108 calls useGanttEvents(), backed by a default-less createContext at GanttEvents.tsx:23. In 2.0 that throws ContextNotFoundError outside a provider, and Gantt.tsx never mounts GanttEventsProvider (only three demo shells do) while BarDemo/ShowcaseDemo render <Bar> bare — so every Bar in the primary render path throws on setup. One-line fix, must land first. (2) The drag pipeline is architecturally built on synchronous store propagation, with the assumption written down as a comment at useBarDrag.ts:270. useDrag.handleMouseUp deliberately runs the final onDragMove (which writes) and then onDragEnd (which re-reads the store) in one synchronous stack; under deferred writes the drop position (useBarDrag.ts:271), the drag-end progress (line 281), the keyboard-resize constraint cascade (Bar.tsx:302) and the summary-bar drop cascade (SummaryBar.tsx:148) are all one tick stale, silently persisting wrong dates and progress to the host app. A single flush() at useDrag.ts:163 fixes four of the eight hazards as a stopgap; the correct fix is to thread the applied geometry from onDragMove into onDragEnd and delete the store reads.\n\nAlso: useBarDrag.ts:250 does a whole-task read-modify-write that clobbers concurrently-staged writes; taskStore's updateBarPosition/batchMovePositions/updateTask/removeTask all use removed APIs (produce, path setters, undefined-as-delete), and the recommended draft-setter rewrite is what breaks SummaryBar.tsx:63's memo-over-a-store-proxy. topLevelReadCount is 8, all in one object literal at Bar.tsx:195-205, where they also genuinely freeze the handler set at setup.\n\nSMA cross-check: sma-report.txt lists ZERO sites in the five batch files; its only entry touching anything I followed is useDrag.ts:250 (onCleanup), which I DISPUTE — onCleanup is still exported in rc.6 and valid at hook/component-owner scope. Everything else here is net-new.
  STRUCT: The entire drag pipeline is built on the assumption that store writes propagate synchronously, and it is stated as an explicit design intent in a comment (useBarDrag.ts:270 'Read directly from store to avoid reactive timing issues'). useDrag.handleMouseUp (useDrag.ts:160-177) deliberately runs the last onDragMove and then onDragEnd in one call stack so the end handler can observe the move's effect. In 2.0 that ordering is exactly wrong. This needs a contract change, not a local rewrite: onDragEnd must receive the geometry that onDragMove applied, rather than re-deriving it from the store. Recommendation: extend UseDragOptions so onDragMove can return/record an applied-state object that useDrag threads into onDragEnd, then delete every store read from useBarDrag.onDragEnd and SummaryBar.onDragEnd. A single flush() at useDrag.ts:163 is the cheap stopgap and fixes four of the eight hazards, but it papers over the inverted dependency.
  STRUCT: Bar.tsx:108 + contexts/GanttEvents.tsx:23 is a hard blocker, not a warning. createContext<GanttEventHandlers>() with no default makes useContext throw ContextNotFoundError in 2.0 (verified in rc.6 client/core.d.ts:31 and :89-102), and useGanttEvents' `if (!context)` no-op fallback becomes unreachable dead code. Gantt.tsx never mounts GanttEventsProvider — only the three demo shells do — and BarDemo.tsx / ShowcaseDemo.tsx render <Bar> bare. So under rc.6 the library's primary render path throws during Bar setup. Fix is one line (createContext<GanttEventHandlers | null>(null)) but it must be scheduled first or nothing in this batch can be tested.
  STRUCT: Bar.tsx's getTask() re-lookup (lines 110-118) exists solely to defeat <Index> snapshot pooling — the comment names <Index> explicitly. TaskLayer.tsx:217/249 is where those <Index> sites live (SMA-flagged) and they become <For keyed={false}>, whose callback still yields an item ACCESSOR, so the dual value-or-accessor handling in getTask() and in the taskPosition accessor at Bar.tsx:178-184 remains correct. Still, once TaskLayer migrates, re-validate whether the store re-lookup is still needed at all: if <For keyed={false}> hands down a live accessor over the store proxy, the re-lookup is now pure overhead on every x()/y()/width()/height() read (four store lookups per bar per frame).
  STRUCT: SummaryBar.tsx:63 memoizes the raw _bar store proxy while Bar.tsx:171-174 documents deliberately NOT doing that. The two components disagree about the same problem. Today SummaryBar's memo happens to invalidate because taskStore replaces state[id] wholesale; the recommended 2.0 rewrite of updateBarPosition/batchMovePositions to draft mutations removes that whole-object churn and the memo stops invalidating. Rendering survives (proxy property reads still track in the consuming JSX effect) but the memo is then load-bearing-looking dead code and a trap for anyone who later adds snapshot()/deep()/an identity comparison to it. Align SummaryBar on Bar's pattern: read the fields directly, no memo over the proxy.
  STRUCT: Eager memo creation is a per-bar cost multiplier. useBarConfig creates 7 memos per Bar (useBarConfig.ts:37-75) and Bar adds 4 more (lines 134, 335, 346, 370). In 1.x these were lazy; in 2.0 createMemo computes on creation, so a 1000-row chart performs ~11,000 eager computations at mount that previously happened on first read (and never at all for off-screen virtualized bars). Given this repo has an explicit perf posture (BarMinimal.tsx, GanttPerfIsolate.tsx, prof.start instrumentation), benchmark mount time after migration and apply { lazy: true } to the config memos if it regresses.
  STRUCT: RowLayout.taskPositions is a Map (rowLayoutCalculator.ts:10) read through props in ExpandedTaskContainer.tsx:72, 84 and 121, and TaskLayer.tsx:39 holds a Map<string, RowLayout>. 2.0 does not proxy built-in collections. These are currently safe because they are memo outputs reassigned wholesale, but this is an invariant nobody has written down — add it as a comment or move to a plain object keyed by task id so the constraint is enforced by the type.
  HAZ[high] src/hooks/useBarDrag.ts:271 — Drop position is read from the store one tick stale. useDrag.handleMouseUp (useDrag.ts:160-163) runs the final onDragMove — which WRITES via taskStore.updateBarPosition (useBarDrag.ts:164/196-199/208) or batchMovePositions (line 149) — and then, still in the same synchronous stack, calls onDragEnd (useDrag.ts:177). onDragEnd's first act (useBarDrag.ts:271) is `deps.taskStore?.getBarPosition(t.id)`, which under deferred writes returns the PREVIOUSLY COMMITTED _bar. Both fallbacks are equally stale: `deps.x()` / `deps.width()` resolve to Bar.tsx:175/185, which read the same store proxy. The resulting {x, width} is handed to onDateChange -> Gantt.handleDateChange (Gantt.tsx:468-477) -> dateStore.xToDate, so the host application is told the task landed at the position it occupied one drag frame earlier. On a fast flick-and-release the error is the full final snap step (one columnWidth); with batchMovePositions every dependent task is reported wrong too. The inline comment on line 270 ('Read directly from store to avoid reactive timing issues') is precisely the assumption 2.0 invalidates. || FIX: Preferred: make onDragMove record what it wrote. Add `let lastGeom: { x: number; width: number } | null = null;` in useBarDrag, set it in each write branch (e.g. `lastGeom = { x: newX, width: originalWidth }` for dragging_bar, `{ x: newX, width: newWidth }` for dragging_left, `{ x: data.originalX as number, width: newWidth }` for dragging_right), reset it to null in onDragStart, and in onDragEnd use `const pos = lastGeom ?? deps.taskStore?.getBarPosition(t.id)`. Minimal alternative: `import { flush } from 'solid-js'` and call `flush()` in useDrag.ts immediately after the pendingMove block at line 163, which fixes this and the SummaryBar/onProgressChange hazards at once.

Deferred writes mean the memo still holds the committed value, so `onProgressChange(id, oldProgress)` fires. In 1.x the store write propagated synchronously and the memo recomputed on read, so this worked. Every progress drag now persists the wrong value to the host app. || FIX: Hoist the computed progress out of the dragging_progress branch: store it (`data['finalProgress'] = newProgress` at line 254, or a hook-local `lastProgress`) and report `deps.handlers.onProgressChange?.(t.id, (data['finalProgress'] as number) ?? t.progress)` at line 281. That resolves to TaskLayer.handleResizeEnd (TaskLayer.tsx:98-102) -> resolveResizeConstraints (utils/taskLayerConstraints.ts:83-105), whose first statement is `ctx.taskStore.getBarPosition?.(taskId)` and which then feeds `taskBar.x, taskBar.width` into resolveConstraints to compute and WRITE cascadeUpdates for every successor. Under deferred writes taskBar.width is the OLD width, so successors are pushed by the wrong amount — the bar grows but its dependents do not move, leaving a visibly violated finish-to-start link until some later unrelated write re-runs the cascade. || FIX: Either (a) `import { flush } from 'solid-js'` and call `flush()` between line 300 and line 302, or (b) better, change resolveResizeConstraints to take the new geometry explicitly — `resolveResizeConstraints(taskId, ctx, { x: x(), width: newWidth })` — and drop its internal getBarPosition read. ### lib-arrows  effort=M conf=high sites=33 hazards={'high': 3, 'medium': 2, 'low': 1} topLevelReads=0
files: src/components/Arrow.tsx(292), src/components/ArrowLayer.tsx(231), src/components/ArrowLayerBatched.tsx(297)
cats: other=17, import-jsx-type=6, createMemo-args=4, jsx-attr=3, config=2, docs=1
risks: mechanical=17, behavioral=10, structural=6
SUMMARY: Three pure-render components with no effects, no onMount, no store writes, no context, no refs, no directives and no Index — so the mechanical surface is small and entirely import-shaped: six JSX-namespace sites (Arrow.tsx:4/178, ArrowLayer.tsx:1/59, ArrowLayerBatched.tsx:1/74) must move to `import type { JSX } from '@solidjs/web'`, plus tsconfig jsxImportSource and the vite-plugin-solid swap. For/createMemo/untrack all still ship from solid-js; the default keyed For overload, fragment children in For rows, SVG hyphenated attributes and undefined-removes-attribute all verified unchanged against rc.6 types, so every JSX element here is migration-inert. Zero top-level reactive reads in any component body. The real work is reactivity architecture, not syntax. Both layers deliberately hide their dependencies behind untrack — ArrowLayer.tsx:103 suppresses the store's ownKeys tracking (confirmed live in dev.js:9271) leaving positionMap dependent on nothing but draggingTaskId, and ArrowLayerBatched.tsx:139-140 suppresses every position read, leaving `void props.positionVersion` (123) as the sole live dependency. Combined with deferred writes this yields the batch's top hazard: ArrowLayer's eager first memo run reads pre-flush state and, having no task dependency, never recovers — the arrow layer renders permanently blank with no error, no warning and no test to catch it. A third high-severity hazard sits one level down in the helper both files read through: taskStore.updateBarPosition (108) guards a staged write with a committed-state read, so a task created and positioned in the same run never gets its _bar, and both layers then silently drop it (ArrowLayerBatched:141, ArrowLayer:106). Secondary: ArrowLayerBatched's module-level caches (70-72) are cross-instance and cross-HMR shared while two separate memos invalidate and consume them through those bindings. One notable free win — 2.0's write coalescing actually fixes a latent 1.x two-phase-load bug in taskCount(), so do not add a flush() between updateTasks and updateBarPosition.
  STRUCT: ArrowLayerBatched's module-level mutable caches (lines 70-72) are shared by every mounted instance and survive Vite HMR reloads, and they encode a cache-invalidation protocol split across two memos (spatialIndex clears at 125-128, batchedPaths reads/writes at 206-260). This needs to become instance state threaded through spatialIndex's return value, not a local rewrite. It is a pre-existing bug that 2.0's flush-time memo scheduling makes materially harder to reason about.
  STRUCT: Both arrow layers replace real store tracking with hand-rolled dependency protocols: ArrowLayer wraps all task reads in untrack (line 103) and depends on nothing but draggingTaskId; ArrowLayerBatched wraps all position reads in untrack (139-140) and depends on a manually incremented positionVersion counter (123). These were 1.x workarounds for synchronous per-write propagation storms during scroll. Solid 2.0 already coalesces writes into a single microtask flush, which is the same optimization done correctly by the runtime. The migration should delete both untrack hacks and re-measure, rather than porting the workarounds; if a derived index is still wanted, `createStore(fn, seed, { key })` / createProjection is the 2.0-native shape for a reconciled spatial index.
  STRUCT: ArrowLayer sources positions from two different reactive graphs depending on drag state (batched positionMap when idle, live per-Arrow getBarPosition when dragging — lines 157-160 feeding Arrow.tsx:181-187). In 2.0 the switch edge lands on a flush boundary, so the frames where draggingTaskId flips render with the outgoing source. Collapsing to one always-live derived source is now viable because the scheduler batches, and would remove the entire cachedPositionsForFilter mechanism.
  STRUCT: Arrow.tsx's header comment (lines 1-2) records a deliberate decision to avoid createMemo because of 'reactive cascades that hurt scroll performance'. That cost model is 1.x-specific: rc.6 memos are eager/non-lazy by default with an explicit `{ lazy: true }` opt-in, and propagation is flush-scheduled. The decision's concrete cost is generatePath running three times per update (lines 244, 256, 258). Re-benchmark under rc.6 before carrying it forward.
  STRUCT: None of the three files has any test coverage (tests/ contains no arrow tests; the only arrow-adjacent files are demos and examples/arrow.html). The two high-severity hazards here — untrack-hidden dependencies producing a silently blank arrow layer, and the stale existence guard in updateBarPosition — are exactly the failure class that produces no error and no warning at runtime. A regression test that mounts ArrowLayer against a taskStore populated in the same synchronous run, calls flush(), and asserts a non-empty positionMap should be written before the migration, not after.
  STRUCT: The SMA report contains zero findings for any file in this batch (grep for 'components/Arrow' returns nothing; its only arrow-adjacent hits are src/demo/ArrowDemo.tsx:350 and src/entries/arrow.tsx:1, both outside this batch). Its rule set covers onMount, one-arg createEffect and createMemo initial values, none of which occur here — so it found nothing, and in particular missed all six JSX-namespace import sites, which are hard compile errors.
  HAZ[high] src/components/ArrowLayer.tsx:103 — positionMap's untrack(...) block suppresses every dependency on task data (verified: the store proxy's ownKeys trap DOES track — dev.js:9271 — so `for (const taskId in tasks)` would subscribe to the key set if it were not wrapped). The memo's only live dependencies are `props.taskStore` identity and `draggingTaskId()`. WRITE: any code path that stages tasks / _bar positions into the taskStore. READ: this memo, whose eager first run happens during ArrowLayer's component setup and reads the last COMMITTED store value. Under 1.x, a parent that wrote tasks synchronously before mounting ArrowLayer had those tasks visible on the memo's first run. Under 2.0 deferred writes, if the mount happens in the same synchronous run as the writes (createRoot body, an event handler, or a setup helper such as utils/ganttSetup), the first run sees the pre-flush state, `positions` is empty, `visibleDependencies` drops every dependency at line 139, and nothing ever re-triggers positionMap — the arrow layer stays permanently blank with no error and no warning. || FIX: Make the dependency real. Minimum: hoist the key-set read out of untrack — `const tasks = props.taskStore?.tasks; const ids = tasks ? Object.keys(tasks) : [];` (tracked via ownKeys) and iterate `ids` inside the untrack for the _bar reads. Better: delete the untrack entirely and let 2.0's flush coalesce the fine-grained _bar invalidations, which is what the untrack was hand-rolling in 1.x. If a manual trigger is preferred, adopt the ArrowLayerBatched positionVersion prop and read it as a used value, not `void`.
  HAZ[high] src/components/ArrowLayerBatched.tsx:123 — `void props.positionVersion;` is the sole tracked dependency that reflects bar movement, because both position reads (lines 139-140) are wrapped in untrack. WRITE: producers call taskStore.updateBarPosition / batchMovePositions and then separately bump the counter (GanttPerfIsolate.tsx:1529 `triggerArrowUpdate`, invoked at 1587 inside a `batch()` that must be deleted in 2.0). READ: this memo re-runs and calls getBarPosition. The 2.0 flush ordering is actually favourable — the counter write and the position writes land in the same flush, so the memo reads committed positions. The hazard is the two failure modes the protocol allows: (a) any producer that writes positions WITHOUT bumping the counter leaves the spatial index and arrowPathCache stale indefinitely, and (b) any caller that bumps the counter and then, in the same synchronous stack, reads arrow output (batchedPaths(), or measures the rendered <path> via getBoundingClientRect) gets pre-flush geometry, because the memo has not recomputed and the DOM has not been patched. || FIX: Delete the counter protocol: remove the untrack at lines 139-140 so getBarPosition subscribes normally, and drop line 123 plus the positionVersion prop (line 40). If the manual protocol must stay for perf, (1) change line 123 to a used read included in the memo's return value so it cannot be elided, (2) delete the now-removed `batch()` wrapper at the producer, and (3) require any synchronous read-back of arrow geometry to call `flush()` first.
  HAZ[high] src/stores/taskStore.ts:108 — Followed one level from both ArrowLayer and ArrowLayerBatched, which read positions through this store. `updateBarPosition` guards its write with a read of COMMITTED state: `if (!tasks[id]) return;` at line 108, before `setTasks(produce(...))` at line 110. Under 2.0 deferred writes, a setup or drag sequence that adds a task and sets its bar position in the same synchronous run has the task write still staged when the guard runs, so the guard sees undefined, returns early, and _bar is never written. getBarPosition then returns null (line 81), ArrowLayerBatched skips the relationship at line 141, and ArrowLayer's positionMap skips it at line 106 — arrows for freshly-created tasks silently never appear. || FIX: Move the existence check inside the draft, where staged state is visible: `setTasks(s => { const t = s[id]; if (!t?._bar) return; t._bar = { ...t._bar, ...position }; })`. This also removes the `produce` import (produce is deleted in 2.0 — its body IS the setter body now) and the path-setter forms at taskStore.ts:100/134.
  HAZ[medium] src/components/Arrow.tsx:186 — While a drag is active ArrowLayer.tsx:158-159 passes positionMap={null}, so every Arrow falls back to this live per-endpoint `props.taskStore.getBarPosition(taskId)` read. WRITE: the pointermove handler stages _bar.x. READ: this call, and any constraint/snap/measurement code that runs later in the same handler. Under deferred writes both the read here and any getBoundingClientRect() on the arrow <path> in the same frame observe the pre-write geometry, because neither the store commit nor the DOM patch has happened yet. In 1.x the store commit was synchronous, so a drag handler could write and immediately re-read to derive a clamp or a cascade offset. || FIX: In drag handlers, derive constraint/clamp math from the value about to be written rather than re-reading the store afterwards (pass the new x explicitly into resolveConstraints instead of calling getBarPosition again). Where a genuine post-write measurement is unavoidable, wrap it: `flush(); const rect = el.getBoundingClientRect();`.
  HAZ[medium] src/components/ArrowLayerBatched.tsx:125 — The spatialIndex memo invalidates module-level caches (arrowPathCache, lastVisibleSet, cachedResult) that a DIFFERENT memo, batchedPaths, both reads (lines 206-210, 229) and writes (lines 212, 232, 260). WRITE: spatialIndex clears the caches. READ: batchedPaths consults them. The ordering is correct today only because batchedPaths destructures spatialIndex() on its first line (169), forcing the clear to run first. In rc.6 memos are eager and scheduled at flush time; if anyone adds `{ lazy: true }`, reorders those reads, or reads batchedPaths() from a scope that has not pulled spatialIndex, batchedPaths serves paths generated from superseded positions. Independently, because the caches are module-scoped (lines 70-72), two mounted instances — or a Vite HMR reload, which preserves module state — cross-contaminate: instance B's mount clears instance A's cache and instance A's setsEqual short-circuit can return instance B's cachedResult. || FIX: Make the caches instance state and pass them through the dataflow rather than through module bindings: have spatialIndex return `{ index, positions, pathCache: new Map<number, ArrowPaths>() }` and let batchedPaths use `spatialIndex().pathCache` plus component-scoped `let lastVisibleSet / cachedResult` declared inside ArrowLayerBatched.

### lib-chrome  effort=M conf=high sites=32 hazards={'low': 2} topLevelReads=0
files: src/components/Grid.tsx(240), src/components/DateHeaders.tsx(250), src/components/ResourceColumn.tsx(212), src/components/ColumnPanel.tsx(240), src/components/TaskDataModal.tsx(262), src/components/TaskDataPopup.tsx(70)
cats: createMemo-args=9, other=8, import-jsx-type=6, jsx-attr=5, top-level-read=3, primitives-in-callback=1
risks: behavioral=16, mechanical=13, structural=3
SUMMARY: Read all 6 files in full (1274 lines) plus one-level follow-through into src/stores/resourceStore.ts (toggleGroup/displayResources), src/stores/taskStore.ts (tasks is a createStore proxy) and src/utils/jsonFormatter.ts (pure, no solid-js imports). 31 sites. Mandatory edits are small and mechanical: 6 JSX-namespace import rewrites (solid-js no longer exports JSX; @solidjs/web does, and isolatedModules forces `import type`) and one attribute rename, TaskDataModal.tsx:72 tabIndex -> tabindex (camelCase tabIndex is gone from @solidjs/web jsx.d.ts; only lowercase tabindex plus a `prop:tabIndex` never-marker remain). Accessor/Setter still come from solid-js, so those imports stay. The one substantive migration is ColumnPanel.tsx:133/202/211, where task-store proxy reads happen in <For> callback bodies: rc.6 sets strictRead='<For>' around the map callback (signals dev.js:11997) and the store get trap warns STRICT_READ_UNTRACKED (dev.js:9149), producing O(rows x tasks) warnings per render and confirming an existing silent non-reactivity bug in panel cells; fix is a row-level createMemo plus inlining the cell expression into JSX (creating primitives inside a For callback is legal — mapArray gives each row a plain createOwner, not a children-forbidden scope). Zero top-level component-body reactive reads: every prop/signal read in this batch is inside an arrow function, a memo compute, or a JSX expression. Only two writes exist (ResourceColumn:149 toggleGroup from onClick, TaskDataModal:34/35/37/38 from a post-await continuation and setTimeout), both in owner-free scopes so the REACTIVE_WRITE_IN_OWNED_SCOPE guard does not fire, and both free of same-tick reads — hence two low-severity sync hazards, no high or medium ones. Remaining sites are behavioural-only: 8 memos become non-lazy/eager (all verified safe on a setup-time run; { lazy: true } recommended for the modal/popup formatters that used to be gated behind <Show>) and 4 <For> lists over freshly-allocated memo output that would benefit from keyed={false}. innerHTML, style objects/strings, hyphenated SVG and data-* attributes, camelCase event props and Show/Match element children all verified unchanged. The SMA report has no findings at all for these six files.
  STRUCT: ColumnPanel's cell-value model is the only real redesign in this batch. Row data is snapshotted once per row creation (line 202) and per cell (line 211) by reading a createStore proxy from inside a <For> callback body. Under rc.6 that scope carries strictRead='<For>', so every one of those store-proxy gets emits a STRICT_READ_UNTRACKED console.warn — O(visible rows x tasks) warnings per re-render, enough to make the dev console unusable on a large chart. Fixing it (row-level createMemo + inlining the cell expression into JSX) simultaneously repairs a pre-existing silent bug (panel cells never update when task data changes) but changes the ColumnDef.render contract: render(task, resourceId) becomes a function called inside a tracking scope on every dependency change, so it must be pure and must not create primitives. That contract change is public API (ColumnDef is exported) and should be documented.
  STRUCT: tsconfig.json sets jsxImportSource: 'solid-js'. All six files depend on that being flipped to '@solidjs/web' before their `import type { JSX } from '@solidjs/web'` lines and their intrinsic-element typing line up. That file is outside this batch but it gates every site here; the six import rewrites are not independently landable.
  STRUCT: Memo laziness is a batch-wide behavioural delta: all 8 createMemo calls (Grid 86/147, DateHeaders 57/129, ResourceColumn 68, ColumnPanel 142, TaskDataModal 19, TaskDataPopup 16/23) become eager, one-shot-computed at component creation and non-autodisposing. I read each compute: every one is optional-chained/defaulted, so none crash on a setup-time run with unpopulated props. The two modal/popup pairs are the ones that meaningfully regress (they used to be gated behind <Show>) and are the right candidates for { lazy: true }.
  STRUCT: Four of the five <For> lists in this batch (Grid 196, DateHeaders 212 and 229, ColumnPanel 200) iterate memos that allocate fresh objects on every recompute, so identity keying tears down and recreates every row owner on each viewport change. This is technically unchanged from 1.x, but migration is the natural moment to switch them to keyed={false} — that mode is now the ONLY replacement for <Index> in the codebase (the SMA report flags <Index> in TaskLayer.tsx, TaskLayerMinimal.tsx and DateHeadersOptimized.tsx), so the callback shape (item accessor, plain number index) has to be learned for those files anyway and should be applied consistently here.
  STRUCT: The advisory SMA report contains ZERO findings for these six files (verified: grep of sma-report.txt for src/components/{Grid,DateHeaders,ResourceColumn,ColumnPanel,TaskDataModal,TaskDataPopup}.tsx returns nothing; its only src/components hits are Gantt.tsx, GanttContainer.tsx, TaskLayer.tsx, TaskLayerMinimal.tsx). Its analyzers only look for onMount/createEffect/Index/Context.Provider/createMemo-initial-value shapes, so it missed all six JSX-namespace import changes, the tabIndex -> tabindex breakage, and the three ColumnPanel strict-read sites. Nothing to dispute; the omission is a coverage gap, not a disagreement.
  STRUCT: No onMount, onCleanup, createEffect, createComputed, on(), batch, produce, unwrap, reconcile, path-setter, mergeProps, splitProps, createResource, createSelector, Index, Suspense, ErrorBoundary, Context.Provider, classList, use: directive, on:/oncapture: namespace, attr:/bool: namespace, /*@once*/, ref callback, Dynamic or render() usage exists anywhere in these six files. The batch note's characterisation ('mostly render-only') is accurate; the only reactive write is the delegated toggleGroup click and the setTimeout-driven copy feedback, both of which are in owner-free scopes and therefore pass the rc.6 write guard unchanged.
  SITE[structural/top-level-read] src/components/ColumnPanel.tsx:133 — const tasks = props.taskStore?.tasks; ... for (const id of Object.keys(tasks)) { const t = tasks[id] => Keep the helper, but only ever call it from inside a tracking scope — see the fix at line 202. Body needs no edit.
  SITE[structural/top-level-read] src/components/ColumnPanel.tsx:202 — {(item) => { const task = firstTaskForResource(item.id); return ( <div class="column-panel-row" ...> => {(item) => { const task = createMemo(() => firstTaskForResource(item.id)); return (<div class="column-panel-row" data-resource={item.id} style={rowStyle(item)}> ... </div>); }}

### CRITIC library: missed=4 disputed=4
SUMMARY: I re-ran every FACTS pattern over all 38 in-scope files and compared hit-by-hit against the nine audits. On raw site coverage they are close to complete: every removed/renamed symbol occurrence is claimed (20 JSX-namespace imports, 3 <Index>, 2 .Provider, 2 default-less createContext, 3 onMount, 3 onCleanup, 12 createEffect, 6 produce, 5 path setters, 2 reconcile, 1 batch, 1 createComputed, 4 three-arg createMemo, 5 refs), and all 37 SMA lines for in-scope files are covered. I found no unclaimed occurrence of any removed API. Two systematic sweeps confirmed the audits rather than extending them: a diff of the 1.x vs rc.6 jsx.d.ts attribute sets against every JSX attribute used in scope surfaces only `tabIndex` (3 sites, all already claimed — camelCase HTML aliases are gone, events keep camelCase), and an AST-ish scan for reactive mutators inside memo bodies found only plain local Set/Map writes already covered.

The value is therefore not in more sites but in four rewrites that contradict the runtime, which I verified by reading the published rc.6 dist rather than the FACTS. (1) lib-gantt's Gantt.tsx:234 claim that onSettled is tracked is false — `onSettled` is `createTrackedEffect(() => untrack(callback))` (dev.js:7220), so its prescribed untrack wrapper is dead code, and it directly contradicts lib-scroll-container's correct note on the same API. (2) lib-gantt prescribes `flush()` inside a createEffect apply callback at Gantt.tsx:316/321; `runEffect` sets `inEffectCallback=true` (dev.js:6229) and `flush()` returns early with a FLUSH_IN_EFFECT_CALLBACK warning (dev.js:2286), so the stale-viewMode bug it targets survives the fix — while two sibling audits reached the right answer on the same code path. (3) lib-scroll-container's stated last resort of `flush()` between GanttContainer.tsx:172 and :187 sits inside onSettled, where flush THROWS (dev.js:2280); it converts a silent bug into a crash. (4) lib-bars' #1-ranked "showstopper" rests on "Gantt.tsx never mounts GanttEventsProvider" — it does, at Gantt.tsx:45/540, as the JSX root wrapping TaskLayer→Bar, so no Bar in the library path throws; the fix is still right but must not gate sequencing. The parallel GanttStores claim, by contrast, is a genuine showstopper.

Four genuine misses: the `useGanttEvents` public contract at index.ts:26 (the one index.ts export lib-contexts-index skipped while making exactly this decision for useGanttStores); unproxied `Date` fields inside ganttConfigStore's createStore, which the recommended makeSetter draft rewrite actively hands raw to consumer updaters; the children-forbidden scope that onSettled imposes on the public `onReady` callback; and strictRead propagating through a split effect's apply into consumer callbacks. Cross-cutting, the headline is that FACTS #2 is wrong — `setSignal` has no `_root` exemption where `devGuardStoreSetterWrite` does, so "wrap tests in createRoot" is right for stores and wrong for signals — a deviation only two audits caught and which should be corrected upstream before any batch writes test code.

  MISSED[store-builtin-collection] src/stores/ganttConfigStore.ts:127 — No audit recorded the unproxied-Date invariant for the store that most directly holds Dates. `GanttConfigState` declares `ganttStart: Date` (line 35), `ganttEnd: Date` (36) and `ignoredDates: Date[]`  => Record the invariant on GanttConfigState and enforce it in the makeSetter rewrite. Either normalise on write — `setState(s => { const next = typeof value === 'f
  MISSED[onMount] src/components/Gantt.tsx:234 — The public `onReady` contract silently narrows and no audit registered a fix. `onMount(() => props.onReady?.(api))` becomes `onSettled(...)`, and I verified in rc.6 that `onSettled` builds `createTrac => onSettled(() => { const cb = props.onReady; if (cb) queueMicrotask(() => cb(api)); });  // no untrack needed — onSettled already runs its callback under untrack
  MISSED[createEffect-split] src/components/Gantt.tsx:187 — Diagnostic leakage into consumer code, missed by every audit. When this effect is split as lib-gantt proposes, `props.onSelectionChange` moves into the apply half. I verified that rc.6's `runEffect` s => Do not call consumer callbacks directly from apply. createEffect(() => ({ ids: selectionStore.selectedIds(), cb: props.onSelectionChange }), ({ ids, cb }) => {

It states: "onSettled is a TRACKED effect: the bare `props.onReady` read subscribes to prop identity and would re-fire onReady whenever the parent passes a new function literal (very common). Must untrack the prop read to preserve 'fire
  DISPUTED src/components/Gantt.tsx:316 — lib-gantt prescribes a `flush()` that is a documented no-op, so the bug it is meant to fix survives the migration. Its rewrite for this effect is `createEffect(() => ({...}), ({viewMode, tasks}, prev) => { ... dateStore.changeViewMode(viewMode); flush(); if (tasks?.length) runSetup(tasks); }, { defe
  DISPUTED src/components/GanttContainer.tsx:172 — lib-scroll-container's fallback for syncHazard[0] is not a fallback — it throws. It writes: "or, as a last resort, call `flush()` between the write at 172 and the callback at 187." Both lines sit inside the `onMount` body at line 170, which the same audit migrates to `onSettled`. Verified: `onSettle
  DISPUTED src/components/Bar.tsx:108 — lib-bars' #1-ranked finding rests on a false premise about this repo. It asserts "CRITICAL, definite runtime break … Gantt.tsx never mounts GanttEventsProvider (grep: only the three demo files do)" and, in its structuralConcerns, "under rc.6 every Bar in the main Gantt path throws on setup" and "mus
  CROSS: THE BRIEFED FACTS ARE WRONG ABOUT createRoot, AND ONLY TWO OF NINE AUDITS NOTICED. FACTS #2 lists "createRoot bodies" among the scopes where writes are allowed. I confirmed by reading the published runtime that this holds for STORE setters only: `devGuardStoreSetterWrite()` tests `if (context && !context._root && !(context._config & CONFIG_CHILDREN_FORBIDDEN))` with the comment "Roots are not owned computation scopes — setters inside createRoot bodies are legal" (@solidjs/signals 2.0.0-rc.6 dist/dev.js:4892-4909), whereas `setSignal`'s guard is `!(el._config & CONFIG_OWNED_WRITE) && !(context && context._config & CONFIG_CHILDREN_FORBIDDEN) && context && el._firewall !== context` — no `_root` branch at all (dev.js:4916-4930). A plain signal write inside a createRoot body therefore throws REACTIVE_WRITE_IN_OWNED_SCOPE. lib-stores-task and lib-stores-resource-selection each found this independently and flagged it as a deviation; the other seven inherited the FACTS wording. Consequence: the reflex "wrap the test in createRoot" is correct for taskStore's store writes and wrong for every signal-only store (selectionStore, resourceStore, ganttDateStore, useDrag, useBoxSelect, useGanttModals). Fix the FACTS document once, before any batch writes test code.
  CROSS: flush() LEGALITY IS SCOPE-DEPENDENT AND THE AUDITS GOT IT WRONG IN BOTH DIRECTIONS. There are three distinct behaviours in rc.6 and the nine audits collectively prescribe flush() in all three without distinguishing them: (a) LEGAL — event handlers, timers, promise continuations, plain test bodies (this covers lib-bars' useDrag.ts:163 fix, Bar.tsx:300→302, and every `flush()` in the three test files); (b) SILENT NO-OP + console warning — inside a createEffect/createRenderEffect apply callback, because `runEffect` sets `inEffectCallback = true` (dev.js:6229) and `flush()` returns early on `globalQueue._running` with `[FLUSH_IN_EFFECT_CALLBACK]` (dev.js:2286-2298); (c) THROW — inside `onSettled` or `createTrackedEffect`, because `trackedEffect.run()` sets `inTrackedQueueCallback = true` (dev.js:6269) and `flush()` throws "not reentrant there" (dev.js:2280-2285). lib-gantt lands in (b), lib-scroll-container lands in (c), while lib-stores-config-date and lib-stores-resource-selection independently reached the right answer for the same code. Produce ONE table of the three cases in the migration guide and re-audit every "insert flush() here" recommendation against its caller's scope. The runtime supplies the escape hatch in its own error text for both (b) and (c): `queueMicrotask(() => flush())`.
  CROSS: onSettled HAS TWO SEPARATE CONSTRAINTS AND THE AUDITS EACH REMEMBERED ONLY ONE. The implementation is `createTrackedEffect(() => untrack(callback), { name: "onSettled" })` when an owner exists and is not children-forbidden, else a bare `globalQueue.enqueue` whose returned cleanup throws SETTLED_CLEANUP_UNOWNED (dev.js:7217-7239). So: (1) the BODY IS UNTRACKED — no prop read inside subscribes, and no defensive `untrack()` is needed (lib-scroll-container right, lib-gantt wrong); (2) the OWNER IS CHILDREN-FORBIDDEN — no primitive creation and no `onCleanup` anywhere in the body's call stack, return a cleanup instead. Both onSettled sites in this area hand control to a consumer callback (`props.onReady` at Gantt.tsx:234, `props.onContainerReady` at GanttContainer.tsx:187), so constraint (2) propagates into user code through public props. One shared rule covers all of it: never call a consumer callback synchronously from an onSettled body — defer with queueMicrotask, which restores the 1.x freedom for the consumer and costs nothing.
  CROSS: READ-YOUR-OWN-WRITE IS ONE ARCHITECTURE, NOT ~15 INDEPENDENT SITES, AND IT IS BEING FIXED INCONSISTENTLY. The same shape recurs across six batches: ganttDateStore.setupDates writes signals then ganttSetup.ts:69-84 reads them back; ganttSetup.ts:101 writes resources then reads the resourceIndexMap memo on 102; changeViewMode stages viewMode then generateDates reads the step()/unit() memos derived from it; useDrag runs the final onDragMove (which writes) then onDragEnd (which re-reads) in one stack; Bar.tsx:300 writes then :302 re-reads via resolveResizeConstraints; taskStore.updateBarPosition guards on a committed read before a staged write. Different audits proposed structurally different fixes for the same pattern — return-the-computed-value, thread-geometry-as-arguments, move-the-guard-into-the-draft, and flush() — and per the item above, two of the flush() variants do not work at all. Adopt ONE rule before touching any of them: producers return their computed values, consumers never read back state they just wrote, and store guards move inside the draft callback (the draft, unlike the outer proxy, does reflect staged state). Then the constraint/cascade layer (taskLayerConstraints, constraintEngine, useBarDrag, SummaryBar) can be migrated as a unit instead of site-by-site.
  CROSS: { lazy: true } IS RECOMMENDED BY FOUR AUDITS BUT ONLY ONE STATES ITS SECOND EFFECT. MemoOptions.lazy exists in rc.6 (signals.d.ts:189) — that much is right — but its docstring reads: "When true, defers the initial computation until the value is first read, AND opts the memo into autodisposal — once it has no remaining subscribers it is torn down and recomputed from scratch on the next read. Non-lazy owned memos live for their owner's lifetime and never autodispose." lib-hooks flags the tradeoff; lib-chrome, lib-stores-config-date and lib-virtualization recommend it without it. That matters most exactly where it is being recommended: useBarConfig creates 7 memos per Bar and useTaskVirtualization/useGanttModals sit behind virtualized rows, so `lazy` trades a one-time mount cost for teardown-and-recompute churn every time a row scrolls out and back. Make this one measured decision across the ~25 affected memos rather than 25 local guesses; apply it unconditionally only to the genuinely rarely-read ones (showExpectedProgress, ignoredPositions, the modal/popup formatters behind <Show>).
  CROSS: BUILT-IN COLLECTIONS IN STORES IS THE REPO'S MOST-REPEATED TRAP AND THE CODEBASE ALREADY CONTAINS BOTH THE BUG AND THE FIX. Four audits describe it independently. The broken instance is ganttConfigStore's `expandedTasks: Set<string>` inside createStore, mutated in place through produce (lines 175-201) — unproxied, so expand/collapse notifies nothing and Gantt.tsx:371 plus useTaskVirtualization.ts:161 go dead. The correct pattern is already present three times: taskStore.collapsedTasks, resourceStore.collapsedGroups and selectionStore.selectedIds are all Sets held in signals and replaced immutably. Add to that the Date fields nobody flagged in ganttConfigStore (see missed). Write ONE invariant — built-ins inside a store are replace-only; prefer a signal holding an immutable collection, or Record<string, true> when per-key tracking is wanted — and encode it in types.ts alongside `expandedTasks?: Set<string>` (line 352) and the ProcessedTask Date fields (198, 216), which is where the public option shapes are declared.
  CROSS: <For> INSTALLS A STRICT-READ SCOPE THAT <Index> DID NOT, SO THE Index→For MIGRATION IMPORTS A NEW DIAGNOSTIC. I verified that solid-js dev.js:1174 sets `options.name = "<For>"` and mapArray then wraps the map callback in `setStrictRead(options.name)` / `setStrictRead(false)` (signals dev.js:11994-12005), so a store-proxy read taken directly in a For callback BODY (as opposed to inside JSX or a memo) hits the store get trap's `if (strictRead && !inDraft(target) && typeof key === 'string' && getObserver() === null)` and warns STRICT_READ_UNTRACKED (dev.js:9147-9158). lib-chrome found this and built its central ColumnPanel.tsx:202/211 finding on it; lib-virtualization, which owns the three <Index>→<For keyed={false}> conversions (TaskLayer.tsx:217/249, TaskLayerMinimal.tsx:130), describes the callback shape as "byte-identical" and does not mention that the replacement carries a strict-read label the original lacked. I checked all three bodies plus the other nine <For> sites in scope and every one is an expression-bodied arrow returning JSX, so nothing warns today — but the rule needs writing down before someone adds a `const task = store.tasks[id]` statement to a pooled row callback, which is precisely the shape that broke ColumnPanel.

## AREA demo

### demo-main  effort=S conf=high sites=13 hazards={} topLevelReads=0
files: src/demo/GanttDemo.tsx(180), src/demo/GanttProjectDemo.tsx(137), src/demo/GanttSubtaskDemo.tsx(382), src/demo/GanttResourceGroupsDemo.tsx(239)

SUMMARY: All four demo-main files read in full (180 + 137 + 382 + 239 = 938 lines). They are the cleanest batch in the repo: each is a single stateless component that hardcodes task/resource/options literals into never-written signals and passes them straight to <Gantt>. Zero required code changes for solid-js 2.0 semantics. Verified absent by targeted grep across all four files: onMount, onCleanup, createEffect, createMemo, createComputed, createStore, produce, unwrap, batch, on(), untrack, mergeProps, splitProps, createResource, createSelector, Index, Show, Match, For, Suspense, ErrorBoundary, Dynamic, .Provider, createContext, ref=, use:, on:, attr:, bool:, /*@once*/, classList, and any 'solid-js/web' or 'solid-js/store' import. The only Solid import in the batch is `createSignal` from 'solid-js' (L2 of each file), which is unchanged in rc.6. No signal or store is ever written, so there are no deferred-write sync hazards, no write-in-owned-scope throws, and no test-flush concerns. topLevelReadCount is 0: every `tasks()` / `resources()` / `options()` read sits inside a JSX component-prop expression (compiled to a getter, i.e. tracked), and the only component-body top-level reads (GanttProjectDemo L60-64) are `.filter()` calls over a plain array returned by the pure, import-free src/utils/subtaskGenerator.ts, so they do not trip STRICT_READ. The 13 recorded sites are advisory/mechanical: four @ts-nocheck + jsxImportSource config notes, the unused setTasks binding, constant-in-a-signal cleanups, one explicitly-cleared write-in-component-body candidate (generateSubtaskDemo), and one downstream Set-in-store pointer to ganttConfigStore.ts:146. The SMA report lists nothing for these files. The actual blockers for running these demos live outside the batch: src/entries/{gantt,subtask,resource-groups}.tsx still import render from 'solid-js/web', and GanttSubtaskDemo.tsx has no entry file at all.
  STRUCT: No sync hazards exist in this batch: none of the four files performs a single signal or store write. Every `createSignal` destructuring except GanttDemo.tsx:10 discards the setter, and GanttDemo's `setTasks` is never referenced (verified by grep). All three event handlers per file only `console.log` (or are empty no-ops in GanttResourceGroupsDemo L140-142). Deferred writes therefore cannot change observable behaviour here.
  STRUCT: SMA COVERAGE: the official migration assistant's report (/tmp/.../sma-report.txt) lists ZERO sites for all four of these files (grep for the filenames returns no match; it flags GanttExperiments.tsx, GanttMinimalTest.tsx, GanttPerfDemo.tsx, GanttPerfIsolate.tsx, GanttProfiler.tsx instead). I therefore have no SMA finding to agree with or dispute in this batch, and the sites above are additive to it.
  STRUCT: MOUNT POINTS (adjacent, not in this batch): /home/kaalin/dev/chart/gantt/src/entries/gantt.tsx:1, /home/kaalin/dev/chart/gantt/src/entries/subtask.tsx:1 and /home/kaalin/dev/chart/gantt/src/entries/resource-groups.tsx:1 each do `import { render } from 'solid-js/web'` — must become '@solidjs/web'. These three files are the only things that mount GanttDemo, GanttProjectDemo and GanttResourceGroupsDemo, so this batch is not runnable until they are migrated. GanttSubtaskDemo.tsx has NO entry file at all (grep across src shows it is only self-referenced) — it is dead demo code today; decide whether to keep migrating it or delete it.
  STRUCT: REAL RISK IS ENTIRELY DOWNSTREAM: these demos are thin data-and-props shells over <Gantt> (/home/kaalin/dev/chart/gantt/src/components/Gantt.tsx), which imports onMount, untrack and the JSX type from 'solid-js' (L1-9) and pulls in five stores plus createVirtualViewport, ganttSetup.initializeTasks, useGanttModals and useGanttScroll. The demos will change only if the migrated <Gantt> public prop surface changes (e.g. if `options` must become an accessor or the tasks array must become a store). Re-audit this batch after the Gantt/store batches settle; the diff here should stay near zero.
  STRUCT: GanttProjectDemo is the stress case for the Gantt-side migration: 100 parent tasks with subtasks (parentTaskRatio 1.0) and a 100-entry expandedTasks array that ganttConfigStore.ts:146 converts into a Set held inside a createStore. Under rc.6 that Set is not proxied. The store already replaces rather than mutates it, but every path setter around it (ganttConfigStore.ts:174/188/198/206/210) must be rewritten as a draft callback; this demo is the only regression test that exercises that code path.
  STRUCT: TOOLING: the demos are consumed through Vite HTML entries; swapping vite-plugin-solid for @solidjs/vite-plugin@3.0.0-next.35 plus tsconfig jsxImportSource '@solidjs/web' is the only build-side work these files depend on. No @solid-primitives, no createResource, no Suspense/ErrorBoundary, no Dynamic, no Portal, and no test file targets this batch.

### demo-components  effort=M conf=high sites=37 hazards={'high': 2, 'medium': 4, 'low': 2} topLevelReads=2
files: src/demo/ArrowDemo.tsx(1347), src/demo/BarDemo.tsx(663), src/demo/ConstraintDemo.tsx(1186), src/stores/taskStore.ts(270), src/stores/ganttConfigStore.ts(340), src/utils/constraintEngine.ts(1054)
