risks: mechanical=17, behavioral=11, structural=9
SUMMARY: Read all three files in full (1347 + 663 + 1186 lines) plus, one level down, taskStore.ts, ganttConfigStore.ts (makeSetter) and constraintEngine.ts (pure, no Solid imports). 32 sites, 8 sync hazards, 8 structural concerns.\n\nMECHANICAL CORE (small): 3 import lines (each imports removed symbols from 'solid-js'; the package itself does not move — there is no solid-js/web or solid-js/store import and no JSX type import in any of the three, since all carry // @ts-nocheck), 3 onMount -> onSettled, 1 Index -> For keyed={false}. All four SMA findings for this batch (ArrowDemo:350, BarDemo:173, BarDemo:397, ConstraintDemo:662) are confirmed; I dispute none. Two dead imports the SMA missed: createMemo in ArrowDemo:2 and onCleanup in ConstraintDemo:2. All three onMount bodies are clean for onSettled's children-forbidden scope — no onCleanup, no primitives created directly or through taskStore.updateTasks, no async work — and since onSettled wraps its callback in an unlabeled untrack (signals dev.js:7220) it runs once and emits no STRICT_READ, so onMount -> onSettled is a true rename here.\n\nWHAT THE SMA MISSED, AND WHY THIS BATCH IS M NOT S: every store write in all three demos routes through taskStore.ts, which uses produce (110, 161), path setters (100), setTasks(id, undefined) (134) and reconcile (129, 139) — none of which survive rc.6. No demo can be migrated before that store lands. And ConstraintDemo:777-779 is a hard break, not a warning: setSelectedScenario(v) followed synchronously by updateTasks(), which reads allTasks() — a memo of selectedScenario. Under deferred writes the memo has not recomputed, so the store is seeded with the OLD scenario while <For each={allTasks()}> renders the NEW one; every new row's pos() misses and the scenario renders as invisible bars. ConstraintDemo's resolveMovement (90-275) is the structural one: a recursive solver that interleaves store reads with four updateBarPosition writes (178, 203, 239, 264) across loop iterations and recursion depths — line 239 writes a predecessor and line 251 reads that same predecessor's _bar back to clamp newX. It needs a pure-planner rewrite over a local working copy, not a local patch.\n\nEverything else is quieter. ArrowDemo is nearly clean: one onMount, twelve setConfig({...config(), k}) read-modify-writes that are safe per-event but should become functional setters, no read-after-write in either drag handler. BarDemo's onConstrainPosition creates no primitives and is called from unowned event scopes, so it is safe today — but it resolves constraints against the live store, which breaks the moment it is called twice in a tick. taskStore's `if (!tasks[id]) return` guard (line 108) reads committed state outside the draft and will silently drop position writes that share a tick with a reconcile.\n\nNo classList, use:, on:/oncapture:, attr:/bool:, /*@once*/, ref callbacks, Context.Provider, createResource, batch, on(), createComputed, mergeProps/splitProps, unwrap, Dynamic, render, @solid-primitives usage or tests anywhere in this batch. Two createMemo calls, both 1-arg, so the (fn, initialValue, options) reshape does not bite. topLevelReadCount is 2 (BarDemo:462 and :478) and both are inside a For child body, not a component body — the actual component bodies of all three files contain zero top-level reactive reads, and ConstraintDemo:902 / ArrowDemo:1034 already use the correct thunk pattern.
  STRUCT: ConstraintDemo's resolveMovement (lines 90-275) is the one function in this batch that genuinely needs a redesign rather than a local rewrite. It is a recursive constraint solver that interleaves taskStore reads and taskStore.updateBarPosition writes across loop iterations and recursion depths, and its correctness depends entirely on 1.x's synchronous write propagation. Rewrite it as a pure planner over a local Map<taskId,{x,y}> working copy (reads fall through to taskStore.getBarPosition on first touch, writes only touch the map) that returns the full update set; handleMouseMove then applies it in one write. This also removes N store notifications per pointer move.
  STRUCT: ConstraintDemo maintains store state through two disconnected imperative paths — an onMount seed (662) and a manually-called updateTasks() (667, invoked from onChange at 779 and onClick at 812) — while allTasks() is already a memo of selectedScenario. The whole arrangement should collapse into one split createEffect(() => allTasks(), taskStore.updateTasks). That single change deletes the highest-severity hazard in the batch instead of patching it with flush().
  STRUCT: All three demos are blocked on the taskStore.ts migration: createStore's setter is used exclusively through removed APIs (produce at 110 and 161, path setters at 100, setTasks(id, undefined) at 134, reconcile at 129/139). No demo file can be migrated independently — taskStore must land first, and its updateBarPosition guard (line 108, committed-state read outside the draft) has to move inside the draft as part of that work.
  STRUCT: ganttConfigStore.makeSetter (151-166) is a Setter<T> emulation over a store path that both computes `prev` from committed state and returns committed state after the write. Under deferred writes both halves are wrong. BarDemo only rides one of its twenty products (setShowExpectedProgress) with a plain value, so this batch survives a naive port — but the shim's public type (Setter<GanttConfigState[K]>) is a lie in 2.0 and should be narrowed to (v: T | ((prev: T) => T)) => void so no caller can depend on the return value.
  STRUCT: The Index -> For keyed={false} swap at BarDemo:397 is mechanical at the call site, but the comment on line 395 ('use Index instead of For to prevent component recreation on position updates') encodes a real pooling assumption: Bar instances must survive position writes. keyed={false} preserves exactly that model, but it is worth a runtime check that Bar's internal memos and useBarDrag state are not torn down when allTasks() returns a fresh array after a reconcile.
  STRUCT: BarDemo's debug overlay (460-529) reads the store non-reactively inside a For child body. 2.0 emits no warning (mapArray untracks without a strict-read label) so this will not surface during migration testing — it just quietly renders stale or empty coordinates. Worth fixing to thunks while the file is open, matching ConstraintDemo:902 and ArrowDemo:1034.
  STRUCT: All three files carry `// @ts-nocheck` on line 1. TypeScript will not flag the removed Index/onMount imports, the new For keyed overloads, or the StoreSetter signature change in this batch, so a green tsc proves nothing here. Remove the directive during the migration and fix the fallout, or these files will be the last place the 2.0 breakages surface.
  STRUCT: The demos import '../stores/taskStore.js' and '../stores/ganttConfigStore.js' while the files on disk are .ts. That resolution is provided by Vite today; confirm @solidjs/vite-plugin@3.0.0-next.35 (native compiler by default) preserves the .js -> .ts extension rewrite before assuming these files even load.
  HAZ[high] src/demo/ConstraintDemo.tsx:778 — onChange writes selectedScenario then, in the same synchronous handler, calls updateTasks() which reads allTasks() — a memo whose only dependency is selectedScenario() (line 628). Under deferred writes the signal write is staged, the memo has not recomputed, and allTasks() returns the PREVIOUS scenario's task list. taskStore.updateTasks then reconciles the store to the OLD tasks while <For each={allTasks()}> at line 900 (evaluated after the flush) renders the NEW tasks. Every new row's pos() lookup misses, so rect x/y/width are undefined and the newly selected scenario renders as invisible bars; the stale scenario's ids linger in the store. In 1.x the memo recomputed synchronously on read and this worked. || FIX: Delete the imperative pairing. Replace the onMount at 662 and the updateTasks helper at 667 with a split effect: createEffect(() => allTasks(), (tasks) => taskStore.updateTasks(tasks)) — compute reads the memo (tracked, write-free), apply does the store write (untracked, writes allowed). Then onChange becomes just setSelectedScenario(e.target.value), and the 'Reset Positions' button at 812 needs a separate explicit re-seed (e.g. taskStore.updateTasks(buildTasks(untrack(selectedScenario))) in the handler). If the imperative shape must be kept, insert flush() between the write and the read.
  HAZ[high] src/demo/ConstraintDemo.tsx:251 — In the successor branch of resolveMovement, line 239 writes the predecessor's position via taskStore.updateBarPosition(otherTaskId, ...), and then line 251 (the maxDistance clamp) reads predTask._bar.x and predTask._bar.width off the same store proxy to compute newX. Under deferred writes that read returns the pre-write x, so the successor is clamped against where the predecessor USED to be. Line 248's guard also uses newDistance computed at line 220 from pre-write state. The same shape exists at 178 -> 190 (predecessor branch: push the successor, then read otherTask._bar.x for the maxDistance clamp) and at 203/264. || FIX: Make resolveMovement pure. Build a local Map<taskId, {x,y}> seeded lazily from taskStore.getBarPosition, have every read go through a getPos(id) helper that checks the map first, have every 'write' set the map entry, and return the accumulated updates. handleMouseMove then applies the whole plan in one taskStore.batchMovePositions / one setTasks(draft => ...) call. This is also strictly faster (one store notification per pointer move instead of N).

Chains of three or more constrained tasks therefore converge one pointer-move behind instead of within a single move, and a task participating in two relationships can be positioned inconsistently. || FIX: Same fix as the previous hazard: a single working-copy map threaded through the recursion so reads see this move's accumulated decisions. It becomes wrong the moment onConstrainPosition is invoked twice in one tick — which is exactly what a multi-select / batch drag would do, and what Bar.tsx:306 already does on the keyboard path if a caller ever stops returning null. The second call would resolve constraints against positions the first call staged but did not commit. || FIX: Pass the resolver an explicit position provider rather than binding it to the live store (context.getBarPosition = (id) => pendingPositions.get(id) ?? taskStore.getBarPosition(id)), and record every write into pendingPositions. If a seeding write (updateTasks/reconcile) and a position write ever land in the same tick — e.g. ConstraintDemo's onChange re-seeds and a queued pointer-move fires before the flush — the guard sees the pre-reconcile map, the id looks absent, and the position update is dropped with no error. || FIX: Move the existence check inside the draft callback, where staged state is visible: setTasks(state => { const task = state[id]; if (!task?._bar) return; Object.assign(task._bar, position); }).
