### demo-showcase  effort=L conf=high sites=54 hazards={'high': 10, 'medium': 1} topLevelReads=0
files: src/demo/ShowcaseDemo.tsx(2030)
cats: store-path-setter=38, import-store=8, onMount=2, batch=2, config=1, onCleanup-in-lifecycle=1, other=1, store-unwrap=1
risks: mechanical=39, behavioral=15
SUMMARY: Read all 2030 lines plus the delegated helpers (taskStore.ts createTaskStore: getTask/getBarPosition/updateTask/updateBarPosition/updateTasks; ganttConfigStore.ts createGanttConfigStore; constraintEngine.ts resolveConstraints - pure) and the call sites in Bar.tsx / useBarDrag.ts that invoke this file's callbacks. 55 sites: 5 import-level changes (onMount, onCleanup, batch specifiers, solid-js/store -> solid-js, and the @ts-nocheck that will hide all of it), 7 createStore declarations whose setters change shape, 1 onMount -> onSettled, 1 batch removal, 31 path setters setX('key', v) and 7 object-merge setters setX(obj) that must all become draft callbacks, 1 store-proxy spread (6 lines), and 1 dead createGanttConfigStore call. No Index/Show-callback/Context.Provider/classList/ref/use:/Dynamic/render/createEffect/createComputed/on()/produce/unwrap/mergeProps/splitProps/createResource in this file, and zero top-level reactive reads (all reads are inside memos, JSX or event handlers). The real work is 11 sync hazards: 7 input handlers that write a config store then immediately call updateTaskA/updateTaskB which re-read that same store (stale by one edit; the two 'locked' ones invert drag behaviour), applyPreset reading taskStore.getTask after staging four updateBarPosition writes and then clobbering them with whole-node replacements (all position-changing presets silently stop working), and two constraint callbacks (handleResizeEnd, handleConstrainPosition) that re-read positions their callers wrote earlier in the same tick. SMA's three sites (11:29 store import, 660:5 onMount, 776:9 batch) are all confirmed and included; it found none of the 38 setter rewrites and none of the hazards. Note one dispute with the supplied runtime facts: rc.6 signals.d.ts states onSettled reads are NOT tracked and create no subscription, so the config reads inside initializeTasks will not turn the seed into a re-running effect.
  STRUCT: The core pattern of this demo - 'write config store, then synchronously read that same config store to mirror it into taskStore' (updateTaskA at 665-678 and updateTaskB at 681-693, invoked from 7 input handlers) - is exactly what deferred writes break, and it cannot be fixed by a local rename. Replace the two imperative mirrors with two split createEffects: compute reads taskConfig/taskBConfig fields and returns a PLAIN object (never the store proxy - reading a proxy in apply warns STRICT_READ_UNTRACKED), apply calls taskStore.updateTask. That also deletes 7 hazard sites at once and makes the mirror correct for preset changes too (applyPreset currently duplicates the same mirroring logic inline at 828-875 precisely because the effect-less design forces it).
  STRUCT: taskStore.updateTask(id, taskData) (taskStore.ts:99-101 -> setTasks(id, taskData)) is a WHOLE-NODE REPLACEMENT API. Every one of this file's 6 call sites builds the replacement from `{...taskA}` - a shallow spread of the live store proxy. Under deferred writes any write to that task staged earlier in the same tick (a bar position, a drag delta) is silently discarded by the replacement. The API should become a patch-merge performed inside the draft (setTasks(d => { Object.assign(d[id], patch) })) before this file is migrated; otherwise hazards #8/#9 will keep reappearing in new call sites.
  STRUCT: Seeding via lifecycle is avoidable. initializeTasks (613-658) exists only to fill the task store once; converting onMount -> onSettled preserves it but keeps a write-from-a-lifecycle-hook and a one-settle window where getBarPosition returns null (the arrow fallbacks at 960-987 paper over it with duplicated hardcoded coordinates). Prefer passing the initial task array to createTaskStore() so the store is born populated; then onSettled/onMount disappear, the duplicated fallback literals at 960-987 can go, and there is no lifecycle write at all. Note that calling initializeTasks() directly in the component body is NOT an option - a store write during component setup throws REACTIVE_WRITE_IN_OWNED_SCOPE in 2.0 dev.
  STRUCT: The onConstrainPosition / onResizeEnd callback contract (Bar -> useBarDrag -> this file) assumes synchronous propagation: the callee re-reads the store to discover what the caller just wrote. Under deferred writes the contract must carry the geometry in its arguments. This is a cross-file API change (Bar.tsx:295-313, useBarDrag.ts:143-190/260-283, GanttEvents.tsx, TaskLayer.tsx, Gantt.tsx all implement the same signature), so it needs to be decided once for the whole repo rather than patched per demo.
  STRUCT: createGanttConfigStore is instantiated at line 546 and never used. It should be deleted here, but note that keeping it anywhere in the repo requires migrating two 2.0-breaking constructs inside ganttConfigStore.ts: the makeSetter shim (returns Setter<T>, computes prev from committed state and returns state[key] after the write - both semantics are unrepresentable under deferred writes) and setState('expandedTasks', produce(set => ...)) (produce is removed, and Set is not proxied in 2.0 so in-place mutation notifies nothing; the property must be reassigned to a new Set).
  STRUCT: Passing accessors (popupVisible, popupPosition, hoveredTask, modalTask, relationships) as props to TaskDataPopup/TaskDataModal still works, but hoveredTask/modalTask (595-606) return the live store PROXY from taskStore.getTask. If those children read the proxy inside a createEffect apply callback they will emit STRICT_READ_UNTRACKED in 2.0 dev; the memos should return snapshot(task) (or the children must do their reads in compute).
  HAZ[high] src/demo/ShowcaseDemo.tsx:1085 — HAZARD #1 (name): `setTaskConfig('name', e.target.value)` at 1084 is immediately followed by `updateTaskA()` at 1085, and updateTaskA (665-678) reads `taskConfig.name` at line 670 to build the object it writes into taskStore. Under deferred writes the store read at 670 still returns the PREVIOUS committed name, so every keystroke writes the stale name into taskStore and <Bar task={{name: taskConfig.name}}> (1898) and the task store permanently disagree by one edit; on the first keystroke the bar keeps the old name forever until another keystroke arrives. || FIX: Stop round-tripping through the store: give updateTaskA a patch parameter - `const updateTaskA = (patch = {}) => { const prev = snapshot(taskStore.getTask('task-a')); taskStore.updateTask('task-a', { ...prev, name: taskConfig.name, ..., ...patch }); }` and call `updateTaskA({ name: e.target.value })`. Cleanest: delete updateTaskA entirely and replace it with a split createEffect - compute reads taskConfig.name/progress/color/colorProgress/locked/invalid into a plain object, apply writes taskStore.updateTask (apply may write and is untracked). A one-line stopgap is `flush()` between the setter and updateTaskA(), which is legal inside an event handler.
  HAZ[high] src/demo/ShowcaseDemo.tsx:1098 — HAZARD #2 (color): `setTaskConfig('color', ...)` at 1097 then `updateTaskA()` at 1098, which reads `taskConfig.color` at 672. Stale by one edit - the color swatch (JSX, reactive) updates but the stored task keeps the previous color. || FIX: Same as hazard #1: pass the new value explicitly (`updateTaskA({ color: e.target.value })`) or derive taskStore's task-a from taskConfig with a split createEffect.
  HAZ[high] src/demo/ShowcaseDemo.tsx:1119 — HAZARD #3 (colorProgress): `setTaskConfig('colorProgress', ...)` at 1115-1118 then `updateTaskA()` at 1119, which reads `taskConfig.colorProgress` at 673. Stale by one edit. || FIX: Same as hazard #1: `updateTaskA({ colorProgress: e.target.value })`.
  HAZ[high] src/demo/ShowcaseDemo.tsx:1142 — HAZARD #4 (progress): `setTaskConfig('progress', parseInt(...))` at 1138-1141 then `updateTaskA()` at 1142, which reads `taskConfig.progress` at 671. On a range input this fires continuously, so the stored progress trails the slider by exactly one input event - and when dragging stops, the last value is never committed to taskStore. || FIX: Same as hazard #1: `updateTaskA({ progress: parseInt(e.target.value) })`, or derive via split createEffect so the trailing value is always applied.
  HAZ[high] src/demo/ShowcaseDemo.tsx:1192 — HAZARD #5 (locked): `setTaskConfig('locked', e.target.checked)` at 1188-1191 then `updateTaskA()` at 1192, which reads `taskConfig.locked` at 674 into `constraints: { locked }`. The checkbox flips but taskStore keeps the OPPOSITE lock state, so drag/constraint behaviour (resolveConstraints reads task.constraints.locked via context.getTask) is inverted relative to the UI until the next toggle. || FIX: Same as hazard #1: `updateTaskA({ constraints: { locked: e.target.checked } })`. This one is a behaviour inversion, not just a display lag - fix it before shipping.
  HAZ[high] src/demo/ShowcaseDemo.tsx:1217 — HAZARD #6 (invalid): `setTaskConfig('invalid', e.target.checked)` at 1213-1216 then `updateTaskA()` at 1217, which reads `taskConfig.invalid` at 675. Stale by one toggle. || FIX: Same as hazard #1: `updateTaskA({ invalid: e.target.checked })`.
  HAZ[high] src/demo/ShowcaseDemo.tsx:1776 — HAZARD #7 (Task B locked): `setTaskBConfig('locked', e.target.checked)` at 1775 then `updateTaskB()` at 1776, which reads `taskBConfig.locked` at 690. Same inverted-lock-state failure as hazard #5, for task-b. || FIX: Give updateTaskB a patch parameter and call `updateTaskB({ constraints: { locked: e.target.checked } })`, or derive task-b from taskBConfig with a split createEffect.
  HAZ[high] src/demo/ShowcaseDemo.tsx:829 — HAZARD #8 (applyPreset clobbers the preset positions): lines 807-826 call taskStore.updateBarPosition for task-a..task-d (each stages a draft write that rewrites state[id]._bar). Line 829 then reads `taskStore.getTask('task-a')`, which under deferred writes returns the task with the OLD _bar, and line 831 writes it back with `setTasks('task-a', {...taskA, ...})` - a whole-node replacement that overwrites the staged _bar. Result: clicking any preset button updates colors/names but the bars stay at their previous x positions, i.e. every preset that moves bars (constrained, fixedOffset, startToStart, finishToFinish) silently stops working. || FIX: Do the field update and the position update in ONE draft write, e.g. `setTasks(d => { const t = d['task-a']; Object.assign(t, {name: preset.taskConfig.name, progress: ..., color: ..., colorProgress: ..., invalid: ...}); t.constraints.locked = preset.taskConfig.locked; Object.assign(t._bar, {...positions['task-a'], y: 70, height: 30}); })` - add a taskStore method that takes (id, patch, barPatch). Failing that, reorder so all updateTask calls precede all updateBarPosition calls, or call flush() at line 828 (legal inside this click handler).
  HAZ[high] src/demo/ShowcaseDemo.tsx:843 — HAZARD #9 (same clobber for tasks B/C/D): `taskStore.getTask('task-b')` at 843 (and 'task-c' at 854, 'task-d' at 865) read committed state after the staged updateBarPosition writes at 812/817/822, then lines 845/856/867 write the stale `{...taskX}` back as a whole-node replacement, discarding the new _bar for all three tasks. || FIX: Same fix as hazard #8, applied to task-b/task-c/task-d: fold the position reset and the field reset into a single draft write per task (or one draft write for all four).
  HAZ[high] src/demo/ShowcaseDemo.tsx:910 — HAZARD #10 (resize-end constraint cascade): handleResizeEnd reads `taskStore.getBarPosition(taskId)` at 910 and feeds taskBar.x/taskBar.width into resolveConstraints at 919-924. Its caller Bar.tsx:300 writes the new width (`props.taskStore?.updateBarPosition(taskId, { width: newWidth })`) and then calls onResizeEnd on line 302 in the SAME synchronous handler (keyboard shift+Arrow resize). Under deferred writes handleResizeEnd sees the pre-resize width, so the cascade positions the successors for the OLD duration and the chain visibly lags one keystroke behind. || FIX: Change the onResizeEnd contract to pass the new geometry: `onResizeEnd(taskId, { x, width })` (Bar.tsx already has newWidth in scope) and have handleResizeEnd use the passed values instead of re-reading the store. If the contract must stay, call flush() at the top of handleResizeEnd - it runs from an event handler so flush() is legal there.
  HAZ[medium] src/demo/ShowcaseDemo.tsx:880 — HAZARD #11 (constraint cascade reads committed positions): handleConstrainPosition reads `taskStore.getBarPosition(taskId)` at 880 and resolveConstraints re-reads every predecessor/successor position through `context.getBarPosition` (885) while the cascade loop at 899-902 stages writes for those same successors. Within one pointermove this is still correct (useBarDrag.ts:155-164 calls onConstrainPosition BEFORE its own updateBarPosition), but any caller that writes a position and then calls onConstrainPosition in the same tick - or two constrain calls in one tick, e.g. auto-repeat on Bar.tsx:307 - resolves against stale geometry and produces a cascade that undershoots by one step. || FIX: Pass the authoritative geometry into the callback instead of re-reading the store (onConstrainPosition(taskId, newX, newY, {width}) - Bar/useBarDrag already know the width), and build the constraint context from a snapshot() taken once per gesture rather than from live store getters. Do not add flush() inside the drag path; it would force a full settle per pointermove.

### demo-perf-isolate  effort=M conf=high sites=35 hazards={'high': 1, 'medium': 1, 'low': 2} topLevelReads=6
files: src/demo/GanttPerfIsolate.tsx(2093)
cats: context-default=9, other=5, onCleanup-in-lifecycle=3, batch=3, top-level-read=3, onMount=2, ref-owned=2, context-provider=2, index-for=1, import-store=1, store-builtin-collection=1, store-produce=1, write-in-component-body=1, import-jsx-type=1
risks: behavioral=19, mechanical=14, structural=2
SUMMARY: GanttPerfIsolate.tsx (2093 lines, 17 bar variants + one 640-line main component) needs 4 import fixes (onMount, Index, batch removed from the 'solid-js' block at L2-11; 'solid-js/store' -> 'solid-js' with produce dropped at L12), one produce->draft-setter rewrite (L1516, the file's only store write path), two batch removals (L1580, L1644), and one onMount->onSettled conversion (L1758) whose body must return a merged cleanup for both the resize listener (L1769) and the currently-uncancelled rAF loop (L1806). All five SMA findings for this file are confirmed; I add ~25 sites it missed. The two highest-value findings are not import-level: (1) a deferred-write break at L1645->L1648, where handleConstrainResize writes the dragged bar and then calls calculateCascadeUpdates, which reads the bar's width back through the live store reader mockTaskStore.getBarPosition — every cascaded successor gets positioned with the previous frame's width and the last frame's error persists after mouseup; (2) createContext<GanttEventHandlers>() with no default (src/contexts/GanttEvents.tsx:23) makes useGanttEvents() throw ContextNotFoundError for the 6 bar variants that are not auto-wrapped by the L1468-1470 gate — including ?bar=dragconst, the variant this file's own header text advertises. The batch note's hover-zone suspicion is cleared: setHoverZone (L1220-1222 / L1367-1369) is write-only in the mousemove handler and is only read from JSX via getCursor(), so there is no same-stack read-after-write there. Writes are otherwise all owner-free (DOM handlers / rAF) or inside onSettled, so no REACTIVE_WRITE_IN_OWNED_SCOPE throws; no createEffect, no on(), no createComputed, no Index/Suspense/classList/use:/attr: usage; all 20 createMemo calls are single-argument. Six STRICT_READ top-level prop/store reads sit in three already-broken legacy variants.
  STRUCT: Context redesign, not a local rewrite: 8 useGanttEvents() call sites (L434, 500, 576, 672, 754, 836, 918, 1282) rely on useContext returning undefined outside a provider, and 6 of them are NOT covered by the auto-wrap rule at L1468-1470 (experiments/nochildren/contained/willchange/combined/dragconst). In rc.6 useContext throws ContextNotFoundError (verified in solid-js 2.0.0-rc.6 dist/dev.js: useContext -> getContext -> throw when the resolved value is undefined). Fixing it properly means changing createContext<GanttEventHandlers>() to a null default in src/contexts/GanttEvents.tsx, which touches every other consumer of that context; the cheap demo-local alternative is to always render <GanttEventsProvider> at L2086.
  STRUCT: useDrag coupling: 12 of the 17 bar variants instantiate useDrag in their component bodies. Its top-level onCleanup (src/hooks/useDrag.ts:250) stays legal (component-owned scope), but its state machine assumes synchronous signal propagation for its re-entrancy guards (see syncHazards). This demo cannot be migrated or perf-tested independently of the useDrag migration.
  STRUCT: Eager memos invalidate the harness's purpose: this file exists to isolate per-primitive rendering cost, and in 2.0 memos are non-lazy by default. The 10 never-read dummy memos (L1714-1753) plus every per-bar `t` memo now compute at creation and live for the owner's lifetime, so all recorded 1.x baselines (?bar=, ?memos=, ?virt=) must be re-measured rather than compared. Adding { lazy: true } restores 1.x shape but not 1.x scheduling.
  STRUCT: Dead/broken variants should be deleted or normalized before migrating: BarMinimal, BarWithText, BarWithHandles, BarReactive, BarWithDrag, BarWithEvents and BarFull treat props.task as a plain object, but the <For> at L2038 passes an accessor (`task={() => tasks[task.id]}`). They are already broken in 1.x (`task._bar` is undefined) and in 2.0 they additionally emit STRICT_READ warnings. V8-V17 use the `typeof props.task === 'function' ? props.task() : props.task` shape; converging on that (or on a plain store row) removes all 6 top-level reads at once.
  STRUCT: Tooling/verification gap: the file is `// @ts-nocheck` (L1), so none of the type-level breakage (JSX namespace move, StoreSetter signature, removed exports) will surface at build time — this batch must be validated by actually loading the page for each ?bar= variant. Also needs the repo-level moves: vite-plugin-solid -> @solidjs/vite-plugin 3.0.0-next.35 and tsconfig jsxImportSource -> '@solidjs/web'. Note that under @solidjs/vite-plugin's vitest posture, test.environment 'node' resolves the SERVER build (no subscription graph), so any smoke test of this component must stay on jsdom.
  STRUCT: Drag pipeline timing: all position writes now land on the microtask flush rather than inside the rAF callback. Functionally this is an improvement (one flush per frame instead of per-setter work), but it means the DOM transform for a dragged bar is applied after the rAF callback returns rather than during it; if the perf traces in this repo assert on within-frame ordering (rAF -> style write), they will need re-baselining.
  HAZ[high] src/demo/GanttPerfIsolate.tsx:1648 — handleConstrainResize writes the dragged task at L1645 (updateBarPosition -> setTasks draft, setting startHours AND durationHours) and then, in the same synchronous call, reads the store back through calculateCascadeUpdates(taskId, finalX, context) at L1648. That helper (src/utils/constraintEngine.ts:554) calls context.getBarPosition = mockTaskStore.getBarPosition (L1537), which reads tasks[id].startHours/durationHours. In 1.x the store write was already applied, so predecessor bars carried the NEW width; in rc.6 the write is staged until the microtask flush, so the read returns the previous width. The helper seeds updates.set(taskId,{x:newX}) so the x is fine, but FS/SF successor minima are computed as predBar.x + predBar.width (getMinSuccessorX), so every cascaded successor is placed with the stale width. Because useDrag drives this once per animation frame, the error from the FINAL frame is never corrected and persists after mouseup. || FIX: Move the cascade computation above the write and feed the new geometry explicitly: const cascadeCtx = { ...context, getBarPosition: (id) => id === taskId ? { ...mockTaskStore.getBarPosition(id), x: finalX, width: finalWidth } : mockTaskStore.getBarPosition(id) }; const cascadeUpdates = calculateCascadeUpdates(taskId, finalX, cascadeCtx); then updateBarPosition(taskId, { x: finalX, width: finalWidth }); then the cascade loop; then triggerArrowUpdate(). Do NOT reach for flush() here — it would force a full settle inside a 60fps rAF loop.
  HAZ[medium] src/hooks/useDrag.ts:196 — startDrag's re-entrancy guard reads dragState() at L196 and the same function stages the write at L218-219 (setDragState/setIsDragging); handleMouseUp mirrors this with the guard at L155 and the writes at L183-184. Under deferred writes, a second startDrag dispatched inside the SAME synchronous event still observes 'idle', so it passes the guard, overwrites dragData and installs a second pair of document mousemove/mouseup listeners. Every bar variant in this batch routes mousedown through startDrag (L382, L403, L417, L448, L524, L545, L559, L617, L641, L655, L716-720, L1231-1235, L1389-1393); today only e.stopPropagation() in the child handles prevents a double dispatch, so the guard is load-bearing and now unreliable. || FIX: Back the guard with a plain closure boolean that flips synchronously (let active = false; if (active) return; active = true; ... set active = false in handleMouseUp) and keep dragState/isDragging purely for rendering; alternatively call flush() immediately after setDragState in startDrag. Cross-file: fix must land with the useDrag batch, but this demo is one of its main consumers.
  SITE[structural/onMount] src/demo/GanttPerfIsolate.tsx:1758 —     onMount(() => { => onSettled(() => { const updateSize = () => {...}; updateSize(); window.addEventListener('resize', updateSize); let rafId = null; if (autoTest && scrollerRef) { const tick = () => { ...; rafId = requestAnimationFrame(tick
  SITE[structural/context-provider] src/demo/GanttPerfIsolate.tsx:2086 —     return useContext ? (
        <GanttEventsProvider>{content}</GanttEventsProvider> => Keep <GanttEventsProvider> (it is a component), but fix its body in src/contexts/GanttEvents.tsx; simplest demo-level fix is to mount it unconditionally so no variant can hit a missing context.

### demo-experiments  effort=M conf=high sites=33 hazards={'medium': 2, 'low': 3} topLevelReads=1
files: src/demo/GanttExperiments.tsx(1217), src/demo/DateHeadersOptimized.tsx(240), src/contexts/GanttEvents.tsx(69), src/hooks/useDrag.ts(292)
cats: other=6, index-for=5, onMount=2, onCleanup-in-lifecycle=2, config=2, createMemo-args=2, write-in-compute=2, context-provider=2, context-default=2, import-web=1, import-store=1, store-path-setter=1, top-level-read=1, ref-owned=1, dynamic=1, show-match-callback=1, import-jsx-type=1
risks: mechanical=21, behavioral=12
SUMMARY: Read both files in full (1217 + 240 lines) plus the two helpers they delegate to (hooks/useDrag.ts, contexts/GanttEvents.tsx). 36 sites. Mechanical core: 4 import changes (Dynamic -> @solidjs/web, createStore -> solid-js, onMount -> onSettled, Index -> For), 3 <Index> -> <For keyed={false}> conversions whose callback shapes are already correct, and one onMount/onCleanup pair -> onSettled with a returned cleanup (the body creates no Solid primitives directly or through callees, and both of its writers - a setTimeout and a ResizeObserver callback - are unowned, so it is a legal onSettled body; the two timers should also be cleared in the cleanup). Highest-value finding, which the advisory report mis-classified: the two 'createMemo initial value' hits at L706 and L722 are not initial values at all - they are already options-shaped, which means under solid-js 1.9 the { equals: idsEqual } object was silently swallowed as `prev` and the splitEquals experiment ran with NO custom equality; porting to rc.6 activates that equality with a zero-line diff and changes what the harness measures. The demo never writes its store (createStore setter is discarded), so there are no store-path/produce/reconcile/unwrap sites and no store-side deferred-write exposure; the sync hazards are all signal-latch shaped - runStressTest using running() as its own start/stop guard (medium) and useDrag calling onDragStart after writing dragState/isDragging (medium, latent here because the demo passes no-op callbacks). Structurally, the real cost is not the rewrite but the harness: rc.6 memos are eager by default, two experiment variants keep incremental caches inside memo computes, and every timing this file records moves from inline-in-the-scroll-handler to the microtask flush.
  STRUCT: Memo eagerness invalidates this harness's premise. rc.6 memos are non-lazy by default (MemoOptions.lazy, signals.d.ts:180-189, is opt-in), so all nine mode-guarded memos (L598, 621, 635, 659, 706, 722, 745, 752, 776, 797, 824, 852) now compute at component setup instead of only when the selected mode reads them. Each early-returns after reading virtMode(), so the standing subscription set stays small, but every virtMode change re-runs all of them and the 1.x 'unselected memos never execute' assumption that this file was written to exploit is gone. Adding { lazy: true } restores 1.x laziness but also opts each memo into autodisposal — which is incompatible with the cached-closure variants below.
  STRUCT: The smartCache (L652-698) and cachedVisibleTasks (L596-599) experiments store incremental state in closure variables mutated from inside memo computes. That was already against the rules in 1.x and is explicitly contrary to the 2.0 compute contract; it does not throw (the writes are plain JS, not reactive), but it cannot be combined with { lazy: true } autodisposal and its correctness now depends on undocumented recompute ordering. These two variants need to be redesigned as a two-stage derived chain (which is what the xySplit variant at L621/L635 already is) or moved into a split createEffect apply before the numbers they produce mean anything.
  STRUCT: The 'splitEquals' experiment (L702-749) never actually had custom equality under solid-js 1.9 — the 2-arg createMemo passed { equals: idsEqual } as the initial value. The port turns that argument into real options, so this variant changes behavior with a zero-line diff. Every recorded comparison between 'splitEquals' and the other modes must be re-measured after migration, and the team should decide whether the intended experiment was 'custom equality' or 'ID-array staging without equality' (the latter now needs the option removed).
  STRUCT: Deferred writes move the entire scroll -> signal -> memo -> DOM pipeline out of the scroll/RAF callback and into the microtask flush. For a file whose only purpose is DevTools frame profiling, this is a harness-design change, not a code change: either insert flush() at the end of the RAF tick to keep work inside the measured frame, or restructure the harness to measure to the flush boundary and re-baseline every stored result.
  STRUCT: Index pooling assumption in DateHeadersOptimized: <For keyed={false}> preserves Index's slot pooling exactly, but only the lower header (numeric slots, L26-34) benefits. upperSlots (L38-95) allocates fresh objects per recompute, so every pooled upper slot's accessor changes identity on every scroll tick and re-runs its bindings — the component's documented 'zero DOM churn' guarantee is half true today and stays half true after the port. Consider memoising the slot objects by (startIndex,text) or expressing the pool with the new <Repeat count>.
  STRUCT: Optional-provider context pattern: useGanttEvents (GanttEvents.tsx:53-66) returns no-op handlers when the context is missing. createContext<T>() with no default throws in 2.0, so that whole fallback is unreachable and the three bar variants here (L225/321/409) become provider-mandatory. Give the context the no-op handler object as its real default before migrating any consumer.
  STRUCT: Out-of-batch blockers for actually running this demo: src/entries/experiments.tsx:1 imports render from 'solid-js/web' (must become '@solidjs/web'); package.json:85 still pins vite-plugin-solid ^2.11.12 and :101 solid-js ^1.9.12; tsconfig.json:8 sets jsxImportSource 'solid-js' and must become '@solidjs/web'. Until those move, nothing in this batch can be validated at runtime.
  STRUCT: Both files are // @ts-nocheck, so the type-level half of this migration (JSX namespace relocation, For keyed overloads, DynamicProps, StoreReturn) produces zero diagnostics. Recommend removing @ts-nocheck for the duration of the port and re-adding it only if the pre-existing untyped props are not worth fixing.
  HAZ[medium] src/demo/GanttExperiments.tsx:898 — runStressTest uses the reactive signal `running()` as its own start/stop latch: it reads running() at L898 and writes setRunning(true) at L905 / setRunning(false) at L901 and L929. Under deferred writes running() returns the last committed value, so a second entry before the microtask flush (the autoTest timer at L982 racing a user click on V-Scroll/H-Scroll/Both at L1089/1102/1115, a double click, or the Stop button at L1070 followed immediately by a start) still sees the pre-write value: two RAF tick loops can be started against a single `stressAbort` controller (L893/L907-908), and the first loop then becomes unstoppable because stressAbort only points at the second controller. || FIX: Make the latch non-reactive: `let isRunning = false;` (or test `stressAbort !== null`) for the guard at L898 and for the early-return, and keep setRunning()/setTestMode() purely as UI mirrors. Alternatively call flush() immediately after setRunning(true) — but a plain flag is cheaper and removes the race entirely.
  HAZ[medium] src/hooks/useDrag.ts:218 — Followed one level from GanttExperiments.tsx:254/344/444. startDrag writes setDragState(state) (L218) and setIsDragging(true) (L219) and then, still in the same synchronous mousedown stack, calls onDragStart?.(dragData, state) at L221. Any consumer callback that reads dragState() or isDragging() to learn the new state gets the stale 'idle'/false under deferred writes. The demo's callbacks are no-ops (L255-257 etc.) so this batch is unaffected, but the same hook backs the real bar drag code. || FIX: Consumers must use the `state` argument that is already passed to onDragStart rather than reading the hook's signals; if a consumer genuinely needs the committed value, call flush() before invoking onDragStart, or mirror the state into a plain `let currentState` that the hook reads internally.

### demo-perf-profiler  effort=M conf=high sites=37 hazards={'high': 1, 'medium': 1, 'low': 2} topLevelReads=1
files: src/demo/GanttMinimalTest.tsx(705), src/demo/GanttPerfDemo.tsx(683), src/demo/GanttProfiler.tsx(406), benchmarks/profiler/index.js(245), benchmarks/profiler/instrumentation/memoTracker.js(358)
cats: other=7, primitives-in-callback=4, onMount=4, onCleanup-in-lifecycle=4, import-web=3, solid-primitives-dep=2, docs=2, createMemo-args=2, import-store=1, store-builtin-collection=1, top-level-read=1, index-for=1, write-in-component-body=1, ref-owned=1, context-provider=1, context-default=1, import-jsx-type=1
risks: mechanical=22, behavioral=10, structural=5
SUMMARY: 35 sites: 31 in the five listed files plus 4 in delegated helpers (3 in src/contexts/GanttEvents.tsx reached from GanttMinimalTest, 1 hazard in src/hooks/useDrag.ts). All 11 sma-report entries for this batch are reproduced and agreed with; the SMA missed the removed-symbol import lines (GanttMinimalTest:2 onMount+Index, GanttPerfDemo:2, GanttProfiler:2), the eager-memo behaviour change that invalidates this batch's entire purpose, the Date instances held in createStore, the top-level Object.keys(store) STRICT_READ, the dead ref on <Gantt>, the ESM/require defects in benchmarks/profiler/index.js, the createMemo prev-argument forwarding in memoTracker, the context provider/default sites the demos depend on, and - most importantly - that GanttPerfDemo:334's onSettled body reads a signal through @solid-primitives/raf's start(), which makes the body re-run and loadTasks() fire twice. Mechanical work is small: 5 import lines, 4 onMount -> onSettled with 4 onCleanup -> returned disposers, 1 Index -> For keyed={false}, 1 three-arg createMemo. The real cost is behavioural - deferred writes push RAF-driven DOM patches into a trailing microtask and eager memos change what the profiler measures, so every number these demos produce needs re-baselining - and @solid-primitives/raf must go to 4.0.0-next.2 or nothing in GanttPerfDemo resolves.
  STRUCT: createMemo is EAGER (non-lazy) by default in rc.6. This batch exists to measure Solid's reactive cost, so the change invalidates its baselines: GanttMinimalTest creates one t() memo per visible bar (151) plus five window memos (277/280/292/299/306), and benchmarks/profiler/instrumentation/memoTracker.js is built around counting pull-driven memo invocations. Decide per memo whether to pass { lazy: true } for 1.x parity or to re-baseline; either way analyzeMemos' CHEAP/EXPENSIVE/CASCADE thresholds (memoTracker.js:218-221) and its per-frame cascade grouping (176-205) now describe a different scheduler.
  STRUCT: Frame attribution changes across all three demos: signal writes made from RAF callbacks (GanttMinimalTest measureFrame 442-468, GanttPerfDemo createRAF callback 64-105, GanttProfiler countFrames 86-91) are staged, and the resulting DOM patches run on the microtask flush after the callback returns. Work that used to appear inside the RAF task in a trace now appears in a trailing microtask - the FPS / worst-frame / avg-frame figures these demos print, and the Chrome traces the bench scripts capture, must be re-baselined rather than compared to 1.x runs.
  STRUCT: onMount -> onSettled is not a pure rename here: onSettled is a tracked, children-forbidden effect. Each of the four bodies in this batch (GanttMinimalTest:417, GanttPerfDemo:269 and :334, GanttProfiler:165) must be audited for (a) synchronous reactive reads, which now create subscriptions that re-run the whole body - GanttPerfDemo:334 already trips this through createRAF's start(); (b) onCleanup calls, which throw and must become a single returned disposer; (c) primitive creation, directly or through any helper/callback invoked from the body.
  STRUCT: benchmarks/profiler/index.js mixes ESM exports with CommonJS require() in createBenchmarkRunner (71-82) and window.__ganttPerf (241). Those paths throw ReferenceError today in the browser and will keep throwing after the migration; anyone verifying the perf demos post-migration via window.__ganttPerf or createBenchmarkRunner will misread it as a Solid 2 regression. Fix it in the same pass using the static imports the module already has.
  STRUCT: The three demos cannot boot until src/entries/minimal-test.tsx, src/entries/perf.tsx and src/entries/profiler.tsx switch `import { render } from 'solid-js/web'` to '@solidjs/web' (one line each), tsconfig sets jsxImportSource '@solidjs/web', and config/vite/{solid,demo}.js swaps vite-plugin-solid ^2.11.12 for @solidjs/vite-plugin 3.0.0-next.35 (babel-preset-solid ^1.9.12 can then be dropped from devDependencies). Those files are outside this batch but gate any runtime verification of it.
  STRUCT: GanttProfiler's instrumentStore (41-73) monkey-patches window.__ganttTaskStore.getBarPosition/getTask/updateBarPosition. Under deferred store writes, updateBarPosition returns before the store commits, so the wall-clock the profiler attributes to that method no longer includes the propagation it triggers - that cost moves into the scheduler flush and is invisible to the callTree instrumentation. If the tool's purpose is finding hot reactive paths it needs an extra hook around the flush, or it will systematically under-report writes and over-report the first read after a flush.
  STRUCT: GanttMinimalTest stores Date instances inside createStore (initialTasks spread at 121 from parsedTasks 94-98). rc.6 does not proxy Date/Map/Set, so any future in-place mutation of task._start/_end notifies nothing. Normalise to epoch numbers (or keep the dates outside the store) while the file is being touched.
  HAZ[high] src/demo/GanttPerfDemo.tsx:336 — startFpsCounter() is @solid-primitives/raf's start(), whose body is `if (running()) return; setRunning(true); requestAnimationFrame(loop)`. It is invoked from inside onMount -> onSettled, which in rc.6 is a TRACKED effect. Two failures compound: (1) the running() read subscribes the onSettled bundle to createRAF's internal signal and the setRunning(true) in the same body dirties it, so after the microtask flush the whole onSettled body re-runs and loadTasks() executes a second time (setTasks re-applied, renderTime and domStats measured twice, benchmark numbers polluted); (2) the read-then-write guard itself is evaluated against COMMITTED state, so if start() is ever reached twice before a flush it starts two RAF loops. || FIX: onSettled(() => { untrack(() => { loadTasks(); startFpsCounter(); }); }); - untrack removes the subscription while writes stay legal in an onSettled body. Belt and braces: guard with a plain `let fpsStarted = false` closure latch so re-entry does not depend on committed signal state.
  HAZ[medium] src/hooks/useDrag.ts:196 — startDrag guards re-entry with `if (dragState() !== 'idle') return;` (196) and only then writes setDragState(state) (218) / setIsDragging(true) (219). Under deferred writes the setter stages the value, so a second startDrag reached in the SAME synchronous dispatch still reads the committed 'idle', passes the guard, overwrites dragData (209-216) and registers a second pair of document mousemove/mouseup listeners. GanttMinimalTest reaches exactly this shape: TestBar's resize handles (206-213) and the bar's own onMouseDown (203) both call startDrag from one delegated mousedown dispatch, relying on e.stopPropagation() plus this guard. || FIX: Back the guard with non-reactive state: `let active = false; if (active) return; active = true; ...`, clear it in handleMouseUp alongside setDragState('idle'), and keep dragState()/isDragging() purely for rendering. Same treatment for the `dragState() !== 'idle'` checks at 130 and 155.
  SITE[structural/onMount] src/demo/GanttPerfDemo.tsx:334 — onMount(() => { loadTasks(); startFpsCounter(); }); => onSettled(() => { untrack(() => { loadTasks(); startFpsCounter(); }); });
  SITE[structural/other] benchmarks/profiler/index.js:71 — const { createFrameMetrics } = require('./metrics/frameMetrics.js'); => delete the three require() blocks (71-82) and use the module's own static ESM imports/re-exports
  SITE[structural/other] benchmarks/profiler/index.js:77 — const { startMemoTracking, stopMemoTracking, analyzeMemos, clearMemoTracking } = require('./instrume => replace with a top-level `import { ... } from './instrumentation/memoTracker.js'`
  SITE[structural/other] benchmarks/profiler/index.js:82 — const { createBenchmarkResult, downloadJSON, generateReport } = require('./export/jsonExporter.js'); => replace with a top-level `import { ... } from './export/jsonExporter.js'`
  SITE[structural/other] benchmarks/profiler/index.js:241 — createFrameMetrics: () => require('./metrics/frameMetrics.js').createFrameMetrics(), => createFrameMetrics: () => createFrameMetrics(), using the static import

### demo-db  effort=M conf=high sites=23 hazards={'high': 2, 'medium': 3, 'low': 2} topLevelReads=1
files: src/demo/DbDemo.tsx(602), src/demo/db/TaskForm.tsx(770), src/demo/db/Modal.tsx(64), src/demo/db/ResourceManager.tsx(192), src/demo/db/BlockedManager.tsx(222), src/demo/db/api.ts(111)
cats: other=10, custom-setter-shim=4, onMount=2, on-helper=2, docs=1, primitives-in-callback=1, createEffect-split=1, top-level-read=1, import-jsx-type=1
risks: behavioral=13, mechanical=10
SUMMARY: 24 sites across 6 files. Mechanical work is small: 3 import lines (DbDemo onMount->onSettled+flush, TaskForm drop `on`, Modal `import type { JSX } from '@solidjs/web'`), one onMount->onSettled, one createEffect(on(...)) split, one untrack around a top-level props read, and four functional-updater rewrites in TaskForm. ResourceManager.tsx, BlockedManager.tsx and api.ts need no code changes at all. The risk is concentrated in DbDemo's drag pipeline: onDateChange is invoked synchronously from useDrag.handleMouseUp AFTER that handler drained the final pending move (which stages _bar writes), so every `taskStore.tasks[id]._bar` read at line 196 is one drag-frame stale — the PATCH loses the last frame and dependents that only moved on that frame are skipped entirely; worse, the post-await re-snapshot at line 239-242 reads the committed value, so lastX/lastWidth desync from dbState and the error compounds per drag. Fix with flush() at the top of onDateChange plus capturing bar.x/width into the patches array. Same class of problem at refetch's queueMicrotask(snapshotChartX) (line 100), which assumed a single microtask was enough for the Gantt's effect-driven re-layout. Two non-obvious traps: (1) the naive `onSettled(() => refetch('Loaded'))` THROWS in dev because the arrow returns a Promise and trackedEffect rejects non-function returns (@solidjs/signals dev.js:6283) — wrap in a block; (2) the onReady callback at line 364 fires from Gantt's onMount->onSettled (children-forbidden), so it must never create primitives — it currently only schedules a timer, which is legal. SMA found 3 of these 24 sites (DbDemo:111, TaskForm:231, TaskForm:232 — all confirmed) and missed Modal.tsx's JSX import, every import line, all four setter shims, the top-level props read, and all seven sync hazards. topLevelReadCount=1 (TaskForm.tsx:200).
  STRUCT: DbDemo's entire drag->PATCH strategy is built on synchronous store commits: it scrapes window.__ganttTaskStore.tasks[id]._bar (a live store proxy exposed at src/components/Gantt.tsx:238) at three different points in the drag/refetch lifecycle and diffs pixel positions across them. Under 2.0 every one of those reads sits on a different side of a flush boundary. Sprinkling flush() makes it work, but the durable fix is an API change in the Gantt: onDragEnd already holds the exact final {x, width} it wrote (useBarDrag.ts:270-273) and Gantt.handleDateChange already converts them (Gantt.tsx:467-476) — pass the positions (or precise dates) through onDateChange so the demo never has to re-read the store.
  STRUCT: DbDemo's timing cannot be validated until the Gantt batch is fixed: Gantt.tsx runs runSetup (which performs taskStore/resourceStore/dateStore writes) inside onMount (line 287) and inside a single-argument createEffect body under untrack (lines 288-303, 305-329). Both throw REACTIVE_WRITE_IN_OWNED_SCOPE in 2.0 dev (untrack does not clear the owner), and the fix chosen there determines when _bar values commit relative to DbDemo's queueMicrotask/setTimeout snapshots.
  STRUCT: taskStore itself (src/stores/taskStore.ts) uses the removed store APIs that DbDemo depends on for _bar semantics: produce (lines 111-120, 160-172), path setter setTasks(id, taskData) (line 101), setTasks(id, undefined) as a delete (line 135), reconcile at the root (lines 128, 139), plus a Set held in a signal for collapsedTasks. Those rewrites can change the granularity/ordering of _bar notifications that DbDemo's delta diffing observes.
  STRUCT: TaskForm mirrors props.initial into ten local signals and re-syncs them from a deferred effect. With staged writes the mirror is always one flush behind props, and DbDemo re-creates the props.initial object on every dragVersion bump (spread at DbDemo.tsx:124), so the reset fires on every drag. Consider deriving the form values (createMemo over props) or remounting TaskForm keyed by selectedId instead of the effect-driven reset.
  STRUCT: All five .tsx files in this batch begin with // @ts-nocheck, so none of the removed-export or JSX-namespace breakage (onMount, on, JSX from solid-js) will be caught by tsc during the migration. Remove the pragmas at least for the duration of the port.
  STRUCT: Modal renders its overlay inline rather than through a Portal; nothing to migrate today, but if a Portal is introduced during the port it must be imported from '@solidjs/web', not 'solid-js/web'.
  HAZ[high] src/demo/DbDemo.tsx:196 — onDateChange runs synchronously inside the mouseup call stack (useDrag.ts:159-177 drains the pending move, writing every dragged/batch-moved bar's _bar via taskStore.updateBarPosition/batchMovePositions, then calls onDragEnd -> useBarDrag.ts:263-275 -> Gantt.handleDateChange:467-476 -> this handler). It then READS taskStore.tasks[id]._bar.x/.width for every task to compute dxPx/dwPx against lastX/lastWidth. Under deferred writes the final drag frame's writes are still staged, so every bar reports its previous position: the PATCHed start/end silently omit the last frame of movement, and any dependent that only moved on that final frame fails the `Math.abs(dxPx) < 0.5` test at line 210 and gets no PATCH at all. If the user drags and releases within a single RAF frame the whole gesture can be discarded ('produced no PATCH (no Δ)'). || FIX: Call flush() (exported from solid-js) as the first statement of onDateChange — the handler is unowned, so flush is allowed — before the `for (const id of Object.keys(taskStore.tasks))` loop. Structurally better: change the Gantt/useBarDrag contract so onDragEnd captures the final {x, width} it just wrote and passes them down, instead of every consumer re-reading the store.
  HAZ[high] src/demo/DbDemo.tsx:240 — After `await Promise.all(patches.map(api.patchTask))` the microtask flush has run, so `taskStore.tasks[p.id]._bar` here returns the COMMITTED final position, while the delta that produced the persisted start/end was computed at lines 196-212 from the pre-flush (stale) position. lastX/lastWidth are therefore re-based to a pixel position that does not correspond to dbState's dates, so the next drag's delta is off by exactly the frame lost in the previous drag — the error compounds across drags. || FIX: Record the exact values used for the delta: push `{ id, start, end, x: bar.x, width: bar.width }` in the loop at line 219 and, after the PATCH, do `lastX.set(p.id, p.x); lastWidth.set(p.id, p.width);` instead of re-reading the store. (Combine with the flush() fix so both reads agree.)
  HAZ[medium] src/demo/DbDemo.tsx:100 — refetch stages setBundle(data) at line 93 and then relies on `queueMicrotask(snapshotChartX)` to observe the re-processed bar positions. In 2.0 the bundle write commits in the scheduled flush, during which Gantt's createEffect (src/components/Gantt.tsx:288-303) calls runSetup -> initializeTasks -> taskStore writes that produce the new _bar values. A single queued microtask is no longer a guarantee that those effect-phase store writes have committed, so lastX/lastWidth can be snapshotted from the OLD layout; the first drag after a refetch then computes its delta against stale baselines and PATCHes wrong dates. || FIX: queueMicrotask(() => { flush(); snapshotChartX(); }) — or move the snapshot behind a setTimeout(…, 0) like the onReady path, with flush() inside. Long term, have the Gantt expose a post-settle 'layout ready' callback carrying the positions instead of scraping window.__ganttTaskStore.
  HAZ[medium] src/demo/db/TaskForm.tsx:254 — updateConstraint computes the next ConstraintRow from a committed read of constraints() and writes the whole object back. With staged writes, two calls in the same synchronous turn (a paste/auto-fill that sets several constraint fields, a future 'clear constraints' helper, or any programmatic multi-field patch) both read the same pre-flush value and the earlier write is silently lost. || FIX: setConstraints((prev) => ({ ...prev, [key]: value })) — functional updaters compose against the staged value.
  HAZ[medium] src/demo/db/TaskForm.tsx:266 — updateDep (and addDep at 257, removeDep at 263) rebuild the deps array from a committed deps() read. The rendered rows only reflect a staged write after the flush, so any second mutation issued in the same turn — e.g. a handler that changes maxMode and resets maxValue, or two onInput events dispatched in one task during tests — is computed against the pre-write array and clobbers the first. || FIX: setDeps((prev) => prev.map((d, i) => (i === idx ? { ...d, ...patch } : d))); apply the same functional-updater form to addDep and removeDep.

### demo-features  effort=S conf=high sites=13 hazards={'high': 2, 'medium': 2, 'low': 4} topLevelReads=0
files: src/demo/BoxSelectDemo.tsx(149), src/demo/CriticalPathDemo.tsx(143), src/demo/ExportDemo.tsx(203), src/demo/FilterSearchDemo.tsx(201), src/demo/MultiSelectDemo.tsx(131)
cats: config=5, createEffect-split=2, other=2, jsx-attr=2, primitives-in-callback=1, docs=1
risks: mechanical=7, behavioral=4, structural=2
SUMMARY: Five demo files, 827 lines, mostly static task fixtures. No import changes are needed anywhere in this batch (each file imports only createSignal from 'solid-js'); no onMount/onCleanup/createEffect/createMemo/batch/on/produce/unwrap/Index/Show-callback/context/classList/ref/use:/attr: sites exist locally, and topLevelReadCount is 0 (every accessor read sits in JSX or an event handler). The real breakage is inherited through the Gantt props surface: (1) BoxSelectDemo:141 and MultiSelectDemo:123 pass setSelectedIds into onSelectionChange, which Gantt.tsx:184-188 invokes from an effect COMPUTE under untrack — a hard REACTIVE_WRITE_IN_OWNED_SCOPE throw in rc.6 dev (verified against dist/dev.js setSignal), fixable only by splitting that effect so the callback runs in apply; (2) ExportDemo:197's onReady write is legal because Gantt.tsx:234's onMount becomes onSettled, a children-forbidden scope that permits writes but forbids primitive creation and onCleanup — a new, undocumented contract for the GanttAPI bridge; (3) CriticalPathDemo:135 embeds a bare accessor call in the options object literal, making the whole prop a rebuilt getter that amplifies Gantt's illegal in-compute store writes (ganttConfig.updateOptions, runSetup) — rewrite to a per-key getter like FilterSearchDemo:189-194; (4) CriticalPathDemo:112 is a committed-value read-modify-write toggle that must become setCriticalPath(v => !v); (5) FilterSearchDemo:105 should become a createMemo so props.options.filter stops minting a new predicate identity per read. Eight sync hazards, two high (the selection callbacks). The SMA report lists zero sites for all five of these files, so every finding here is additional. Total 13 sites, effort S, mechanical except for the two structural Gantt-side dependencies.
  STRUCT: Both selection demos (BoxSelectDemo:141, MultiSelectDemo:123) are blocked on Gantt.tsx:184-188: a single-arg createEffect that calls props.onSelectionChange inside untrack. Any consumer callback that writes a signal throws REACTIVE_WRITE_IN_OWNED_SCOPE in rc.6 dev (confirmed in @solidjs/signals dist/dev.js setSignal — the guard exempts only CONFIG_OWNED_WRITE signals and CONFIG_CHILDREN_FORBIDDEN contexts, and untrack leaves `context` set). This is a public-API contract change, not a local rewrite: every callback prop Gantt invokes from an effect must move to the apply phase, and the prop docs (Gantt.tsx:124-125) should state that callbacks run untracked/unowned after the flush.
  STRUCT: ExportDemo's imperative bridge depends on Gantt.tsx:234 onMount(() => props.onReady?.(api)) becoming onSettled. onSettled is children-forbidden: any consumer that creates a primitive in its onReady callback (or in a helper the callback invokes) throws, and onCleanup inside throws CLEANUP_IN_FORBIDDEN_SCOPE. Either document 'onReady: writes allowed, primitive creation forbidden' on GanttAPI, or fire onReady from an effect apply / queueMicrotask so consumer callbacks run in a fully unowned scope with no restrictions.
  STRUCT: CriticalPathDemo's inline `options={{ ..., criticalPath: criticalPath() }}` (line 135) makes the entire options prop a rebuilt-per-read getter, which drives Gantt.tsx:306-309 (ganttConfig.updateOptions inside an effect compute — a store write that throws in 2.0) and Gantt.tsx:288-302 (runSetup -> setRelationships/setLegacyResources inside an effect compute). Per-key getters in the demo shrink the invalidation surface, but the toggle only works after those Gantt effects are split into compute/apply. FilterSearchDemo already uses the correct getter shape and therefore only trips the same Gantt effects via search/filter changes.
  STRUCT: Out-of-batch but required to run these five demos: src/entries/{box-select,multi-select,export,filter-search,critical-path}.tsx each do `import { render } from 'solid-js/web'` -> '@solidjs/web'; tsconfig jsxImportSource must become '@solidjs/web'; vite-plugin-solid must be swapped for @solidjs/vite-plugin@3.0.0-next.35.
  STRUCT: All five files open with `// @ts-nocheck`, so none of the 2.0 type-level breakages (JSX namespace moving to '@solidjs/web', Setter/StoreSetter signatures, removed solid-js exports) are visible to tsc. The migration should strip ts-nocheck per file as each is converted, otherwise these demos will only fail at runtime in the browser.
  STRUCT: None of the five files import anything that moved: each imports only `createSignal` from 'solid-js' (plus a type-only import of GanttAPI in ExportDemo). There are no onMount/onCleanup/createEffect/createMemo/createComputed/batch/on/produce/unwrap/Index/Suspense/ErrorBoundary/classList/ref/use:/context sites in this batch — every breakage here is inherited through the Gantt props surface (callback ownership + deferred writes) rather than through local API renames.
  HAZ[high] src/demo/BoxSelectDemo.tsx:141 — WRITE: setSelectedIds(...) executed from Gantt.tsx:184-188's effect COMPUTE (wrapped in untrack, which does not clear the owner). READ: selectedIds() in JSX at 131-132. In rc.6 dev the write throws REACTIVE_WRITE_IN_OWNED_SCOPE before any read happens; in prod the write is staged and the badge updates only after the microtask flush, so any same-tick readback of selectedIds() is stale. || FIX: Split the Gantt effect: createEffect(() => Array.from(selectionStore.selectedIds()), (ids) => props.onSelectionChange?.(new Set(ids))). Compute must return a plain array (not the Set behind a store proxy) so apply never triggers STRICT_READ_UNTRACKED; drop untrack. Demo file needs no change.
  HAZ[high] src/demo/MultiSelectDemo.tsx:123 — Identical to BoxSelectDemo:141 — parent setter invoked from Gantt's selection effect compute; write throws in dev, and the `Selected (n)` readback at 113-114 lags one flush behind the click once fixed. || FIX: Same split-effect fix in Gantt.tsx:184-188; no change in the demo.
  HAZ[medium] src/demo/CriticalPathDemo.tsx:112 — WRITE then READ of the same signal in one expression: setCriticalPath(!criticalPath()) reads the committed value to compute the next one. Under deferred writes, a second toggle dispatched in the same synchronous tick (test harness, programmatic .click(), a synthetic double event) reads the pre-flush value and cancels the first write. || FIX: setCriticalPath((v) => !v) — dev.js setSignal applies updater functions to the staged `_pendingValue`, so chained toggles compose correctly. In tests, call flush() after the click before asserting the button label/props.options.criticalPath.
  HAZ[medium] src/demo/ExportDemo.tsx:101 — WRITE: setApi(a) at line 197 fires from Gantt's onSettled (ex-onMount) and is STAGED. READ: api() at 101/109/119 inside the click handlers. A click (or programmatic click in a test) that lands in the same tick as mount, before the flush, sees null and the handler silently `return`s with no user feedback. || FIX: In tests, flush() after render before clicking. In the demo, either disable the buttons off `api()` (`disabled={!api()}`) so the dead window is visible, or set a status message in the early-return branch instead of a bare `return`.
  SITE[structural/createEffect-split] src/demo/BoxSelectDemo.tsx:141 — onSelectionChange={(set) => setSelectedIds(Array.from(set).sort())} => Demo text can stay, but it only works once Gantt.tsx:184-188 becomes a split effect: createEffect(() => [...selectionStore.selectedIds()], (ids) => props.onSelectionChange?.(new Set(ids))) — callback then runs in the unt
  SITE[structural/createEffect-split] src/demo/MultiSelectDemo.tsx:123 — onSelectionChange={(set) => setSelectedIds(Array.from(set).sort())} => Same as BoxSelectDemo: requires Gantt.tsx:184-188 split into compute (returns a plain array snapshot) + apply (invokes props.onSelectionChange). No textual change needed in this file.

### entry-index-test  effort=M conf=high sites=41 hazards={'medium': 2, 'low': 2} topLevelReads=7
files: src/entries/index-test.tsx(2360), src/hooks/useDrag.ts(292)
cats: other=8, top-level-read=7, index-for=5, config=4, solid-primitives-dep=2, primitives-in-callback=2, onCleanup-in-lifecycle=2, import-web=1, onMount=1, createEffect-split=1, import-store=1, context-default=1, show-match-callback=1, jsx-attr=1, ref-owned=1, dynamic=1, render=1, context-provider=1
risks: mechanical=27, behavioral=14
SUMMARY: index-test.tsx is a 2360-line standalone perf harness (20 bar variants + one demo shell) whose required 2.0 changes are almost entirely mechanical and concentrated in the last 70 lines: 3 import lines (solid-js/web -> @solidjs/web; solid-js/store -> solid-js; drop the removed-but-unused onMount and the removed Index), 4 <Index> -> <For keyed={false}> sites at L2298/2307/2316/2325 (callback shape is already (accessor, number) so no callback edits), and <TestEventsContext.Provider> -> <TestEventsContext> at L2343 plus its closing tag at L2357 (which the advisory report omits). There are no store writes, no path setters, no produce/unwrap/reconcile, no batch, no on(), no createComputed, no createResource, no classList/use:/on:/attr:, no ref callbacks, and no 2-or-3-arg createMemo anywhere in the file - I checked all 90 createMemo calls with a paren-matching scan and every one is single-argument, so the batch note about '3-arg createMemo calls' does not hold for this file. Every setter call lives in an event handler, setInterval or requestAnimationFrame callback, so nothing trips REACTIVE_WRITE_IN_OWNED_SCOPE; the onCleanup at L1995 sits directly in a component body and stays legal (disputing the advisory report's 'migrate to returned cleanup' item, since there is no enclosing effect/onSettled). The real work is behavioral: @solid-primitives/raf 2.3.4 statically imports 'solid-js/web' and blocks the build until 4.0.0-next.2, deferred writes move the DOM update out of the frame the harness is timing (so all Index-recycling baselines must be re-measured), seven component-body top-level reactive reads will emit STRICT_READ 340x/frame in dev, and three toggle-guard/read-after-write patterns (startTest L1947, the setOffset updater L1965, useDrag's startDrag guard L196) read a signal they stage in the same task. Plus config prerequisites: tsconfig jsxImportSource, vite-plugin-solid -> @solidjs/vite-plugin in both vite configs, and the package.json dependency set.
  STRUCT: The harness's premise changes under 2.0. Its whole purpose is timing <Index> DOM-node recycling; <For keyed={false}> is a different reconciler (patch-driver / driveList in @solidjs/web) AND writes are deferred to a microtask flush, so the DOM update caused by setOffset in the interval callback (L1964) no longer happens inside that callback's task. createRAF's frame-to-frame measurement (L1879) therefore attributes the render to a different frame than in 1.x. All recorded baselines must be re-measured, and if synchronous-update semantics are wanted for comparability the interval body should be wrapped in flush(() => setOffset(...)).
  STRUCT: The 'force memo evaluation' probes at L848, L903 and L974 (variants V7d/V7e/V7f) are meaningless in 2.0: createMemo computes eagerly on creation, so nothing is being forced, and each probe additionally emits a dev STRICT_READ warning 340x per frame (once per bar instance), which will itself dominate the profile in dev builds. These three variants need redesign or deletion rather than a mechanical rewrite.
  STRUCT: All 20 variant components normalize their row prop with `typeof props.taskId === 'function' ? props.taskId() : props.taskId` (and `props.task` for the directTask family) purely because <Index> passed an accessor. <For keyed={false}> passes exactly the same accessor, so the shim keeps working, but the migration is the right moment to type these props as Accessor<T> and drop the runtime typeof branch from 20 call sites.
  STRUCT: Dev-mode diagnostics volume: with 340 bars rendered per frame, every per-component STRICT_READ (L1283/1284 in TestBarWithContext, L1505 in TestBarCombined, plus the three void probes) fires per instance per re-create. Fix these before doing any perf comparison against 1.9 numbers, otherwise the 2.0 results are diagnostics-bound.
  STRUCT: @solid-primitives/raf is a hard blocker, not a version nicety: the installed 2.3.4 build statically imports 'solid-js/web', which does not exist in Solid 2, so this entry will not even bundle until the 4.0.0-next line is installed. The FPS/worst-frame/avg-frame instrumentation of the whole harness depends on it.
  STRUCT: @ts-nocheck at L1 means TypeScript will report nothing for this 2360-line file: removed APIs (Index, onMount), the moved store import, and the Context.Provider removal will all surface only at runtime. Recommend removing @ts-nocheck for the duration of the migration and re-adding it (if desired) once the entry runs.
  HAZ[medium] src/hooks/useDrag.ts:196 — Re-entrancy guard `if (dragState() !== 'idle') return;` reads a signal that startDrag itself writes at L218 (`setDragState(state)`). Under deferred writes the write is staged, so a second startDrag call in the SAME task (two mousedown handlers on nested elements where stopPropagation is missing, or a synthetic double-dispatch) reads the committed 'idle' and starts a second drag: duplicate document mousemove/mouseup listeners are registered (L224-225) and only one set is removed on mouseup, leaking a listener and double-firing onDragMove. index-test drives this via TestBarWithDrag L1451 and TestBarCombined L1550/1564/1569/1574. || FIX: Track drag activity with a plain closure boolean that is not subject to deferred commit: `let active = false;` set/cleared alongside setDragState, and use `if (active) return;` as the guard; keep the signal purely for rendering.
  HAZ[medium] src/entries/index-test.tsx:1947 — startTest reads `running()` as a toggle guard (`if (running()) { stopTest(); return; }`) and then writes `setRunning(true)` at L1952. The write is staged until the microtask flush, so any second invocation of startTest inside the same task (double click dispatch, programmatic call, or a stopTest()/startTest() pair in one handler) still sees running()===false and starts a second setInterval (L1963) and a second RAF loop, orphaning the previous intervalId/scrollAnimId — the onCleanup at L1995 only clears the latest one. || FIX: Keep a non-reactive `let isRunning = false;` as the source of truth for the guard and mirror it into the signal for rendering, or wrap the toggle in flush(() => { ... }) so the read after the write sees the new value.

### entries  effort=S conf=high sites=26 hazards={} topLevelReads=0
files: src/entries/arrow.tsx(10), src/entries/bar.tsx(7), src/entries/box-select.tsx(7), src/entries/constraint.tsx(10), src/entries/critical-path.tsx(7), src/entries/custom-columns.tsx(7), src/entries/db.tsx(7), src/entries/experiments.tsx(7), src/entries/export.tsx(7), src/entries/filter-search.tsx(7), src/entries/gantt.tsx(7), src/entries/minimal-test.tsx(7), src/entries/multi-select.tsx(7), src/entries/perf-isolate.tsx(7), src/entries/perf.tsx(7), src/entries/profiler.tsx(7), src/entries/resource-groups.tsx(7), src/entries/showcase.tsx(7), src/entries/subtask.tsx(7)
cats: import-web=19, config=6, render=1
risks: mechanical=22, behavioral=3, structural=1
SUMMARY: All 19 entry files are pure bootstraps with exactly one migration site each: line 1, 'solid-js/web' -> '@solidjs/web'. render()'s rc.6 signature is byte-compatible (code, MountableElement, init?, options?) => dispose, so none of the 19 render() calls change. Zero stores, effects, lifecycles, context, control flow, refs, primitives-in-callbacks, sync hazards or top-level reactive reads exist in the batch. The SMA found exactly these 19 import lines and nothing else. Beyond them I found 6 config sites the SMA missed that this batch cannot ship without: config/vite/solid.js:19 external list and :21 UMD globals map still keyed on 'solid-js/web' (the 'globals' item in the batch notes), the vite-plugin-solid -> @solidjs/vite-plugin swap in both config/vite/solid.js:3 and config/vite/demo.js:3 (demo.js is what builds perf/perf-isolate/experiments), tsconfig.json:7 jsxImportSource, and package.json's missing @solidjs/web dependency. Dispose handling: all entries discard render()'s dispose, which is unchanged from 1.9 and safe since no entry uses import.meta.hot. subtask.tsx mounting #root rather than #app is correct (examples/subtask.html:25 declares it).
  STRUCT: No sync hazards, stores, effects, lifecycles or JSX control flow exist anywhere in this batch: all 19 files are 7-10 line bootstraps of the identical shape (import render -> document.getElementById -> if (root) render(() => <Demo />, root)). The batch note's expectation of pure render-import swaps is confirmed for every file.
  STRUCT: DISPOSE: all 19 entries discard render()'s returned dispose function. This is unchanged behavior from 1.9 (render also returned dispose there) and is fine for page-level entries that live until unload. There is no import.meta.hot usage in any entry, so no HMR teardown path needs the dispose handle. No change required, but if the migration adds an HMR accept hook later, the dispose must be captured.
  STRUCT: MOUNT TIMING (behavioral, no action): rc.6 render() schedules the top-level insert through the effect queue and drains it with a tail flush() before returning, so the DOM attach remains synchronous on the no-async path. It additionally opens a dev-only ASYNC_OUTSIDE_LOADING_BOUNDARY window scoped to the initial mount. I verified no demo component mounted by these entries uses createResource or any async read (the sole grep hit, DbDemo.tsx:539 api.createResource, is a REST helper, not a Solid primitive), so no <Loading> boundary is needed at any entry today. If the demo batch introduces createAsync/async reads, the corresponding entry must wrap its root in <Loading>.
  STRUCT: MODULE-LEVEL GLOBALS (no action): every entry calls document.getElementById at module top level, outside any owner. That is unaffected by the 2.0 write guard (no reactive primitive is touched) and stays valid. arrow.tsx and constraint.tsx additionally console.error on a missing root; the other 17 fail silently. Not a migration issue.
  STRUCT: BUILD-CONFIG COUPLING (real blocker, missed by the SMA): config/vite/solid.js externalizes and UMD-maps the literal string 'solid-js/web'. Renaming the imports in these entries and in src/ without updating rollupOptions.external (line 19) and output.globals (lines 21-22) silently inlines the whole @solidjs/web renderer into the published library bundle and breaks the UMD global contract. Treat the import swap and the config swap as one atomic change.
  STRUCT: EXAMPLE HTML EXTENSION MISMATCH (pre-existing, not Solid-related): 11 of the example pages load these entries with a .jsx URL for a file that is actually .tsx (examples/bar.html:86 -> /src/entries/bar.jsx, plus arrow, perf, perf-isolate, experiments, subtask, showcase, resource-groups, constraint, gantt, profiler). This only works because Vite's TS-output path guessing maps .jsx to .tsx. It resolves identically under @solidjs/vite-plugin (the plugin transforms the resolved .tsx id, not the request URL), so it is not a migration blocker, but it is brittle enough to be worth normalizing while the entries are being touched.
  SITE[structural/config] package.json:118 — "solid-js": "^1.9.12"  (deps); "vite-plugin-solid": "^2.11.12", "babel-preset-solid": "^1.9.12" (dev => deps: solid-js 2.0.0-rc.6 + @solidjs/web 2.0.0-rc.6; devDeps: @solidjs/vite-plugin 3.0.0-next.35, drop vite-plugin-solid and babel-preset-solid; @solid-primitives/raf -> 4.0.0-next.2, @solid-primitives/utils -> 7.0.0-nex

### CRITIC demo: missed=6 disputed=5
SUMMARY: Ran independent greps for every FACTS pattern across all 47 in-scope files and reconciled hit-by-hit against the eight audits, then verified the sharp claims against the rc.6 tarballs (@solidjs/signals dist/dev.js + types, @solidjs/web types/jsx.d.ts + dist/dev.js, @solid-primitives/raf 4.0.0-next.2). SMA coverage is complete — all 64 in-scope sma-report lines are accounted for by some audit, none dropped — and the audits' import/Index/onMount/produce/batch/path-setter inventories are accurate (I re-counted ShowcaseDemo's 38 store setters, GanttExperiments' two options-shaped 2-arg memos, GanttPerfIsolate's 8 useGanttEvents sites and 6 top-level reads, index-test's 7 top-level reads and 4 Index sites, and confirmed zero classList/use:/on:/attr:/bool:/@once/createComputed/createResource/Suspense/Portal/createRoot/destructured-props anywhere in the area).

The biggest gap is a hazard class nobody named: rc.6 validates the RETURN VALUE of onSettled callbacks (dev.js:6282-6287, reached via `createTrackedEffect(() => untrack(callback))` at 7220) and of createEffect's apply phase (dev.js:6232-6241), throwing on any non-undefined, non-function return — while Setter<T> still returns the new value. That turns four in-scope concise arrows into dev throws (ExportDemo:197 onReady, BoxSelectDemo:141 and MultiSelectDemo:123 onSelectionChange, plus the already-caught DbDemo:111), and it silently breaks the split-effect rewrites that demo-features, demo-components and demo-showcase prescribe. I also found four missed read-then-write latches of a class other batches did flag (GanttMinimalTest:340, GanttPerfDemo:110/161/211) and one dead import (ShowcaseDemo:6 Show).

Five disputes, two of them consequential. demo-perf-profiler's only high-severity finding — that GanttPerfDemo:334's onSettled body re-subscribes through createRAF and fires loadTasks twice — is refuted twice over: onSettled untracks its callback (signals.d.ts:645-646) and raf 4.0.0-next.2 marks its latch `ownedWrite: true`. The same 'onSettled is tracked' premise (which the FACTS brief itself asserts, and which only the demo-showcase audit challenged) also invalidates a forward-looking warning at GanttPerfIsolate:1758. demo-db's DbDemo:100 queueMicrotask hazard does not survive scheduler ordering — setBundle's schedule() enqueues the flush microtask before queueMicrotask(snapshotChartX), and Gantt's runSetup writes land inside that flush — and demo-features' ExportDemo:101 null-api window is closed by render()'s tail flush(). Cross-cutting, the area has one architectural defect (write-config-then-re-read-to-mirror) filed as three separate hazard sets, one lifecycle body copy-pasted five times, one latch pattern repeated seven times, and one unaudited file (CustomColumnsDemo.tsx) that is in src/demo but in no batch's scope list.
