  DISPUTED src/demo/GanttPerfDemo.tsx:336 — WRONG PREMISE, HIGHEST-SEVERITY FINDING IN THAT BATCH. demo-perf-profiler files a `severity: high` sync hazard claiming that because onSettled is a tracked effect, `startFpsCounter()`'s internal `running()` read subscribes the onSettled bundle to createRAF's signal, `setRunning(true)` dirties it, an
  DISPUTED src/demo/GanttPerfIsolate.tsx:1758 — Same wrong premise, propagated into a forward-looking warning. Note (e) on this site asserts 'onSettled is a TRACKED effect, and the body registers zero dependencies ... so it runs once — but note the body writes viewportWidth, so adding any reactive read to the body later would create a self-trigge
  DISPUTED src/demo/DbDemo.tsx:100 — MEDIUM HAZARD LIKELY NOT REAL — the microtask ordering works out. demo-db claims 'a single queued microtask is no longer a guarantee that those effect-phase store writes have committed', so snapshotChartX can read the OLD layout. But `setBundle(data)` at line 93 runs first and its write calls schedu
  DISPUTED src/demo/ExportDemo.tsx:101 — Overstated. demo-features files a medium hazard that 'a click (or programmatic click in a test) that lands in the same tick as mount, before the flush, sees null'. @solidjs/web render() ends with an explicit `flush()` inside its try block (dist/dev.js:377-402: createRoot(...) { insert(..., { schedul
  DISPUTED src/demo/GanttPerfIsolate.tsx:1879 — Minor factual overreach. The note says to use a block body because '2.0 reserves ref-callback return values'. @solidjs/web types/jsx.d.ts:243-244 declares `type RefCallback<T> = (el: T) => void; type Ref<T> = T | RefCallback<T> | undefined | Ref<T>[]` — the return is typed void, and there is no runt
  CROSS: RETURN-VALUE VALIDATION IS A NEW, UNNAMED HAZARD CLASS AND NO AUDIT NAMES IT. rc.6 validates the return of two callback shapes: onSettled/createTrackedEffect (dev.js:6282-6287) and createEffect's apply phase (dev.js:6232-6241), both throwing 'callback returned an invalid cleanup value. Return a cleanup function or undefined.' Meanwhile Setter<T> still RETURNS the new value (signals.d.ts:60-65; setSignal ends `return v`). So every concise arrow of the shape `() => setX(...)`, `(v) => setX(v)`, or `() => somethingAsync()` becomes a dev throw the moment it lands in an onSettled body or an effect apply. In this area that is at least four live sites — DbDemo.tsx:111 (async, the one audit that caught it), ExportDemo.tsx:197, BoxSelectDemo.tsx:141, MultiSelectDemo.tsx:123 — plus every split-effect rewrite the audits propose with a concise apply (demo-features' Gantt.tsx:184-188 fix, demo-components' ConstraintDemo:662 fix, demo-showcase's updateTaskA/updateTaskB mirrors). File it as one repo rule: any callback that crosses a component boundary or is handed to createEffect/onSettled gets a block body, full stop. It is mechanical, greppable (`=> set[A-Z]`, `=> props\.on[A-Z]`), and invisible to tsc while @ts-nocheck stands.
  CROSS: onSettled IS NOT TRACKED, AND THE BRIEF SAYS IT IS. FACTS item 3 calls onSettled 'a children-forbidden tracked effect'; four of the eight audits built reasoning on that (demo-perf-profiler's only high-severity hazard, demo-perf-isolate's L1758 note (e), demo-experiments' L964 note, demo-components' correct-but-differently-argued ArrowDemo:350 note). The implementation is `createTrackedEffect(() => untrack(callback), { name: 'onSettled' })` (dev.js:7220) and the doc is explicit: 'Reactive reads inside the callback are *not* tracked — to react to subsequent settles, register a new onSettled each time' (signals.d.ts:645-646). Correct the brief once rather than per batch: onSettled never re-runs, never subscribes, and never emits STRICT_READ; the real trap is the opposite (a read placed there is silently inert).
  CROSS: THE 'running()/recording() AS ITS OWN LATCH' PATTERN IS ONE ISSUE, NOT FIVE. Every perf harness in this area reimplements a start/stop toggle whose guard reads the signal the same function writes: GanttExperiments.tsx:898 (flagged), index-test.tsx:1947 (flagged), GanttMinimalTest.tsx:340 (missed), GanttPerfDemo.tsx:110/161/211 (missed), GanttProfiler.tsx:83/87 (flagged low), plus the same shape inside @solid-primitives/raf's own start() and src/hooks/useDrag.ts:196. Under deferred writes all seven read committed state. One shared fix — a plain closure boolean as the latch, the signal kept purely as a render mirror — resolves the whole set, and it is cheaper than the flush()-based fixes several audits propose.
  CROSS: THE onMount + ResizeObserver/setTimeout/RAF + onCleanup BODY IS COPY-PASTED FIVE TIMES. GanttMinimalTest.tsx:417/439, GanttExperiments.tsx:964/978, GanttPerfIsolate.tsx:1758/1769, GanttPerfDemo.tsx:269/276/298, GanttProfiler.tsx:165/177 are near-identical: measure the container, observe resize, start a RAF/interval, register onCleanup. Every one needs the same three edits (onSettled, onCleanup -> a single returned disposer merging listener + timer + RAF handles) and three of them leak an uncancelled RAF today (GanttMinimalTest:468, GanttPerfIsolate:1806, GanttExperiments' stress loop). This should be one `onSettled`-based viewport/FPS helper in the demo tree, migrated once, not five hand-rolled conversions with five chances to drop a clearTimeout.
  CROSS: 'WRITE A CONFIG SIGNAL/STORE, THEN SYNCHRONOUSLY RE-READ IT TO MIRROR INTO taskStore' IS ONE ARCHITECTURAL DEFECT SPREAD ACROSS THREE FILES. ShowcaseDemo hazards #1-#7 (input handler -> setTaskConfig -> updateTaskA re-reads taskConfig), ConstraintDemo hazard #1 (setSelectedScenario -> updateTasks re-reads the allTasks memo), and DbDemo hazards #1/#2 (drag -> _bar re-read from the live store proxy) are filed as three unrelated hazard sets across three batches. They are the same shape and have the same fix: derive the mirror with a split createEffect (compute reads the config into a PLAIN object, apply writes the store) instead of pairing a write with an imperative re-read. Doing it once removes ~10 of the area's high-severity hazards; patching each with flush() leaves the shape in place to be re-introduced by the next handler.
  CROSS: createMemo's 1.x INITIAL VALUE HAS A REAL rc.6 SUCCESSOR AND NO AUDIT MENTIONS IT. MemoOptions now carries `loadingValue?: T` — 'Commit #0: a committed value the memo is born with ... The loading value is also the compute's first prev' (signals.d.ts:200-224), with a dedicated overload `createMemo<T>(compute, options: MemoOptions<T> & { loadingValue: T })` (signals.d.ts:295-297). Both the SMA guidance and the demo-perf-profiler rewrite for benchmarks/profiler/instrumentation/memoTracker.js:136 treat the initial value as simply deleted (drop the `undefined`, move options into slot 2) and recommend a default parameter on the compute. That is right for this call, but the migration playbook for the whole repo should record loadingValue as the first-class option, since createTrackedMemo is a public profiling API whose callers may have relied on an initial value.
  CROSS: SCOPE-PARTITION GAP: src/demo/CustomColumnsDemo.tsx belongs to this area, is mounted by an in-scope entry (src/entries/custom-columns.tsx:6), and appears in NO batch's file list. I read it: 158 lines, zero solid-js imports, no For/Show/Index/store/lifecycle, one plain `render: (task) => <div>…</div>` column callback that creates no primitives — so there are no migration sites, but it does contain JSX and therefore depends on the tsconfig jsxImportSource flip to '@solidjs/web' like every other file here. Worth recording explicitly so nobody assumes the file was audited and found clean when it was simply never opened. (src/demo/db/types.ts is likewise out of the list; it is types-only.)
  CROSS: TOOLING VERIFIED FROM THE TARBALLS, TWO AUDIT CAVEATS CAN BE CLOSED. @solid-primitives/raf 4.0.0-next.2 still returns `[running, start, stop]` and now creates its latch with `createSignal(false, { ownedWrite: true })`, so the write-guard worry raised for GanttPerfDemo.tsx:64 and index-test.tsx:1875 is moot and the existing tuple destructures need no change; it imports `isServer` from '@solidjs/web' and `noop` from @solid-primitives/utils, confirming both peer bumps are mandatory. @solidjs/web render() ends with an explicit flush() and wraps the mount in enforceLoadingBoundary(true) (dist/dev.js:377-402), which confirms the entries batch's ASYNC_OUTSIDE_LOADING_BOUNDARY note and closes the 'does the mount still attach synchronously' question for all 20 entries. SMA coverage is complete for this area: all 64 in-scope SMA lines are reproduced by some audit, and I found no SMA site that was dropped.

SUMMARY: Tooling is a small, well-bounded change; the docs are the long tail. DEPENDENCIES (package.json): bump solid-js ^1.9.12 -> 2.0.0-rc.6 (L101) and ADD @solidjs/web 2.0.0-rc.6 (mandatory - the native compiler emits every JSX runtime import with moduleName '@solidjs/web', the plugin peer-requires it, and @solid-primitives/raf@4 imports isServer from it); replace vite-plugin-solid ^2.11.12 (L85) with @solidjs/vite-plugin 3.0.0-next.35; delete babel-preset-solid (L70, no babel config exists in the repo); bump @solid-primitives/raf ^2.3.4 -> 4.0.0-next.2 (L97, the only imported primitive: src/demo/GanttPerfDemo.tsx:3, src/entries/index-test.tsx:15); delete @solid-primitives/memo, resize-observer, scheduled, scroll (L96, L98-100 - repo-wide grep proves all four are unimported); add jsdom if you adopt the plugin's default test environment. VITE LIB (config/vite/solid.js): external L18 must become ['solid-js','@solidjs/web'] and the UMD globals map L22 must be re-keyed to '@solidjs/web' -> SolidJSWeb, or the DOM runtime gets bundled into dist and the UMD build emits a missing-global warning; a side benefit is that the store runtime (today imported from the un-externalized 'solid-js/store') becomes correctly external. TSCONFIG: jsxImportSource 'solid-js' -> '@solidjs/web' (L8; tsconfig.build.json inherits it). VITEST: the sharpest tooling hazard - `environment: 'node'` (L15) puts @solidjs/vite-plugin into its server posture, which resolves the server build with no subscription graph; recommended posture is to delete both `environment` and the hand-written `resolve.conditions` (L11), add jsdom, and let the plugin inject jsdom + solid/development/browser. ESLINT: no globals change is required at all. CI: .github/workflows/publish.yml pins node-version '18' but the plugin requires ^20.19||>=22.12 - a hard release-job break. DOCS: 40 sentences across CLAUDE.md, README.md and 7 doc files go wrong, headlined by README.md:106 ('useGanttStores() returns undefined outside a provider' - createContext<T>() with no default now THROWS ContextNotFoundError, so the documented graceful fallback dies), docs/PERFORMANCE.md:407 (which prescribes wrapping store WRITES in untrack() inside an effect - fatal under the 2.0 write guard), docs/MINIMAL_TEST.md:54/76 (<Index> -> <For keyed={false}>), ANALYSIS.md:110/300 (Index-based headers), and every createStore/solid-js-store reference. Six sync hazards, three of them high: the vitest server-build posture, the untrack-wrapped effect write, and the DbDemo drag-PATCH algorithm documented at docs/DATABASE.md:136-145 / CLAUDE.md:265-269 that writes bar geometry and then reads it back in the same tick to compute pixel deltas. Also note dist-demo/ is committed (9 tracked files carrying the 1.9 runtime) and is what the benchmark scripts serve, so it must be rebuilt with the migration. The SMA report lists nothing for any file in this batch.
  STRUCT: VITEST POSTURE IS THE ONE REAL DECISION IN THIS BATCH. Verified in @solidjs/vite-plugin@3.0.0-next.35 dist: serverTestPosture is set when test.environment is 'node' or 'edge-runtime'; configEnvironment then injects ['solid', 'development'] but NOT 'browser', and config() force-inlines solid-js/@solidjs/web so the server build resolves end to end. solid-js@2.0.0-rc.6's exports map is ordered worker > browser > deno > node > development, so the FIRST matching condition key wins - which is why the repo's hand-written resolve.conditions:['development','browser'] would still (accidentally) select dist/dev.js. Recommendation: delete both `environment:'node'` and the `resolve` block, add jsdom, and let the plugin own it. That is the tested path, and no test in tests/ touches document/window today.
  STRUCT: dist-demo/ IS COMMITTED BUILD OUTPUT AND WILL BE STALE. `git ls-files dist-demo` returns 9 tracked files (3 examples/*.html + 6 hashed chunks, perf-isolate alone 472 KB) refreshed by 'chore(demo): refresh built artifacts' commits (20bd2e6, 2e9d8bd, 4a0633e). .gitignore ignores `dist/*` but not dist-demo. Those chunks embed the Solid 1.9 runtime, and benchmarks/scripts/*.sh benchmark against them via `npx serve dist-demo -l 5174`, so post-migration benchmark runs would measure the OLD runtime unless the migration commit re-runs `pnpm build:demo` and commits the result (or the directory is finally gitignored and the bench docs updated to require a local build).
  STRUCT: NODE VERSION FLOOR MOVES. @solidjs/vite-plugin declares engines.node '^20.19.0 || >=22.12.0'. .github/workflows/publish.yml pins node-version '18', so the release job breaks on the dependency swap. Bump to 22 and, while there, reconcile pnpm/action-setup `version: 9` with packageManager pnpm@10.23.0 (or delete the pin). The workflow runs only prettier-check + build - consider adding typecheck and test so a bad jsxImportSource or a non-reactive test environment is caught in CI rather than at publish time.
  STRUCT: PNPM 10 BUILD-SCRIPT APPROVAL FOR THE NATIVE COMPILER (verify, do not assume). @solidjs/vite-plugin depends on @solidjs/compiler (native binary, wasm fallback on platforms without a prebuilt). pnpm 10 blocks dependency lifecycle scripts unless allow-listed; this repo already maintains two separate allow-lists - pnpm-workspace.yaml (onlyBuiltDependencies: [esbuild]) and package.json pnpm.onlyBuiltDependencies ([better-sqlite3]). If the compiler needs a postinstall on this platform, add it to pnpm-workspace.yaml; consolidating the two lists into pnpm-workspace.yaml would also remove a latent pnpm 9/10 footgun.
  STRUCT: DEPENDENCY CLASSIFICATION FOR A LIBRARY. solid-js sits in `dependencies` today while the lib build externalizes it, which risks a duplicate reactive graph in consumer apps. With @solidjs/web joining it as a second externalized runtime the risk doubles (two packages that must be the same instance as the host app's). The correct shape for ganttss is peerDependencies { solid-js: ^2.0.0-rc.6, @solidjs/web: ^2.0.0-rc.6 } + the same entries in devDependencies. If that is too big a change for the migration PR, at minimum pin exact rc versions and add `resolve.dedupe` guidance to the README.
  STRUCT: THE ENTIRE PERFORMANCE CORPUS BECOMES ADVISORY. benchmarks/traces/ANALYSIS.md, docs/PERFORMANCE.md, docs/DEMOS.md and docs/EXPERIMENTS.md contain ~10 result tables measured on solid-js 1.9.12 (Dec 2025 and the 2026-05-10 post-audit baseline). Deferred writes plus a single microtask flush per tick change effect scheduling and frame accounting, and the harnesses themselves change (<Index> -> <For keyed={false}> in GanttExperiments/TaskLayerMinimal/DateHeadersOptimized, so the 'Index-based headers are slower' finding is testing a different implementation). Plan a re-baseline run as an explicit migration deliverable and date-stamp the old tables rather than deleting them.
  STRUCT: PRE-EXISTING DOC/CODE DRIFT THAT THE MIGRATION PASS SHOULD SWEEP (not caused by Solid 2.0, but it will make the migrated docs untrustworthy): (a) CLAUDE.md lines 96-99/105-113/116-126/143/154-156/188-191 still name .js/.jsx files that are .ts/.tsx; (b) docs/MINIMAL_TEST.md:6-7, docs/EXPERIMENTS.md:9/101-102, docs/DEMOS.md:109/144/210/235-236 and ANALYSIS.md:301 link to a `perf-traces/` directory that is actually `benchmarks/traces/`; (c) ANALYSIS.md:300 and DEMOS.md:76 point at src/components/DateHeadersOptimized.jsx, which now lives at src/demo/DateHeadersOptimized.tsx; (d) CLAUDE.md:50 claims no test runner exists while package.json:28 and 18 test files say otherwise; (e) docs/DATABASE.md:17 names a vite.config.ts that does not exist; (f) docs/ARCHITECTURE.md:398-431 still documents ganttConfigStore as 21 signals after gantt-84c converted it to a store.
  STRUCT: EXAMPLES ARE UNAFFECTED BY THE MIGRATION but worth one note: all 22 examples/*.html use only `<script type="module" src="/src/entries/*.jsx">` with no CDN/importmap/solid reference of any kind, so nothing there changes. (They reference .jsx paths for files that are .tsx - Vite resolves this today and will continue to; not a migration item.) benchmarks/scripts/*.sh reference no vite/plugin machinery at all - they only drive perf.mjs against http://localhost:5174.
  STRUCT: SMA CROSS-CHECK: the official migration assistant's report (sma-report.txt) contains ZERO findings for any file in this batch - no package.json, vite config, vitest config, tsconfig, eslint config, workflow, or markdown entry appears in it. Everything above is additional coverage. The one adjacent SMA finding, benchmarks/profiler/instrumentation/memoTracker.js:136 (createMemo initial-value argument), is outside this batch's file list but confirms the createMemo-args class is live in the benchmarks tree.
  HAZ[high] vitest.config.ts:15 — test.environment:'node' puts @solidjs/vite-plugin into its SERVER posture: the plugin skips injecting the 'browser' condition and force-inlines solid-js/@solidjs/web (test.server.deps.inline=[/solid-js/,/@solidjs[+/]web/]) so the SERVER build resolves - a build with no subscription graph. Every reactive assertion in tests/taskStore.test.ts (createRoot + createMemo over store.tasks[id]._bar.x, lines 87-360) and tests/selectionStore.test.ts (lines 66-95) would write and then read a memo that never recomputed. Combined with 2.0's deferred writes (the read is stale even on the client build without a flush), the suite can go green while asserting nothing, or fail in a way that looks like a store bug. || FIX: Drop `environment: 'node'` and `resolve.conditions` entirely, add a jsdom devDependency, and let the plugin apply its client posture (jsdom + solid/development/browser conditions). Then add `flush()` after every write before an assertion. If a node environment is genuinely required, you must keep an explicit `resolve.conditions: ['solid','development','browser']` and verify at runtime that `import('solid-js')` resolves dist/dev.js, not dist/server.js.
  HAZ[high] docs/PERFORMANCE.md:407 — Documented house pattern 'Gantt Y-sync effect: wrap position updates in untrack() to avoid feedback loop'. In 2.0 untrack() disables tracking but does NOT clear the owner, so a store write executed inside a createEffect compute callback throws REACTIVE_WRITE_IN_OWNED_SCOPE in dev even when wrapped. Any contributor following this doc writes code that throws on first render; worse, the pattern's intent (write derived positions back into the store from an effect) is exactly the write-then-read shape that deferred writes break, because the same effect then re-reads the positions it just staged. || FIX: Rewrite the guidance (and src/components/Gantt.tsx) as a split effect: createEffect(compute, apply) where compute reads plain values (numbers, not store proxies) and returns them, and apply performs the setStore(draft => ...) write. The apply callback runs from the scheduler with no owner, so it may write legally, and it is untracked by construction - the untrack() wrapper becomes unnecessary.
  HAZ[high] docs/DATABASE.md:138 — The documented drag-persistence algorithm (steps 1-5, lines 136-145; implemented in src/demo/DbDemo.tsx) writes bar positions through the chart's store during drag and then, on the onDateChange callback, WALKS THE SAME STORE reading `_bar.x`/`_bar.width` to diff them against a snapshot. If onDateChange is emitted in the same synchronous tick as the store write (drag-end handler, or a constraint cascade applying updates), every read returns the previously committed value, so Δx and Δwidth compute as 0 and no PATCH is sent - the drag silently fails to persist. The move/resize disambiguation table (lines 149-153) keys entirely off those deltas, so a partially-flushed read could also mis-classify a resize as a move. || FIX: Await the flush before diffing: make the onDateChange handler `async (id, dates) => { await flush(); ...walk store... }` (or queueMicrotask), or - better - have the chart pass the new geometry to the callback explicitly so the demo never re-reads the store it just wrote. Update both this doc and CLAUDE.md:265-269.
  HAZ[medium] CLAUDE.md:266 — Same hazard restated as repo guidance: 'it snapshots each bar's _bar.x and _bar.width, computes per-task pixel deltas after drag, and PATCHes in parallel'. As written it instructs future contributors to read store geometry immediately after the chart mutated it. Under deferred writes 'after drag' is not 'after commit'. || FIX: Amend the bullet to state that the delta walk must run after the microtask flush, and add a repo-wide rule near the top of CLAUDE.md: 'never read back a value you just wrote in the same tick - read the draft inside the setter, pass the value explicitly, or flush()'.
  HAZ[medium] docs/ARCHITECTURE.md:903 — The documented constraint resolution flow is a write-then-read chain: resolveConstraints() reads committed geometry through context.getBarPosition (lines 596-604), the caller then calls taskStore.updateBarPosition() for the dragged task, then applies cascadeUpdates to successors, and finally expects arrow paths to recalculate. If any of those steps re-enters resolveConstraints or recomputes cascade geometry in the same tick, it plans against pre-write positions; multi-step relaxation across ticks can also converge on the wrong answer because 'current position (with any pending update)' (line 631) is no longer visible through a store read. || FIX: Apply the dragged position and every cascade update inside ONE setStore(draft => { ... }) callback - the draft reflects staged state, so the relaxation loop can read its own pending updates - and keep resolveConstraints pure over an explicitly passed position map rather than over live store reads. Document the flush boundary in the diagram.
  HAZ[medium] docs/MINIMAL_TEST.md:324 — The 'Target (functional)' drag template writes taskStore.updateBarPosition(taskId, { x: newX }) inside a RAF-driven onDragMove. The write itself is legal (event/RAF scope, no owner), but any code later in the same handler that re-reads the bar position - constraint clamping, arrow endpoints, snap feedback - sees the previous frame's value, producing a one-frame lag or an oscillation during fast drags. || FIX: Annotate the template: compute newX once and pass it to every consumer in the handler; never re-read taskStore for a value written in the same frame. If a helper genuinely needs the post-write state, do the work inside the setter's draft callback.
  SITE[structural/write-in-compute] docs/PERFORMANCE.md:407 — 3. **Gantt Y-sync effect**: Wrap position updates in `untrack()` to avoid feedback loop => Replace with the 2.0 pattern: 'Gantt Y-sync effect: split it - createEffect(() => <plain values read here>, (vals) => { setStore(draft => { ...write here... }) }). ### CRITIC tooling: missed=13 disputed=4
SUMMARY: I re-grepped every FACTS pattern across the 17 in-scope files, read the full sma-report.txt, and verified claims directly against the published tarballs (solid-js/@solidjs/web/@solidjs/signals 2.0.0-rc.6, @solidjs/vite-plugin next.35 AND next.38, @solid-primitives/raf 4.0.0-next.2). The audit is strong on mechanics — its dependency swap, external/globals re-keying, jsxImportSource change, and the serverTestPosture analysis all check out line-for-line against the plugin dist, and its line numbers are accurate. It has two blind spots.

The first is that its central vitest recommendation is unscoped. 'Delete environment:node, take the plugin's jsdom default' is right for the 11 reactive suites and wrong for tests/server/{routes,crud,adapter,schema}.test.ts, which drive better-sqlite3 and Hono via `app.fetch(new Request(...))` and which the audit never opens — it justifies jsdom with 'no test currently touches document/window', a grep that simply misses them. The plugin's own source says posture is per-vitest-project, so vitest 4 `test.projects` is the only shape that serves both, and the audit's fallback ('keep an explicit browser condition under environment:node') is worse than it looks: that branch also force-inlines the runtimes for a server build, so forcing browser back yields client reactivity + server codegen + no DOM, a hybrid upstream deliberately refuses to emit.

The second is a false-negative on Set-in-store. The audit surveyed taskStore.collapsedTasks (a signal-of-Set, correctly benign) and let that stand as the answer, missing ganttConfigStore.expandedTasks — declared inside GanttConfigState, created inside createStore, and mutated in place at three sites via `setState('expandedTasks', produce(set => set.add/delete(id)))`. That is a triple break (unproxied Set, removed produce, removed path setter) and the only silent one in the store layer.

Beyond those: @solid-primitives/raf is being bumped to a prerelease while left in `dependencies` even though nothing reachable from src/index.ts imports it; the UMD format plus two externalized runtimes is an unsatisfiable contract because neither package publishes a global build; `output: { interop: 'auto' }` in config/vite/solid.js sits at the config top level where Vite ignores it; and tsconfig include, lint globs, prettier globs and CI collectively give every file this migration edits zero static verification. On docs I add nine sites the audit passed over, the most consequential being docs/PERFORMANCE.md:421, where the documented untrack 'fix' only wraps the proxy lookup and leaves every `bar.x`/`bar.width` read tracked — a pattern that is already a no-op in 1.x and starts emitting STRICT_READ_UNTRACKED in 2.0. I also recommend @solidjs/vite-plugin next.38 over next.35 (matching @solidjs/compiler rc.6, and it adds the `mode !== 'test'` guards next.35 lacks) and note that AGENTS.md and docs/RFC-undo-redo.md carry in-scope guidance but were left off the batch list.

## TRACER
VERDICT: Roughly 80% mechanical edits, 20% genuine restructuring, and the restructuring is concentrated in two places rather than smeared across the drag pipeline. The drag/constraint machinery itself does NOT need rewriting: the per-frame loop already reads before it writes, constraintEngine.calculateCascadeUpdates carries its own pending-update overlay (:634-636, :645-647) so a single call is internally consistent, and successive rAF frames are separate tasks with a flush between. What needs real design work is (1) the setup pipeline — ganttSetup.ts plus ganttDateStore's setupDates/generateDates/changeViewMode/extendTimeline must be converted from write-then-read-back into compute-then-apply, with generateDates taking explicit (start, end, step, unit) parameters and the resource index map computed locally from the extracted array instead of round-tripped through the store; and (2) the gesture-commit boundary in useDrag.ts:161-177, where the final onDragMove's write is read back by onDragEnd, onResizeEnd and every downstream consumer in the same stack — thread the committed geometry out as a local or return value and chains C, D and H all close at once, including the DbDemo data-corruption path. Everything else is a one-liner or close to it: delete the committed-state guard at taskStore.ts:108 (the draft callback at :112-113 already checks correctly), change the re-entrancy guard at useDrag.ts:196 from dragState() to dragData (matching the pattern already used at :130 and :155), move expandedTasks to a replace-never-mutate signal, pass measured container dimensions as data rather than accessors in the GanttContainer handshake, move the isScrolling flag and timer out of the tracked compute at useGanttScroll.ts:78-90, give collapseAllTasks and resourceStore.collapseAll an explicit id-list parameter, fix removeTask to actually delete the key, and key the virtualization cache on a version counter instead of a task count. The highest-severity item to fix first is chain A, because it fails closed — a blank chart on first paint with no error and no warning — and the highest-consequence is chain H, because its stale read is persisted to a database rather than merely displayed.
SUMMARY: Traced all ten requested chains plus three additional ones by opening every file in each path. The library splits cleanly into two populations. The per-frame drag loop is largely sound: within one rAF frame every pipeline reads before it writes (useBarDrag.ts:155-164, :183-199; taskLayerConstraints.ts:60-73), rAF frames are separate tasks so cross-frame reads see committed state, and the two write primitives that look dangerous are actually safe — batchMovePositions' produce callback reads state[id] inside the draft (taskStore.ts:161-172), and every selectionStore mutator uses a functional updater that rc.6 folds against the staged value (selectionStore.ts:40-69, exercised in a loop at useBoxSelect.ts:156). Do not "fix" those. What genuinely breaks clusters at four seams. First, the setup pipeline is write-then-read four times over: ganttDateStore.setupDates writes ganttStart/ganttEnd at :193-194 then generateDates reads them back at :202-203, ganttSetup.ts:66 then re-reads all five timeline values at :69-73 and :75-84, and ganttSetup.ts:101 writes resources then reads resourceIndexMap() on the very next line. That last one is worse than previously reported: an empty-but-non-null Map skips taskProcessor's internal fallback at :231-235, so every task gets effectiveRowIndex -1, isHidden true, y -1000 (taskProcessor.ts:243-262, :304) and useTaskVirtualization.ts:87 filters them all out — the first paint is a blank chart, not misplaced bars. Second, the gesture-commit boundary: useDrag.ts:161 runs the final onDragMove (which writes) and useDrag.ts:177 calls onDragEnd in the same synchronous stack, where useBarDrag.ts:271 reads getBarPosition back and useBarDrag.ts:281 reads a progress memo back. That single seam is the root of chains C, D and H, including DbDemo persisting drag deltas short by one frame with an error that compounds across drags (DbDemo.tsx:195-196 vs :239-242). Third, ganttConfigStore's expandedTasks is a raw Set mutated in place inside a store (:146, :172-203) — a total reactivity loss independent of deferral — compounded by committed-read guards at :186/:196 and by the count-only virtualization cache at useTaskVirtualization.ts:78 and TaskLayerMinimal.tsx:53, which rc.6's flush coalescing renders permanently stale. Fourth, taskStore's committed-read guards: updateBarPosition's `if (!tasks[id]) return` at :108 (which silently drops writes for tasks created earlier in the same turn), collapseAllTasks reading the task map at :234 before writing at :243, and removeTask's 1.x delete idiom at :134 that no longer removes the key. Two seed hazards are refuted or downgraded: the ArrowLayer/Arrow chain is demo-only (the shipped Gantt mounts no arrow layer — grep of Gantt.tsx, GanttContainer.tsx and TaskLayer.tsx finds no ArrowLayer usage; only demo/GanttPerfIsolate.tsx:2015 does), and the box-select/selection reads are correct in a real browser because click and mousedown are separate tasks, breaking only under synthetic-event drivers.

### CHAIN A. Setup: Gantt.runSetup -> initializeTasks -> dateStore/resourceStore/ganttConfig/taskStore breaks=True sev=high
   - src/components/Gantt.tsx:288 -> onMount(() => runSetup(effectiveTasks())) — first call
   - src/components/Gantt.tsx:290-304 -> createEffect reads effectiveTasks/collapsedGroups/collapsedTasks and calls untrack(() => runSetup(tasks)) — SECOND call in the same tick as onMount
   - src/components/Gantt.tsx:266-275 -> runSetup calls initializeTasks(rawTasks, stores, true, hasExplicitResources), then WRITES setRelationships (:273) / setLegacyResources (:274)
   - src/utils/ganttSetup.ts:66 -> WRITE dateStore.setupDates(rawTasks)
   - src/stores/ganttDateStore.ts:193-194 -> WRITE setGanttStart(start) / setGanttEnd(end)
   - src/stores/ganttDateStore.ts:195 -> calls generateDates() in the same stack
   - src/stores/ganttDateStore.ts:202-203 -> READ ganttStart() / ganttEnd() — returns the committed values, i.e. the two `new Date()` defaults seeded at :69-74 on the first run; the while loop at :210 never executes and setDates([]) at :215 stages an EMPTY dates array
   - src/utils/ganttSetup.ts:69-73 -> READ dateStore.ganttStart()/ganttEnd()/unit()/step()/columnWidth() (all stale) and WRITE them into ganttConfig via makeSetter
   - src/stores/ganttConfigStore.ts:161-164 -> makeSetter reads committed state[key] for `prev` (:161), setState (:163), then `return state[key]` (:164) = the pre-write value
   - src/utils/ganttSetup.ts:75-84 -> builds `config` from the same five stale dateStore reads plus ganttConfig.headerHeight()/barHeight()/padding() (:81-83, not written this turn, so those are fine)
   - src/utils/ganttSetup.ts:101 -> WRITE resourceStore.updateResources(extracted) -> src/stores/resourceStore.ts:114-116 setResources(normalizeResources(...))
   - src/utils/ganttSetup.ts:102 -> READ resourceStore.resourceIndexMap() — memo chain resources() (resourceStore.ts:46) -> displayResources() (:57) -> resourceIndexMap() (:62). Stale; on the first run it is an EMPTY Map (non-null)
   - src/utils/ganttSetup.ts:109 -> processTasks(rawTasks, config, emptyMap)
   - src/utils/taskProcessor.ts:231-232 -> externalResourceIndexMap is truthy (empty Map), so the internal fallback at :234-235 is skipped
   - src/utils/taskProcessor.ts:243-248 -> resourceIndex.get(resource) === undefined for EVERY task -> effectiveRowIndex = -1 -> isHidden = true
   - src/utils/taskProcessor.ts:251-262 -> x computed from the stale config.ganttStart/columnWidth; y = -1000 for every task
   - src/utils/taskProcessor.ts:303-304 -> _resourceIndex: -1, _isHidden: true written into every ProcessedTask
   - src/utils/ganttSetup.ts:116 -> READ taskStore.collapsedTasks() (not written this turn — safe)
   - src/utils/ganttSetup.ts:130 -> WRITE taskStore.updateTasks(Array.from(taskMap.values())) via reconcile (taskStore.ts:124-130)
   - src/components/Gantt.tsx:290 effect's runSetup repeats the whole sequence with still-uncommitted state and overwrites the first pass via reconcile — last write wins and it is the wrong one
   - src/hooks/useTaskVirtualization.ts:87 -> READ task._isHidden -> `continue`, so tasksByResource groups nothing and TaskLayer renders zero bars
   - src/components/Gantt.tsx:502-519 -> scrollTo:'start' effect READS tasks[firstTaskId]._bar.x (:514-515); runs in a later flush so it self-heals, but the truthiness guard `if (firstTask?._bar?.x)` at :515 still silently skips a first task at x === 0
   FIX: Restructure initializeTasks from write-then-read into compute-then-apply. (1) Make ganttDateStore.setupDates RETURN {ganttStart, ganttEnd, dates} and make generateDates take (start, end, step, unit) as explicit parameters instead of re-reading its own signals at ganttDateStore.ts:202-205; setupDates then stages all three writes at the end from locals. (2) In ganttSetup.ts, build `config` (lines 75-84) from those returned locals, never from dateStore accessors. (3) Compute the resource index map locally from `extracted` using the same pure helpers the store uses (normalizeResources + computeDisplayResources + the index loop at resourceStore.ts:62-72) and pass THAT to processTasks at :109; call resourceStore.updateResources(extracted) purely as a commit, with no read-back at :102. (4) Collapse Gantt.tsx:288 onMount and Gantt.tsx:290 createEffect into a single createEffect so runSetup runs once per flush. (5) Fix Gantt.tsx:515 to `firstTask?._bar?.x !== undefined`. Do not paper over this with flush() — the pipeline is a pure function of rawTasks and should be written as one.

(1) useDrag.ts:160-177 — have onDragMove return the geometry it committed (or have useBarDrag stash `lastCommitted = {x, width}` in `data` at each of :164/:196-199/:208/:149) and have onDragEnd at useBarDrag.ts:271 use that local instead of getBarPosition; delete the now-false comment on :270. (2) taskStore.ts:104-121 — drop the committed-state guard on :108 entirely; the produce callback at :112-113 already checks `if (task && task._bar)` against the DRAFT, which sees staged writes.
